Chapter 451: Doubts About Heaven and Earth

At Ji Yuan’s level of cultivation, he rarely dreamt. Normally, even if he did dream, they would usually be the kind that he controlled, where he manifested scenes in his dream, similar to entering a meditative state. Given the special nature of his inner world, Ji Yuan’s sleep could be considered a unique form of cultivation.1

Of course, Ji Yuan also slept normally. After all, sleep was quite a comfortable thing. Even cultivators, as far as Ji Yuan knew, needed to sleep. Some even dreamt for half a year or even several years at a time.

Ji Yuan originally intended to consolidate his gains from the Heaven-Rending Sword Technique and, with the help of a trace of profound insight gained by combining supernatural power with the power of the Transformation of Heaven and Earth to perform various techniques, he intended break through the shackles that prevented him from further enhancing his Dimensional Storage.2

Last time, there had been a major breakthrough in the “Hidden Universe in a Sleeve” technique. Ji Yuan also wanted to incorporate the application of spiritual power into it. This was because he had been influenced by the methods used by the Old Dragon when he captured the monk Huitong.3 He had also made a breakthrough right before Lu Chengfeng, who was had been feeling disheartened at the time, came to visit.

That discovery had made the “Hidden Universe in a Sleeve” technique reality, no longer just a pipe dream. It was no longer just an ordinary dimensional storage technique; the space had greatly increased. He no longer had to worry about carrying a bag for his belongings, or having to specially refine and carve up a fishing rod before he could use it.

However, after that last time, it had only progressed to a point. As Ji Yuan’s spiritual power became more and more profound, and his Five Elements were perfected, his dimensional storage grew larger and larger, and he could even divide it into separate areas. He had also developed some wonderful uses for it, but it was still far from what Ji Yuan had imagined.

Although Ji Yuan’s “Hidden Universe in a Sleeve” was now a remarkable dimensional storage technique in the cultivation world—convenient, fast, with a large capacity, and stylish—Ji Yuan wanted more than just storage abilities.

This time, however, Ji Yuan’s sleep was a little unusual. Before he could even enter a meditative state, his fatigue gradually increased as soon as he fell asleep.

He had overexerted his mind when using the Heaven-Rending Sword Technique that day, and afterwards, due to the earth’s poison, he had been constantly on high alert and had not been able to get proper rest, so his mind had not been able to recover. Now that he had relaxed, Ji Yuan was feeling sleepy.

‘Let’s just go to sleep, get some good rest…’

For Ji Yuan, there was no difference between restoring his mind through meditation or through sleep, so he naturally chose the more comfortable method.

With this thought in mind, Ji Yuan finally fell into a deep sleep, and slept for ten days. On the tenth day, he even had a short dream, a real dream, which was quite rare.

As the saying went, what you think about during the day, you dream about at night. In his dream, Ji Yuan seemed to have returned to his past life, back to his home, and even seemed to have become his childhood self, sitting next to his grandfather and watching programs on that bulky old-fashioned black and white television.

This black and white television only had twelve channels, even with cable TV, and one had to manually turn the channel knob to switch it. It had been Ji Yuan’s most important source of joy when he was a child, as he relied on it to watch TV and play video games.

Right now, the TV was playing “Journey to the West.”4 Perhaps due to his thoughts being projected into his dream, several other channels were also showing “Journey to the West,” though they were on different episodes. Ji Yuan switched channels twice and saw two different segments playing each one.

One scene depicted Sun Wukong attempting to somersault out of Buddha’s palm, believing he had reached the ends of the earth, and urinating on Buddha’s finger.

Another scene took place outside Wuzhuang Temple. Sun Wukong, in a fit of temper, broke the ginseng fruit tree. Realizing he had caused trouble, he tried to escape with Tang Sanzang and his junior brothers, only to be captured by Zhen Yuanzi using his “Universe in a Sleeve” technique.

Ji Yuan was now completely focused on the TV, thinking about how Sun Wukong had fallen victim to similar supernatural powers on both occasions.

The dream was short, just a few fragments. Ji Yuan on the bed frowned slightly, and his mind regained control of his thoughts in his sleep.

The dream began to change, transforming into a vast world, with mountains and rivers, and finally into the expansive mountain range that once stood before the fissure in the earth’s veins.

Ji Yuan still stood on that mountain peak, observing the surrounding demons. However, there were no cultivators from Xianxia Island fighting against them; there was only a scene of demons dancing wildly.

“Heaven-Rending Sword.”

He murmured, tracing the Green Vine Sword in his dream with his sword-fingers.

“Shiiing—”

The immortal sword instantly transformed into light and ascended into the sky. Then, the essence of mountains and rivers was manifested within the sword, connecting with the power of heaven and earth.

The sky seemed to gleam with the white light of the sword, pressing down with the force of fthe collapsing heavens. The power of the Heaven-Rending Sword Technique in the dream was far greater than before. Perhaps because the world in the dream was a manifestation of his own mental conception, allowing for the technique to be used more freely. With the Heaven-Rending Sword Technique unleashed, the heavenly energy surged, the mountains and rivers trembled, and a terrifying pressure, as if the end of the world had arrived, descended.

The surrounding demons fled in panic, some even crashing to the ground in terror, appearing almost frenzied. Before the sword strike even landed, some of the demons were already covered in blood and vanished into a bloody mist, caused by the self-destruction of their demonic energy.

Ji Yuan pointed his sword downwards again, and another rain of demons fell. However, this time, many demons in the air either turned into blood mist or spontaneously combusted. When they fell to the ground, none of them were intact; they were all utterly annihilated.

Ji Yuan sat down on the mountaintop again, but this time not out of exhaustion. He simply sat down casually and freely, and looking at everything around him. He couldn’t help but chuckle.

“Tsk tsk, how would I put it in my past life… oh right, it’s all about exuding superiority!”

Then Ji Yuan stood up again, shook his clothes, and looked at his sleeves.

“The Heaven-Rending Sword Technique borrows the power of heaven and earth to strike the heart, while the Universe in the Sleeve borrows the power of heaven and earth to conceal the form… It’s still not quite there… To wield the power of heaven and earth, one must possess the momentum of heaven and earth…”

His thoughts were unsettled in his dream, and Ji Yuan’s brows furrowed slightly outside of it.

Outside, in the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion, a group of little calligraphy spirits had been playing games against each other for a long time, showing no signs of boredom. They were having a great time. Meanwhile, the little paper crane was watching a group of bees busily moving back and forth among the jujube blossoms, acting just like a little supervisor.

A soft “click” drew the attention of the paper crane and all the small characters in the courtyard. They focused their attention on a corner of the courtyard wall, where a red fox stood up from its slightly bent legs, its fur trembling.

“See that? The stupid fox is here!”

“Yes, it’s rude not to knock!”

“But what if the master wakes up when he knocks on the door?”

“Yes, yes, it’s better not to knock!”

“His pelt would be worth a lot of money if sold to a fur merchant, wouldn’t it?”

“Of course, that fur…”

…

The murmur of whispers reached Hu Yun’s ears, causing him to stiffen. He then jumped onto the stone table in the courtyard and looked around.

“Come out, all of you, come out! Where are you? If you’re all here, Mr. Ji must surely be back!”

Hu Yun shouted a few times to the beings around him in a low voice, but the little characters were incredibly good at hiding; their voices could clearly be heard, but they could not be seen.

Then Hu Yun subconsciously looked at the main room of Ju’an Pavilion. The door was closed, and he could neither hear nor sense anything. Mr. Ji was probably sleeping inside.

The little characters didn’t come out, but the paper crane that had been hovering among the trees flew down and landed on the table. Hu Yun smiled happily; Mr. Ji had indeed already returned.

Ji Yuan left in a hurry before, and Hu Yun had been sent back by Bai Qi. He had been back in Niu Kui Mountain for more than a month and was a little worried about Ji Yuan, or rather, he was afraid that Ji Yuan would not come back for a long time. He had visited twice before, but the small pavilion was empty. This time, he came at the right time.

“When did you get back? Oh, by the way, I have something to give to Mr. Ji!”

Hu Yun winked mysteriously at the little paper crane, his fluffy tail swaying rhythmically up and down. The paper crane tilted its head and looked at Hu Yun’s tail; it knew where this fox usually hid things.

“Oh, what is it?”

A calm voice came from inside the house, and then the door creaked open from the inside, revealing Ji Yuan, who had changed into a blue robe.

“Mr. Ji! You’re back?”

“Haha, aren’t I standing right in front of you? What’s wrong? Did Bai Qi bully you?”

Hu Yun shook his head vigorously, like a rattle drum.

“No, no, River God Bai is fine. Oh, by the way, I found something good in the mountains and came to give it to you, sir!”

“Hahaha… what could you possibly have found…”

Ji Yuan trailed off as Hu Yun pulled out two gold nuggets from his large tail. Judging by their size, they were at least half the size of a palm and were definitely not light.

“Hahaha, sir, all mortals love gold. It seems that Lu Shanjun took quite a lot with him before he left, but I couldn’t find any. I was lucky this time; I dug out two pieces while digging in a rabbit’s burrow, so I brought them to you!”

Ji Yuan subconsciously walked out of the courtyard and turned to look in the direction of Niu Kui Mountain.

‘Could there be a gold mine in these mountains?’

“Mr. Ji, how many copper coins is this gold worth? Is it a lot?”

Hu Yun asked. He already had a good grasp of the value of copper coins, but he didn’t know much about gold. Ji Yuan smiled and replied when he heard this.

“It’s worth a lot of money. If I spend it like this, I estimate it would last for a hundred years.”

“Oh, sir, would you like it?”

Hu Yun presented the gold nugget to Ji Yuan like a treasure, and the latter smiled and accepted it without any hesitation.

“Yes, of course I would. I’ve been living in this mortal world for quite some time, and one can never lack money, no matter what.”

Hu Yun, who had already prepared himself for rejection, was overjoyed to see that Ji Yuan had accepted his offer.

Ji Yuan weighed the two gold ingots in his hand. They made a clinking sound. They weighed several kilograms, which was equivalent to several hundred taels of silver, a huge sum of money for Ji Yuan.

After putting the gold nuggets into his sleeve, Ji Yuan looked up at the top of the jujube tree, raised his sleeve, and flicked it upwards.

“Whoosh…whoosh…”

A faint whistling sound came from his sleeve, as if the air inside the Ju’an Pavilion were swirling back towards Ji Yuan’s sleeve, drawing all the bees from the tree towards him.

However, the moment the swarm of bees got close, Ji Yuan immediately dissipated his spiritual power, allowing the panicked bees to escape frantically.

“No, no, that’s not how it works…”

Although the force just now looked impressive, it seemed as if they were being drawn in by the wind, whereas the way an old dragon’s claws grabbed people seemed much more effortless.

T/n: How much do you want to bet that the “grabbing technique” is either insanely high level stuff, or that JY finds a roundabout way to accomplish it that’s somehow more ridiculous? 😀

T/n: man I WISH my sleep could let me cultivate. I would probably get more of it then LOL

T/n: friendly reminder that this is technically called “Universe in a Sleeve” (you might see it translated as that elsewhere) but Dimensional Storage got the meaning across better

See ch. 246

This likely refers to Journey to the West: Legends of the Monkey King, an animated series adapting the original books. I’ve actually watched a bit of it, it’s quite fun.

Chapter 452: The Determination to Learn

Ji Yuan’s move just now left Hu Yun speechless. Although the sleeve technique wasn’t aimed at him, that wave of the sleeve gave Hu Yun the feeling that Mr. Ji was about to encompass the whole courtyard. Hu Yun subconsciously shrank back on the stone table, as if he would be taken away by the sleeve if he didn’t.

“Mr. Ji, what kind of technique is this? That was so scary! I felt like I was going to be stuffed in your sleeve!”

As soon as Hu Yun said this, the calligraphy spirits who had been playing hide-and-seek with him burst into laughter.

“Hahaha, what’s there to be afraid of in the master’s sleeve?”

“Exactly, we’re inside there every day, it’s not scary at all!”

“Yes, yes, sometimes the master even lets us see outside.”

“That’s right, Hu Yun, you should live with us in the Sword Intent Inscription. Spend some time in the master’s sleeve and you’ll understand!”

…

Hu Yun’s words startled Ji Yuan, who was pondering his gains and losses. Ignoring the noise of the small characters around him, he turned to look at the red fox, and found that he was already huddled on the stone table, his fluffy fur sticking straight out of his body.

“What are you afraid of? I was just testing it. I didn’t even manage to get a bee inside. By the way, tell me exactly how it felt.”

At Ji Yuan’s question, Hu Yun straightened up, thought for a moment, and hesitated before answering.

“Yes, alright, I can’t really explain it, but it just felt like when you swung your sleeve, it was like the sleeve was huge, like it was going to swallow me whole. It wasn’t black or white inside, it was blurry and terrifying… Yes, terrifying…”

Hu Yun didn’t know why he was afraid of a sleeve. Logically speaking, even if Mr. Ji really wanted to put him in a sleeve, there was nothing to be afraid of. But just now he was so scared that he huddled on the table.

“Was it a feeling of being swallowed?”

Ji Yuan asked. Hu Yun hesitated, nodded, then shook his head, still unable to give a clear answer.

“Alright, I understand.”

Ji Yuan nodded, no longer asking Hu Yun about it. He figured it was similar to the Heaven-Rending Sword Technique. Although the “Universe Within a Sleeve” technique was far from being fully mastered, Ji Yuan had already incorporated a portion of its power into his application. If he could manipulate that power, he could influence and put awe and fear into people’s hearts, enough that someone like Hu Yun, who had poor composure, would be affected.

“Sir, you still haven’t told me what kind of supernatural power that was just now!”

Hu Yun remained very curious. He thought it was a very interesting supernatural power, since it could attract things, but it was also so frightening that it must be extraordinary.

Ji Yuan looked at him, thought for a moment, and then spoke.

“This is a way of using a highly advanced supernatural power in a much more accessible way. That advanced technique is called ‘A Universe Within a Sleeve’.”

“Oh…it’s a kind of technique that pull people in?”

“Haha, you wouldn’t be wrong to think that way. However, the ‘Universe Within a Sleeve’ can store both people and objects, making it a very mysterious storage technique. It can store one’s own belongings and can also be used to fight enemies.”

Hu Yun looked at his own tail.

“Then I’ll also have similar supernatural powers. My tail can be used to collect things, and also to fight enemies! Hahaha…”

Ji Yuan couldn’t help but laugh at this joke, while the calligraphy spirits around him laughed even more exaggeratedly.

“Hahahahaha…”

“Hahaha, Hu Yun is going to make me laugh to death!”

“Hahaha, that’s hilarious, that’s so funny!”

“Hahaha, Hu Yun, you actually dare to compare yourself to the Master’s ‘Universe Within a Sleeve’! Hahahaha…”

“You arrogant fox, hahahaha…”

“Exactly, exactly! Do you even know what it means to have a ‘vast universe in a sleeve, infinite time in a bottle’?”

“That’s right, that’s right. Within a small space, the Tao encompasses countless things; this is the art of ‘Universe in a Sleeve.’ Your tail is not worthy of being compared to the Master’s vast supernatural powers!”

“Hahahaha…”

The group of idiots laughed incessantly, making Hu Yun’s cheeks puff up into two small chubby balls. These idiots were going too far!

Ji Yuan’s lips twitched slightly. This group of clowns were making fun of Hu Yun based entirely on his previous written deductions…

“Quiet.”

As soon as the Master spoke, only the sound of falling leaves could be heard throughout the entirety of Ju’an Pavilion.

“This ‘Universe in a Sleeve’ is not yet fully complete. Only when I successfully deduce it will it be worthy of the saying ‘vast universe in a sleeve, infinite time in a bottle.’”

Although these were Ji Yuan’s words, the group of small characters had exceptional confidence in their master. So what if it wasn’t complete yet? It would be completed sooner or later. They had watched their master deduce all of his supernatural abilities, and each of them was so powerful. It didn’t matter whether they praised him sooner or later.

Ji Yuan looked up at the top of the jujube tree. In just a short while, more bees swarmed around, flying up and down among the jujube blossoms to collect nectar.

Ji Yuan picked Hu Yun up and placed him on a stool by the table. He then took out his brush, ink, paper, and inkstone and began grinding the ink while pondering his conclusions. When the ink was ready, he took the wolf-hair brush, lightly dipped it in the ink, and began writing on the paper.

Hu Yun watched for a while, wanting to speak many times, but did not dare to disturb Ji Yuan. Seeing that even the small characters didn’t make a sound, just flying around forming various formations to argue with each other, Hu Yun was even more wary of making the slightest sound.

The wait lasted from morning until afternoon. Although Ji Yuan wrote rather quickly, he only managed to finish one page, leaving Hu Yun confused.

Bored, Hu Yun had already started a fire in the kitchen, boiled water, and even brewed tea.

Fortunately, Ji Yuan finally stopped, put the brush down, and sat down on the stool.

At that moment, Hu Yun immediately darted to the kitchen and came out two breaths later holding a wooden tray with his two paws. His head supportedd the tray, and he walked upright on his other two limbs. Although his posture was funny, the tray he was holding was very stable. Balanced atop it was the brewed tea and four teacups.

“Mr. Ji, Mr. Ji, please have some tea!”

As he spoke, Hu Yun lightly jumped onto the stone bench and carefully placed the tray on the stone table. Ji Yuan found this action both amusing and gratifying; this fox was indeed somewhat useful.

“Speak, what do you want to ask of me?”

Ji Yuan could easily see through Hu Yun’s little scheme, but he was happy to sit down and pour himself a cup of tea.

After thinking for a moment, Ji Yuan waved towards the kitchen, summoning a spoon. He tapped the handle of the spoon and handed it to the little paper crane that had just landed on his shoulder.

“Go and get me a spoonful of fresh jujube blossom honey.”

The little paper crane tilted its head and looked at Ji Yuan. Although it had a head and a beak, its beak was closed. It could hold a piece of paper, but how could it grab a spoon? However, it still tried, and found that as long as its beak touched the handle of the spoon, it could pick it up as if it were glued to its body. It immediately flew away with the spoon.

Hu Yun watched the little paper crane fly away, and when he saw Ji Yuan looking at him again, he finally mustered up his courage and spoke.

“Mr. Ji, could I borrow those scissors and yellow paper you used to cut out paper figures last time?”

“Haha, you’ve still been thinking about it after all these years?”

“I’ve always been thinking about it…”

“How could this be a big request?” Ji Yuan stood up directly, went back into the house, took out the small bamboo basket containing the yellow paper and the scissors, and placed it in front of Hu Yun.

“Lending it to you is no problem, but can you use scissors with those claws of yours?”

“It’s alright, it’ll work!”

Hu Yun was a monster after all. He stretched out his right forepaw and placed it on the scissors. With a surge of monstrous power, it stuck to the iron handles on both sides. As the claw opened and closed, it was clear that it would be effortless for him to use the scissors.

“Mr. Ji, is it simply a matter of cutting out a bunch of little figures and then pressing them together?”

Ji Yuan blew on the tea in his teacup, squinted at Hu Yun, then smiled.

“Of course it’s not that simple. Today I’ll teach you how to cut out those little figures. But you definitely can’t forge a guardian general like the Golden Armored Warrior. You will have to settle for second best.”

“As long as I can cut out a little figure, it’s fine!”

Hu Yun wore a happy smile, but inside he was overjoyed. He wasn’t stupid, not at all. Mr. Ji’s words meant that he was going to teach him a technique!

Ji Yuan glanced at the fox. Although Hu Yun tried his best to keep his smile from getting too exaggerated, his tail was wagging several times faster, like a happy little dog.

Hu Yun’s personality was growing up like a child, and it wasn’t too bad now. This little monster naturally had some outstanding qualities, but he was still too weak.

While it might fool ordinary people, there was a saying: “one’s perspective determines one’s perception.” As a monster, he was bound to encounter ghosts and demons, so it would be beneficial to teach him some skills.

A moment later, the paper crane returned, along with a sweet fragrance. The spoon it carried was full of fresh flower honey, brewed from jujube blossom pollen from the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion.

The current Hu Yun was no longer the same as he was before. Back then, Yin Qing had to force him to learn to read, but now he could grit his teeth and stubbornly squat on a stone bench for three days and three nights in order to learn a little technique.

Ji Yuan wanted to see when the fox would complain of being tired or wanting to rest, so he didn’t stop it at all. He continued to let the fox cut continuously as he pointed in various places. When he ran out of yellow paper, he would take more out from his sleeve.

Now, with scraps of paper littered all over the ground, Hu Yun had only managed to cut out thirty complete yellow paper figures. His once lively fox eyes were now bloodshot from exhaustion, and his two paws trembled slightly.

“Let’s take a break.”

Ji Yuan finally told Hu Yun to rest, but the fox hesitated, not daring to put down the scissors.

“Sir, you said that it takes thirty-six complete paper figures to complete the number of Heavenly Spirits, so that it wouldn’t be a waste. I only have thirty…”

“Haha, okay, then you can rest after you’ve finished cutting out all thirty-six.”

Ji Yuan added a sentence in his mind.

‘Or, wait until you mess it up again.’

To Ji Yuan’s surprise, Hu Yun gritted his teeth, absorbed the spiritual energy in the courtyard, and forced himself to work, straining his spirit to successfully cut out the thirty-six yellow paper figures.

This wasn’t just for show. Ji Yuan clearly saw the thirty-six pieces of paper flash with brilliant light in a continuous sequence the moment the cutting was completed. The next stage was about to begin. If they continued cutting, they couldn’t afford to waste a single piece, otherwise all their previous efforts would be in vain. But if they stopped there, the number of Heavenly Spirits would already be complete.

“Not bad, not bad. You managed to cut it perfectly in just three days.”

Ji Yuan smiled and praised. However, when he looked at Hu Yun again, he found that the fox was already swaying, dizzy and disoriented. Just as Hu Yun was about to fall backward, Ji Yuan quickly reached out and caught him, and then picked up the unconscious red fox and placed him on the table.

T/n: I’m starting to appreciate Hu Yun’s character a lot more now. Go Hu Yun! I’m rooting for you!

Chapter 453: I Will Teach You

The scissors and scraps of paper on the stone table had all fallen to the ground. Ji Yuan looked at Hu Yun, who was fast asleep on the stone table; it was as if he was looking at the injured little fox from back then.

Just like he had all those years ago, Hu Yun was sleeping soundly and peacefully, because he knew that he would not be harmed in Ju’an Pavilion.

“Sleeping peacefully, I see.”

Ji Yuan didn’t bother to clean up the large pile of yellow paper scraps on the ground in the courtyard. He planned to wait until Hu Yun woke up so that he could take a look and clean it up himself. This wasn’t because Ji Yuan was reluctant to trouble himself, but rather because Ji Yuan knew that this would give Hu Yun a strong sense of accomplishment.

This feeling was actually similar to how, as a student in his past life, he would painstakingly complete several test papers or solve several difficult problems, and then look at the draft paper next to him filled with the notes he used to solve them.

If one were to truly consider the hardships and difficulties of studying the Tao, a mere three days was nothing. Regardless of anyone else’s experiences, even Ji Yuan himself sat on a mountaintop on an island for years in order to comprehend a sliver of true understanding.

But the spirit of seeking the Tao that Hu Yun demonstrated during these three days was truly precious. Ji Yuan had observed this feeling with careful scrutiny using his supernatural eyes. He believed that Hu Yun’s three days of unwavering dedication were an indication that he could continue on in this matter for three hundred years, or even longer.

After stretching and yawning, Ji Yuan prepared to leave. However, as he reached the courtyard gate, he turned back, looked around the courtyard, and then spoke.

“Don’t wake him up.”

These words were, of course, directed at the group of small calligraphy spirits. Although these small characters were usually noisy and liked to tease Hu Yun, they would never disrupt Hu Yun’s cultivation at crucial moments. They truly regarded this fox as one of their own.

“Don’t worry, Master, we’ll watch over him.”

The voice was very soft, coming from the character for “intent”1, which had pasted itself atop the courtyard gate.

“Hm.”

Ji Yuan responded, then opened the door and went out.

It was early morning, and there were quite a few residents coming and going from Tian Niu Lane. However, few were familiar faces. Most people would just stare at Ji Yuan when they crossed paths, and occasionally, when he met someone he knew, they would greet each other earnestly.

As he passed through the alley outside Shuang Jing Lane, he encountered an old man, who was slightly taken aback when he spotted Ji Yuan. He paused slightly, then quickly took a few steps closer to examine him.

“Mr. Ji? Is it really you?”

Upon hearing the voice, although it had changed slightly due to age, Ji Yuan immediately remembered him.

“It’s you, Brother Qian.”

This person wasn’t particularly close to Ji Yuan, but he used to greet him whenever he saw him. Of course, he was still in his prime back then, or at least, he had black hair, but now his body was hunched over.

“Sir, you still remember me? That’s wonderful! I heard you were back, but I hadn’t seen you yet. But the fragrance of jujube blossoms, which I haven’t smelled in the neighborhood for many years, finally returned. I figured you must be back. I can sleep so well at night with this fragrance.”

“Haha, it does smell nice.”

Ji Yuan stopped and chatted casually with the other man for a few minutes, before saying goodbye and leaving.

Perhaps this timing marked a turning point, because after that, he ran into several more people who recognized him. After leaving Tian Niu Lane, Sun’s noodle stall was packed with people, and there wasn’t a single empty seat.

“Grandpa, Mr. Ji is here!”

Sun Yaya, with her sharp eyes, saw Ji Yuan exit Tian Niu Lane from afar and quickly notified her grandfather, Sun Fu.

Sun Fu looked at the tables in front of the stall, which were now unusually full.

“What do we do now? There are no seats available!”

At a table near the wooden cart, one of the four young men immediately turned to Sun Fu.

“Uncle Sun, we’ll eat quickly and make room for Mr. Ji right away.”

The young man who spoke was the same one who had seen Sun Fu recognize Ji Yuan when Ji Yuan returned last time. He was somewhat related to Sun Fu by blood, and they had a deep impression of Ji Yuan and hoped to run into him again.

“Okay, then you guys eat quickly!”

The two of them quickly shoveled down the noodles in their bowls, making loud slurping noises. But even as they were still stuffing their faces with noodles, Ji Yuan had already reached the stall.

Sun Fu had clearly seen Mr. Ji walking slowly, but he hadn’t expected him to arrive so quickly. Just as he was about to inform Ji Yuan that there would be a seat soon, Ji Yuan smiled and spoke.

“Why don’t you two just sit on one bench? There’s no need to eat so fast. What if you choke?”

The young man was taken aback for a moment, then quickly dragged his bowl to his companion’s side. The latter also hurriedly shifted his position to make room. Eating at the same table as Mr. Ji was even better than standing to the side.

Sun Fu quickly came over to wipe down the table.

“Mr. Ji, please have a seat. The usual, yes?”

“Yes, same as always.”

The other diners probably didn’t recognize Ji Yuan; they just glanced at him and then went back to eating their noodles. Ji Yuan sat down at the table, nodded to a few curious young people, and then turned his gaze to Sun Yaya by the food cart.

Ji Yuan remembered that the little girl was also by the stall when he came back last time. It happened to be a day off then, so he didn’t think much of it. But today was different. This girl should be at the academy.

Ji Yuan gently waved to Sun Yaya, who swung the branch she was using as a wooden sword, turned around, and with two exclamations of “hey~ ho~”, she lightly and cheerfully arrived at Ji Yuan’s side.

“Mr. Ji, you called me?”

Ji Yuan looked at the branch in the little girl’s hand. It was straight, and the bark had been peeled off, indicating that she had obviously been playing with it for a long time. Looking at her chubby little face and bright, lively eyes, he thought she would surely grow up to be a beautiful and spirited girl.

‘The Sun family certainly has good genes.’

“Yaya, why aren’t you at school? The academy isn’t closed today. Has your family never sent you to school before?”

Sun Yaya pouted and shook her head, swaying her body.

“Grandpa sent me to school a long time ago, but I don’t want to go anymore.”

“Why not?”

Sun Yaya lowered her head and waved the branch in her hand, fidgeting and unwilling to answer. Sun Fugang was about to speak, but Ji Yuan didn’t even look up, instead signaling with his hand for him to be quiet.

‘Should I tell Mr. Ji?’

Sun Yaya swayed back and forth, and hesitated for a moment before speaking.

“I don’t want to go to school anymore, they’re bullying me!”

“How are they bullying you? Doesn’t the teacher care?”

Since she had already started, Sun Yaya just poured her heart out.

“The teacher does try to discipline us, but he’s not always around, and the more he disciplines us, the more resentful they become. That’s not all: what’s most infuriating is that there are only three girls in the school, including me. They laugh at us all the time for being clueless, for not being able to recite our lessons, for not being able to answer the teacher’s questions in class, for not being able to write well, and even sprinkle water on our seats… I stood up for the other two and hit the boy who sprinkled water, and the day before they were so grateful to me. But over the next few days they just distanced themselves from me, just like the boys…”

Sun Yaya’s voice trailed off as she spoke, her voice softened, and her head remained lowered. Judging from the change in her tone, she was about to cry.

Sun Fu felt very sorry for his granddaughter. He brought over a bowl of noodles and placed it in front of Ji Yuan’s table, then quickly went to pat her on the shoulder.

“It’s okay, it’s alright. If Yaya doesn’t want to go, then you don’t have to go.”

Ji Yuan shook his head and spoke.

“Yaya, putting aside what the other students are doing, I want to ask you: do you think studying itself is good, or bad?”

Sun Yaya wiped her face with her sleeve and looked up at Ji Yuan.

“It’s good…”

“Yes, studying is a good path. It’s not just for girls; many boys don’t have this opportunity either. You and your classmates are just not familiar with each other yet. Once you get to know each other, you’ll all become good friends.”

Sun Yaya hesitated and didn’t say anything. She glanced at the noodles on the table, and gave a reminder.

“Mr. Ji, please eat your noodles quickly, or they will become soggy and won’t taste good.”2

“Haha!”

Ji Yuan smiled, ruffled Sun Yaya’s hair, and turned around. He picked up his chopsticks and started stirring the noodles to mix them into the sauce. After taking a couple of bites, he lowered his head and said to Sun Yaya.

“How about this, Yaya: go to school. After school, you can come to Ju’an Pavilion. I will teach you calligraphy and reading. Aren’t those children laughing at you? Let’s see if they’ll still be laughing when your studies are better than theirs, and the teacher treats you like a treasure!”

At these words, Ji Yuan added another sentence.

“My calligraphy is quite good, definitely better than your teacher’s!”

Because Yin Zhaoxian had once been a teacher at the Ning’an County School, the school had a very strong academic atmosphere. Children with good grades were respected.

Hearing Ji Yuan’s words, Sun Yaya’s eyes immediately lit up. Meanwhile, Sun Fu, who was serving the cooked mutton offal, subconsciously trembled, but pretended to be nonchalant, picking out the offal that could have been scooped up in two or three spoonfuls and putting it into the bowl one by one.

“How about it?”

Ji Yuan swallowed the noodles in his mouth, then asked with a smile. Sun Yaya looked at her grandfather and saw him wink at her before immediately going back to work. The little girl then looked at Ji Yuan, glanced in the direction of Tian Niu Lane, and finally smiled as she smelled the fragrance of the jujube flowers.

“Mr. Ji, you I hope you’re not lying to me!”

“Hahaha, young lady, why would I lie to you?”

Sun Fu inside the kitchen stall felt relieved. He quickly prepared the bowl of offal and brought it over.

“Mr. Ji, your lamb offal soup is ready. Come, be careful, it’s hot. Enjoy!”

Sun Fu came over with a smile and placed the mutton offal in front of Ji Yuan.

Ji Yuan nodded, picked up some of the food, and placed a portion into the bowls of the two young men.

“Thank you to both of you young men for giving up your seats.”

“Oh, you’re welcome, you’re welcome!”

“Thank you, Mr. Ji!”

The two young men were flattered. Because of Uncle Sun’s attitude earlier, they had asked their elders about Mr. Ji of Ju’an Pavilion and heard that he was an extraordinary person, especially since he was close friends with Star Scholar Yin. He was definitely a prominent figure in Ning’an County, though not many people knew that now.

While eating the mutton offal that Ji Yuan had given them, and seeing that Ji Yuan and Sun Yaya had finished talking, the two young men exchanged a glance. One of them tentatively asked a question.

“Mr. Ji, may I ask how old you are?”

Ji Yuan paused, his chopsticks still. This question really stumped him. He didn’t know how old the original beggar’s body in the Mountain God Temple was. If he were to consider his own age, should he include his past life?

After thinking for a moment, he could only reply with a smile.

“Older than you think, anyway.”

“Oh, oh!”

Sun Fu laughed and scolded from off to the side.

“You two brats, what nonsense are you asking? Your noodles are on me today, so eat up and get the hell out of here!”

“Oh dear, Uncle Sun is kicking us out!”

“Exactly! Let us finish our meal before we leave, we haven’t finished eating yet!”

The two chuckled and squeezed onto a stool together, and joked around.

T/n: thank you Despyte for the cool info: in China the main worries about leaving noodles uneaten for too long are the fact that bloated noodles (that have sucked all the soup/water/etc) are more filling (and prevent you from eating the rest of the meal) and that after a while the noodles clump (坨, tuo) together

Chapter 454: Yin Qing’s Good News

When Ji Yuan returned home, Hu Yun was still asleep. Ji Yuan figured the fox wouldn’t wake up anytime soon, so he spread out his brush and ink on the empty space of the stone table and began to write down his thoughts on his wondrous technique.

In Ju’an Pavilion, unless it was necessary for spiritual practice, Ji Yuan always preferred to follow a normal daily routine, going back to his house at night and leaving at dawn.

The next day, before Ji Yuan rose from sleep late in the morning, he heard Hu Yun stir earlier than he had expected. It seemed that Hu Yun had been thinking about his paper-cutting.

When Ji Yuan opened the door and went out, he saw Hu Yun sweeping the yard. Although he probably hadn’t recovered his mental energy yet, he was in high spirits.

Hu Yun knew he probably couldn’t continue to add more paper talismans, so he didn’t continue cutting them, nor did he rush to combine them. Hu Yun knew that his cultivation level wasn’t high enough to skip Mr. Ji’s guidance, and blindly combining talismans would make all his previous efforts go to waste.

Hu Yun didn’t use a broom or anything, but instead used his claws and tail to sweep. After cleaning for a while, he would stand up and look at the yellow paper talismans on the stone table and bamboo basket, his face full of happiness.

Hearing the door open and knowing that Ji Yuan had come out, he immediately scurried over.

“Mr. Ji, may I begin combining talismans today?”

Ji Yuan looked at the fox’s impatient appearance and nodded.

“Sure, but you can cut out two more paper figures to practice. Combining two won’t do any good, but it will help you get familiar with the process. Combining the figures is simpler than cutting and shaping them. I’m just afraid you’ll forget the feeling after you take a nap.”

“Yes, yes, I’ll listen to you, sir!”

Hu Yun replied with a grin, and began to learn how to combine the talisman figures according to Ji Yuan’s teachings.

By afternoon, Hu Yun finally felt he could stop practicing. Although he was still uneasy, he knew that continuing to practice would yield minimal improvement, so he chose to combine the talismans under Ji Yuan’s supervision.

The process was much smoother than Hu Yun had expected. The talisman was completed almost without any obstacles. Finally, a thin paper figure appeared in his paw. Judging from its appearance alone, it did not seem that much different from the Warrior Talisman in Ji Yuan’s hand. However, both parties understood the true difference between the two talismans.

Nonetheless, at this moment, Hu Yun was filled with excitement. After imbuing the paper figure with the initial drops of his blood that it needed, he couldn’t wait to test the talisman he had crafted. He stood on the stone table, tossed it forward, and shouted solemnly.

“I call upon my Warrior!”

As soon as he finished speaking, the paper figure transformed into streaks of red light, eventually appearing before Hu Yun as a dark gray figure. Upon closer inspection, he could vaguely see glimpses of the far wall through its translucent body. Aside from its dark gray coloring, its face had a faint red glow.

There was no golden armor, of course. Hu Yun didn’t have the mental power to visualize it, nor did he have the spiritual power to condense it. So the figure simply appeared like a human draped in thick gray cloth.

Upon appearing, the figure bowed to Hu Yun, but did not speak. Hu Yun, like many others, lacked the ability to visualize the necessary supernatural organs within its body using his mind and spiritual power during the cutting process.

“Hahahaha… It’s done, it’s done! Mr. Ji, my Warrior Talisman is complete!”

Ji Yuan smiled and looked up and down at the dark gray figure. Its features were barely discernible under the faint red glow. Calling it a warrior was an understatement; it resembled a monster more than it did a warrior.

However, it was certainly not the worst it could have been. The one that Hu Yun had refined was many times stronger than the one that Master Du had made back then. At least Ji Yuan felt that the talisman in front of him could be useful, as it had incorporated many of Hu Yun’s unique ideas and perspective.

“This doesn’t quite fit the description of a Warrior.”

Hu Yun, of course, understood his own shortcomings agreed with Mr. Ji’s assessment. He nodded in agreement.

“Indeed, it doesn’t look as imposing as the Golden Armored Warrior, but it’s something I forged myself. By the way, Mr. Ji, what should this be called?”

As he spoke, Hu Yun pointed with his paw, and the figure in front of him suddenly turned into a dark shadow, wandering around the courtyard like a ghost, circling the jujube tree a few times, going around the kitchen, before finally returning to the table.

Ji Yuan understood: since there were only thirty-six movements visualized, this creature remained mostly still as it moved, but thanks to Hu Yun’s visualization and spiritual technique, its movements were still swift.

“Haha, you created it, so you can name it yourself.”

…

Hu Yun naturally came over from time to time while Ji Yuan stayed at Ju’an Pavilion. However, the fox didn’t stay in the pavilion all the time; he would only come once every now and then, spending the rest of his time in the mountains.

Ji Yuan was not lonely in Ju’an Pavilion. Besides the strange and wonderful spirits around him, the most important thing was that there was a little girl who often ran over to visit the pavilion. She was Sun Fu’s beloved granddaughter, Sun Yaya.

The little girl brought her own writing brush, ink, paper, and inkstone. She would always excitedly arrive at Ji Yuan’s Ju’an Pavilion. Ji Yuan’s calligraphy was very beautiful, and she really wanted to learn it.

At first, the Sun family would bring her over to the pavilion and then pick her up. Later, she came by herself, and the Sun family would only pick her up after. Eventually, Sun Yaya arrived alone, practiced calligraphy, and went home by herself.

Sun Yaya’s arrival instantly made Ju’an Pavilion more vibrant. The cheerful and lively atmosphere even extended beyond the pavilion. Whenever people entered Tian Niu Lane, Sun Yaya, dressed in a well-fitting white scholar’s robe, would greet them on the street.

Many residents of Tian Niu Lane knew this lively and lovely little girl was learning calligraphy from Mr. Ji. Even those who didn’t already know Ji Yuan learned his name indirectly through Sun Yaya.

However, although Hu Yun, Xiao Zi, and Zhi He were very curious about Sun Yaya and even liked her, they all avoided her, and tried to prevent her from seeing them.

Half a year passed peacefully. Sun Yaya’s complaints about the academy gradually turned into cheerful banter. After half a year, Sun Yaya became a star at the academy. Even the teacher said that although Sun Yaya’s writing was still immature, it already possessed a certain charm, and that she was a promising talent.

One day, the little girl was practicing calligraphy in the Ju’an Pavilion when, in a flash of inspiration, she wrote the character she was most satisfied with to date.

“Teacher, look at my handwriting for the character ‘elegant’.1 Isn’t it much better now?”

Sun Yaya grabbed her brush and paper and rushed to the kitchen, eagerly showing them to Ji Yuan, who was chopping vegetables. Ji Yuan glanced at them symbolically, nodded, and praised her.

“Not bad, it is indeed more spirited than before, but it’s not enough. The characters should express their meaning, so think more about their essence when you write.”

“Understood!”

Sun Yaya rushed back to the courtyard to practice her calligraphy. Every time Mr. Ji seemed to offer casual praise, the next day when she showed it to the school teacher, the teacher would often be full of praise, and perhaps even astonished. These moments were particularly fun, and Sun Yaya was already looking forward to them.

“Knock knock knock…”

“Is Mr. Ji here?”

Sun Yaya put down her brush and looked at the kitchen.

“Sir, I’ll go open the door.”

“Go ahead, go ahead.”

The little girl ran excitedly to the courtyard gate, opened it, and saw an official standing outside. While Sun Yaya was sizing him up, the official was also looking at her.

“Who are you, and what brings you to see Mr. Ji?”

The officer smiled and spoke in a slightly louder voice.

“I am an official from the county government, currently in charge of postal mail for Tian Niu Lane and the two neighboring districts. Today I received a letter for Mr. Ji sealed with red wax, which I am bringing here specifically!”

These kinds of wax-sealed letters could only be used by high-ranking officials of the imperial court. When they arrived at the county government office, the bailiffs would not dare delay and would deliver them directly.

Ji Yuan came out, wiping his hands with a cloth. He bowed to the bailiff at the gate and then took the letter. A quick glance revealed that it was signed by Yin Qing.

“Thank you for bringing it, sir. Would you like to come in and have some tea?”

Ji Yuan stepped aside, but the official dared not enter and repeatedly declined.

“No, no, I have official business to attend to, so I won’t disturb you any longer.”

“Oh, then please accept this as a gift, official, in place of treating you to tea!”

Ji Yuan, being very polite, took out a five-wen coin. The bailiff feigned reluctance a couple times before accepting, then bowed and left.

By the time Ji Yuan closed the courtyard gate, Sun Yaya had already been sneaking on tiptoes to peer at the letter for quite some time.

“Sir, whose letter is this, and what does it say?”

“Haha, this is a letter from a very important person. It was written by Yin Qing, the current Vice Minister of Rites.”

Sun Yaya frowned and thought for a moment.

“Yin Qing? Hmm! Surname Yin? Could he be Star Scholar Yin’s son?”

“That’s right.”

Ji Yuan gently pinched Sun Yaya’s nose, then opened the envelope. He took out the letter, shook it, and ran his fingertips over the paper, feeling the essence of the characters to decipher the content.

In the letter, Yin Qing mentioned he had gotten engaged to Princess Changping, and the wedding was to take place a year later. He was inviting Ji Yuan in advance to attend if it was convenient for him, and informing him that he would take Princess Changping back to Ning’an County with him. If Mr. Ji was home, he would definitely come to invite him over.

Yin Zhaoxian was no longer a minor official of Wan Province. When his son got married, all the imperial relatives and civil and military officials of the court would definitely attend. Ji Yuan didn’t care for that kind of scene, but when it came to Yin Qing’s wedding he had to attend no matter what.

“This kid moves quickly!”

“Sir, sir, what does it say? Can you tell me?”

Ji Yuan looked down at Sun Yaya.

“It’s nothing, Yin Qing is getting married.”

“What? Yin Qing is getting married? So fast!”

No sooner had she finished speaking than a surprised voice suddenly rang out from the courtyard, startling Sun Yaya. She turned and looked around the courtyard but couldn’t find anyone speaking. She tugged at Ji Yuan’s sleeve.

“Sir, it sounded like someone was talking just now!”

Sun Yaya’s gaze swept around, and suddenly she sensed a fiery red silhouette beside the stone table. As she stared at it intently, the silhouette grew clearer and clearer, until she finally saw a fox squatting there.

T/n: I’m baaaack! Thank you all for your patience as real life came at me like a truck.

RIP Hu Yun, he was definitely not made for subtlety.

雅 (yǎ): elegant. This character is the one repeated twice in Sun Yaya’s name.

Chapter 455: The Couple Returns Home

Sun Yaya rubbed her eyes to make sure she wasn’t seeing things, but she didn’t actually know what foxes looked like; she had never seen a live fox, only their pelts in the market, which were brightly colored.

“Sir, is there a big red dog over there?”

Sun Yaya tugged at Ji Yuan’s sleeve and pointed to Hu Yun by the stone table. Ji Yuan glanced at Hu Yun, hearing her words, and that the latter’s hair was indeed standing on end.

“I’m not a dog! Have you ever seen such a beautiful dog? I’m a fox! A fox! A red fox!”

Hu Yun stood up, baring his teeth and brandishing his claws, and jumped in front of Sun Yaya, gesturing wildly as he argued with her.1 He hated dogs more than anything else in his life, followed by thugs and hooligans.

This terrified the little girl, who immediately shrank behind Ji Yuan, hugged him, and screamed.

“Sir! Sir! It can talk! It can talk!!!”

Hu Yun immediately covered his mouth and cautiously looked at Ji Yuan, who shook his head slightly and walked back into the courtyard.

Sun Yaya followed nervously, her eyes fixed on Hu Yun.

“Sir, this…this fox can talk, is it a monster? Will it eat people?”

Ji Yuan walked straight to the stone table in the courtyard, sat down, and carefully read the contents of Yin Qing’s letter. He pointed to the paper on the table and spoke.

“Yaya, practice your calligraphy.”2

“But, but…”

Sun Yaya hesitated, her eyes never leaving Hu Yun. Then she looked at Ji Yuan, only to find that her teacher was not reacting in any particular way.

Hu Yun walked back into the courtyard, put his two front paws on his hips, pointed at Sun Yaya, and then pointed at himself.

“You…can you see me?”

Sun Yaya glanced at Ji Yuan again and noticed that he was engrossed in reading the last few pages of the letter and hadn’t noticed what was happening here. Recalling the fox’s words, a thought suddenly struck her.

‘Could it be that Mr. Ji can’t see the fox, and that’s why he’s ignoring it?’

Although Sun Yaya did not answer, the little girl’s gaze was fixed on him, making Hu Yun realize that he had asked a pointless question. So he gathered his monstrous power again and used his spiritual technique.

That wasn’t enough. Hu Yun knew that Mr. Ji had mentioned the issue of focus, so he shifted his body and jumped behind the stone-slab-covered well in the courtyard, making himself disappear from Sun Yaya’s sight. Then he tiptoed out from behind the well again.

Sure enough, Hu Yun saw that Sun Yaya was still staring at the well and hadn’t noticed that he had come out, which meant that she couldn’t see him anymore.

Ji Yuan glanced at Hu Yun. He had also seen the Red Fox’s actions. Looking at Sun Yaya’s reaction, he could only say that the little girl’s own spiritual sense had grown increasingly sharp.

The little girl noticed that the fox hadn’t come out after quite some time. She glanced at Ji Yuan, then walked around the trunk of the jujube tree to the well, and looked around, only to find that the fox wasn’t there. She looked around but couldn’t find it anywhere else.

“Yaya, practice your calligraphy.”

Ji Yuan called out again.

“Oh…”

Hearing her teacher call her for the second time, the little girl responded and obediently returned to the table, picked up her brush, and began practicing calligraphy. However, she kept absentmindedly glancing around the courtyard.

Just as dusk fell and the hour of You arrived,3 Sun Yaya bid farewell to Mr. Ji and left Ju’an Pavilion to go home. As soon as Sun Yaya left, Hu Yun immediately jumped out again.

“Mr. Ji, Yaya really saw me just now! Although it was partly because I spoke up to get her attention, her eyesight has improved a lot.”

“There’s nothing to be surprised about. Young children, especially toddlers, are very perceptive and can see strange things. As Yaya’s spiritual perception is growing stronger, so it’s not surprising that she could see you.”

Then Ji Yuan added another sentence.

“Many children may have seen some supernatural things when they were young, but they suffer from protective amnesia as they grow up.”

Hu Yun made a mental note of it, then jumped onto the stone bench and looked at the letter on the table.

“Sir, when exactly is Yin Qing getting married? If you’re going to his wedding, can you take me with you?”

“No rush, no rush. You don’t need to travel. He will bring his fiancée to Ning’an County and personally visit some close relatives like his uncles and cousins. Of course, he will also come visit Ju’an Pavilion.”

According to the customs of Ning’an County, an engagement could either be a big deal or a casual affair. Some betrothal arrangements only required a verbal promise. However, for weddings, one had to personally visit relatives to invite them.

Given Yin Zhaoxian and Yin Qing’s circumstances, whether due to their status or busy schedules, this step could have been omitted. However, for some reason, Yin Qing clearly wanted to bring Princess Changping along for the invitation.

“Really? When will they arrive?”

Hu Yun suddenly became excited. Compared to the restrictive environment of the capital, Ning’an County was much more comfortable. He could even take Yin Qing to visit Niu Kui Mountain.

Nowadays, none of the spirits, whether they be jackals, tigers, or leopards, dared to provoke Hu Yun. It wasn’t that Hu Yun was powerful enough to be invincible within Niu Kui Mountain, but rather that the animals and spirits in the mountains were afraid of Lu Shanjun, and by extension were are also afraid of Hu Yun, who often appeared alongside him and shared a similar scent.

Niu Kui Mountain even lacked a mountain god for quite some time now entirely due to Lu Shanjun’s presence. The name “Lu Shanjun,” which Hu Yun had learned from a group of spirits, meant “Lord of the Mountains,” and in some obscure tomes it was also used to refer to a fierce tiger that reigned supreme over the mountains.

Unless necessary, Ji Yuan didn’t bother to divine his friends’ trivial matters, but it was clearly permissible in this situation. So, after a quick calculation, he smiled at Hu Yun.

“Their wedding boat is already on the Chunmu River, so probably in a few days. You can make preparations, it might be difficult for you to attend his wedding.”

“Okay, I’ll head back to Niu Kui Mountain now!”

With that, Hu Yun jumped off the stone bench and leapt over the wall. After he left, Ji Yuan frowned slightly as he looked at the letter in his hand.

“It seems Qing’er trusts his future wife very much!”

The fact that he could directly state in his letter that he would bring Princess Changping to Ju’an Pavilion, based on Ji Yuan’s understanding of Yin Qing, implied that there were no secrets between husband and wife. Otherwise, Yin Qing might not have even planned this trip to Ning’an County.

Indeed, it wasn’t just to satisfy Ji Yuan; he knew that Yin Qing’s trip back to his hometown with Princess Changping was less about inviting the elders of the family according to local customs, and more about inviting Ji Yuan specifically.

…

In Chunhui Prefecture, Yin Qing led Princess Changping on a tour of the academy where he had studied. They also went to the large willow tree reaching out over the river, while some of the servants and guards stayed a little further away.

“Dear fiancé,4 is this the place you used to come to study when you were a student?”

“Indeed, this is the place where I came to recite poems to the river, both to cultivate my character and to let the fish and shrimp in the river hear them.”

Princess Changping covered her mouth with her fan and smiled.

“Yes, yes, that was an interesting story you told me, dear fiancé. You said that when you were studying here, a big fish and an old turtle would occasionally come by and linger in the water.

“By the way, dear fiancé, how big were that fish and turtle?”

Seeing Princess Changping’s playful expression, Yin Qing made a serious gesture with both hands.

“Bigger than a person, definitely bigger than you think.”

At this moment, two guards came from the direction of the city. They were not dressed in guard uniforms, but rather in tight-fitting clothes, each carrying a jug of wine.

“Sir, we have bought it.”

“What is this? Wine?”

Princess Changping asked in confusion. Yin Qing picked up one of the jars, shook it, and explained.

“This is Chunhui Prefecture’s famous wine: Qianrichun. These two small jars… right, they’re twenty years old, aren’t they?”

“Reporting to Your Excellency, both jars are 20-year-old Qianrichun. We watched the owner of Yuanzipu dig them up from the wine cellar in the warehouse.”

Yin Qing nodded.

“Alright, you didn’t shortchange him, did you?”

The guard smiled.

“Of course not. If the shopkeeper hadn’t insisted that he wouldn’t sell them, I wouldn’t have shown him the imperial order for procurement.”

“Next time, if you can’t buy a 20-year-old one, then forget it. There’s no need to show the imperial decree.”

Yin Qing offered a light criticism, and the guards quickly clasped their hands and acknowledged it. Their main duty was to protect Princess Changping, but they dared not disobey Lord Yin.

“Alright, there are so many places worth visiting in Chunhui Prefecture. Let’s come back another day to explore them properly. Today we visited the academy and the River God Temple, and strolled along the riverbank. That’s about enough. We should board the boat and set off now.”

“Yes, we’ll do as you say, my lord!”

Thus, the group turned around and started walking towards the dock.

“Splash…”

From the willows along the far bank in the other side of river came the sound of water rippling. Princess Changping subconsciously turned her head to look and vaguely saw a very large fish swimming away in the water.

Following the Chunmu River and branching off onto the Xiaoshun River, the large tower ship finally docked at the pier at the foot of Laohua Mountain. Yin Qing and Princess Changping waited on the ship for a while before boarding a carriage prepared by the servants, and traveled along the mountain road through Laohua Mountain.

While admiring the scenery along the mountain road, Princess Changping suddenly asked a question.

“Dear fiancé, is there really a clear pool deep in Laohua Mountain, with little fish whose bones melt when boiled in soup?”

“Yes, there is indeed…”

…

As a princess and her prospective husband, even if they tried to keep a low profile, they still needed a large entourage. Three carriages and a dozen or so riders accompanied them. In a bustling place like Chunhui Prefecture, this wouldn’t be particularly notable, but in a “countryside” region like Ning’an County, they immediately stood out from the crowd.

As the carriage and its entourage entered the county, many people nearby whispered among themselves, speculating about the identity of the important figures who had just arrived. The carriage proceeded to Tian Niu Lane under this scrutiny, without sending anyone to inform the county government.

T/n: subtlety, thy name is Hu Yun

T/n: Ji Yuan, gaslighter extraordinaire XD

~5-7pm, dusk

So the literal translation here is “my lord,” which can also be translated as “husband,” but they are not married yet and “my lord” is just weird, so I’m using “dear betrothed” or “dear fiancé”

Chapter 456: The Elders of Ju’an Pavilion

After the group passed the main road in the county, Yin Qing ordered everyone to dismount and get out of the carriages. He had some servants take charge of the carriages and horses and bring them to an inn, then led the remaining necessary attendants to Tian Niu Lane.

A short while later, the guards and servants in the group began to look around inexplicably or exchange bewildered glances. Even Princess Changping looked puzzled. Seeing that Yin Qing did not have any special expression, she asked him a question.

“My lord, can you smell that? It smells wonderful! This fragrance is so lovely…”

Yin Qing smiled and gazed into the distance towards Tian Niu Lane.

“Ping’er, that’s the jujube blossom fragrance I told you about, the fragrance of the jujube blossoms in Ju’an Pavilion. This tree doesn’t bloom every year, but when it does, the fragrance fills Ning’an County. My father wrote a line in one of his treatises: ‘Green and yellow blossoms adorn the branches, and half the county is filled with their fragrance.’ That line comes from this phenomenon.”

On the streets outside Tian Niu Lane, Sun’s noodle stall was open as usual. Sun Yaya was naturally not at the stall. Even when she wasn’t in school, she was practicing calligraphy with Ji Yuan.

Yin Qingyuan led Princess Changping over, with plainclothes guards positioned at varying distances, some in front and some on the left and right, but generally in a position that would not disturb the couple. Even so, four maids and servants still accompanied them.

This was still quite conspicuous for the people in Ning’an County. Sun Fu, a seasoned businessman who knew how to read people, was even more astute. Seeing this commotion from afar, he knew that the newcomers were important, so he instructed the four or five customers at his stall:

“Hey, you lot, just eat your noodles and stop staring at those people over there, especially the women, understand?”

“Oh? Old Sun, you know them?”

A chubby young diner looked up at the distance and asked Sun Fu.

Although life in Ning’an County was relatively good, it was still rare for ordinary people to be fat. Even if they didn’t go hungry, they still had to work. This customer was an exception. Since he was a regular at the stall, Sun Fu had a deep impression of him, so he gave him a special explanation.

“You people are too naive and don’t know how dangerous it is outside. Although I don’t know those people over there, judging from their entourage, they must be high-ranking officials or nobles. If you dare to stare at their women, someone with a bad temper will gouge your eyes out!”

“Oh dear, is it that serious?”

Sun Fu nodded.

“Of course! If you have a discreet eye, you can just glance and that’s fine, but the women from those powerful and wealthy families are all so beautiful, I’m afraid you guys won’t be able to stop staring at them.”

Sun Fu deliberately exaggerated the situation to avoid causing trouble at his stall.

“Okay, okay!”

“Okay, let’s hurry up and eat our noodles.”

After chatting for a while at the stall, seeing Yin Qing and the others approaching, the stall owner and the diners stopped talking about the outsiders and shifted the topic to some trivial mundane matters.

However, when Yin Qing and his group grew close, they did not walk directly past the stall. Instead, they slowed down just outside of it. Yin Qing even bowed to Sun Fu.

“Hello, Uncle Sun! It’s so nice to see that your noodle stall is still open.”

Yin Qing knew, of course, that the stall owner had changed long ago. The current stall owner was Sun Fu, the son of the Old Man Sun. However, Sun Fu did not recognize Yin Qing. Seeing such a high-ranking official bowing to him, even though it seemed like this man knew him, he was still a little intimidated.

Sun Fu wiped his hands on his apron and quickly returned the greeting.

“May I ask who you are?”

“Oh, I am Yin Qing, the son of Master Yin from the county school back then.”

Upon hearing this, Sun Fu immediately understood.

“Oh, you are…you are Star Scholar Yin’s son. I heard you’re a high-ranking official in the court?”

“Haha, Uncle Sun, you flatter me. By the way, is Mr. Ji at home?”

Sun Fu hurriedly replied.

“Yes, yes.”

“Okay, you must be busy.”

“Ah, um-!”

Yin Qing turned and walked towards the gate of Tian Niu Lanenot far away, while Princess Changping smiled and nodded at Sun Fu before leaving with Yin Qing.

As soon as they left, the diners who had been silent at the stall quickly asked Sun Fu about Yin Qing. Some even paid their bills in a hurry and left to tell their acquaintances that Star Scholar Yin’s son had returned. Sun Fu slapped his thigh as he chatted, realizing that Sun Yaya was still at Ju’an Pavilion.

Yin Qing felt the same as he had as a child as he walked through the quiet alleys of Tian Niu Lane. It was nearly unchanged. However, most of the residents hid when they saw the commotion of his entourage. Although Yin Qing wanted to greet them, he didn’t want to frighten them, so he let them be.

As they approached Ju’an Pavilion, Yin Qing turned back and gave some instructions to his entourage.

“Everyone else stay outside. Her Highness the Princess, the two head guards, and I will go in.”

“Yes!”

The servants behind them responded in unison.

In fact, Yin Qing didn’t want to bring either of the two head guards, but he had no choice. Giving such an order would put the two of them in a difficult position. Although they knew the trip was safe, they were acting on the emperor’s orders to protect Princess Changping. Dismissing the guards and attendants was already the bottom line, and the two head guards had to be present.

As they approached Ju’an Pavilion, the large jujube tree came into view, resembling a large canopy. The fragrance of its blossoms seemed to have intensified. The scent wasn’t overpowering, but rather refreshing and pleasant.

Soon the other servants stopped, and the four stood alone in front of the small pavilion gate, almost everyone looking up at the plaque above.

“What beautiful calligraphy! It’s certainly on par with Prime Minister Yin’s calligraphy!”

Princess Changping offered a compliment, but Yin Qing felt that although his father’s calligraphy was quite good, it still paled in comparison to Mr. Ji’s.

Just as a guard was about to knock on the door, Yin Qing raised his hand to stop him, then stepped forward and gently knocked on the gate of the small courtyard.

“Knock knock knock…”

“Mr. Ji, it’s me, Yin Qing!”

A series of light footsteps came from the courtyard, followed by the sound of a door latch being pulled.

“Creeak…”

The courtyard gate opened, revealing a little girl dressed in a scholar’s white robe, looking at Yin Qing and Princess Changping with curiosity.

“Qing’er is here? Come in.”

“Teacher wants you to come in!”

Sun Yaya said, stepping aside. Yin Qing and Princess Changping then entered the small courtyard with the two head guards. Apart from Yin Qing, the other three were observing the current state of Ju’an Pavilion and Ji Yuan himself.

The courtyard was very clean and simply furnished. There was a well that had been covered with a stone slab, a stone table, four stone stools, and the jujube tree whose fragrance filled half the county. Ji Yuan had been sitting behind the stone table and had just gotten up to greet everyone.

“This humble commoner Ji Yuan greets Her Highness Princess Changping!”

Ji Yuan bowed to Princess Changping and winked at Sun Yaya, who had returned to his side. The latter immediately understood and followed suit.

“This humble commoner, Sun Yaya, greets Her Highness Princess Changping!”

“Sir, there’s no need for such formality. And you, there’s no need for such formality either. I’m here accompanying my husband to deliver an invitation to our elders. I’m just a junior visiting. Please don’t make me feel uncomfortable.”

Ji Yuan nodded, straightened from his bow, and invited the group to sit down.

“Please have a seat, everyone. I’ll make you some tea.”

Seeing that Yin Qing was about to speak, Ji Yuan raised his hand to stop him and pointed to the stone table.

“Qing’er, take the princess to sit over there. According to the customs of Ning’an County, when the newlyweds visit for the first time, the elders are supposed to make them sweet tea. This tradition cannot be omitted. I will go and prepare it for you.”

Newlyweds arriving hand in hand to share sweet tea brewed by their elders was a gesture that symbolized a sweet and happy occasion. Since Ji Yuan had decreed it should be so, Yin Qing couldn’t refuse.

When Ji Yuan came out with the tea tray, the four teacups, a teapot, and a small ceramic jar had already been arranged atop it.

Ji Yuan personally poured hot tea for Yin Qing and Princess Changping, then opened the small ceramic jar and used a small wooden spoon to scoop some crystal-clear granules into the teacups.

Strangely enough, even in Ju’an Pavilion, surrounded by the fragrance of jujube blossoms, a uniquely sweet aroma wafted out as soon as Ji Yuan opened the ceramic jar. Just smelling it made the four of them feel comfortable.

“Please help yourselves!”

Ji Yuan placed the teacups in front of Yin Qing and Princess Changping, and then poured two more cups and handed them to the two guards.

“Since you’re not newlyweds, you won’t be drinking sweetened tea.”

The two guards were quick-witted and immediately thanked him, accepting the teacups.

“Thank you, sir!”

Yin Qing knew, of course, that this sweet tea was no ordinary tea. He nodded to Yang Ping, raised his cup to taste it first, and drank a mouthful. The sweet syrup went down his throat, leaving not only a mellow aftertaste, but also a refreshing sensation that flowed into his body along with a wave of warmth.

Princess Changping’s eyes widened upon tasting it, and then, captivated by its sweetness, her eyes squinted, narrowing to crescent moons in delight. Before she knew it, she had finished the entire bowl of sweetened tea in one gulp.

“Sir, the sugar tea from Ning’an County is so delicious! It’s more than worthy to be selected as a tribute to the emperor!”

Seeing that Yang Ping still seemed to want more, Yin Qing handed her his cup, which he had only taken a sip of.

“This sweet tea isn’t available everywhere, and there’s certainly not enough to make a tribute. Here, take mine!”

Yang Ping was quick-witted. She glanced at Ji Yuan, then at the tea set and the “sugar” jar on the table. She understood what was going on, as Ji Yuan hadn’t poured her another cup despite her previous words.

Yang Ping initially wanted to decline, but under Yin Qing’s gentle gaze and her lingering delight at the sweet tea, she accepted the teacup. This time, she savored it carefully, sipping it several times and tasting it thoroughly, before giving the remaining half-cup to Yin Qing.

“Hahaha… Seeing how affectionate you two are, I’m relieved!”

Ji Yuan chuckled and sat down on the stone bench opposite them. The two guards on either side of him were standing and didn’t want to sit.

Yang Ping glanced to one side of the stone table, where writing brushes, ink, paper, and inkstones had been laid out. Some of the papers had words written on them, but most of them were the same characters repeated many times. Although they were not written as part of a longer book or article, the words were very lively and elegant.

“Is this Mr. Ji’s calligraphy?”

Yang Ping asked, and Sun Yaya, who was eyeing the sugar jar with longing, quickly jumped up and down a couple of times.

“No, no, Princess, this is my handwriting! Mr. Ji’s is so beautiful, I dare not compare!

Yang Ping was slightly startled. How could such a young child, and a girl at that, write such beautiful characters? But judging from the situation, it was definitely not fake.

“Yaya, that’s enough for today. You can go home now and come back tomorrow.”

Sun Yaya was very excited; she had actually met a princess! But her face fell as soon as she heard Ji Yuan’s words.

“Mr. Ji…”

“Be good, listen to me.”

“Oh… then I’ll go back…”

Sun Yaya began to tidy up the writing implements on the table, putting them into a small book pack next to the stone table. Then, she picked up the small bamboo book pack and left the courtyard, glancing back every few steps.

As soon as Sun Yaya left, Ji Yuan glanced at the two guards on either side. The two of them then wandered away as if bewitched, standing beside the left and right courtyard walls. This surprised Yang Ping slightly, but Yin Qing did not react.

Ji Yuan nodded to Yang Ping.

“You do possess a certain composure befitting royalty!”
