Chapter 457: The Time for Departure Has Arrived

Although Princess Changping had already noticed the unusual situation in the courtyard, she felt no fear as long as Yin Qing sat calmly beside her.

Upon hearing Ji Yuan’s words and confirming that the two guards leaning against the wall were unharmed, Yang Ping turned her gaze away from them and replied to Ji Yuan.

“I am a royal daughter, so naturally I should have royal bearing.”

Yin Qing smiled slightly from the side.

“Sir, Ping’er and I will be getting married next year. We hope that you can come to the capital to attend the wedding if you have the time. It would be best if you could bring Hu Yun with you.”

As he spoke, Yin Qing took out a red-sealed invitation and handed it to Ji Yuan with both hands. The latter reached out and took it, stroking the gold-embossed characters on it. Then he looked at Yin Qing and Yang Ping, eyes filled with emotion.

“Qing’er is finally getting married!”

Ji Yuan stood up, walked to the trunk of the jujube tree, looked at the branches of the jujube tree in the courtyard, and then looked in the direction of Niu Kui Mountain.

“Those who know me in Ning’an County are slowly growing old. In a few years, people will return to dust. The story of my life in the small Ju’an Pavilion may circulate for twenty years more, but I cannot let the townsfolk think I am immortal. Perhaps being gradually forgotten is for the best.”

“Sir, I will never forget you.”

Yin Qing said in a flattering tone.

Upon hearing Ji Yuan’s words, Yang Ping gripped her skirt tightly with her left hand under the table. Noticing this, Yin Qing reached out and took her hand.

Yang Ping’s heart was racing, but she forced herself to remain calm and asked Ji Yuan in a composed tone.

“Sir, do you mean that you are an immortal?”

Ji Yuan turned and looked back at the stone table.

“To be precise, there are no absolute ‘immortals’ in the world. There are only those who cultivate immortality. In the bustling city, there are people who make a living by using talismans. You call them experts or even wandering magicians. In the high mountains, there are people who can summon wind and rain. You call them immortal masters. In reality, they are all the same kind of people. However, although there are no absolute ‘immortals,’ they all possess an immortal aura and spirit.”

Ji Yuan’s words were tantamount to an admission. Yang Ping gripped Yin Qing’s hand even tighter. For a moment she didn’t know what to say.

The Yang family once had a history of using the resources of an entire nation to seek immortality, and now a man who seemed to be an immortal was standing right before them.

“Your Highness, with your intelligence, you should know that the Yang royal family’s several attempts to seek immortality were destined to be fruitless… well, not entirely. They were certainly destined not to achieve the kinds of results they desired.”

The old beggar’s situation prevented Ji Yuan from making a definitive statement, but the meaning was still conveyed.

“Qing’er truly cares for you, which is why he brought you to Ju’an Pavilion. I once told him that couples can have their own little privacies, but it’s best not to have any big secrets between you.”

Yang Ping subconsciously looked at Yin Qing, who was also watching her. Once Yang Ping married into the Yin family, there were only two outcomes: either she would be kept out of all supernatural matters, or her elders would treat her like their own daughter, and her husband would support her through thick and thin.

Clearly, Yin Qing was willing to believe in Yang Ping.

“Perhaps Your Highness might be wondering at this moment whether the Yin family’s success is due to their acquaintance with me?”

Ji Yuan knew that it was inevitable for a normal person to think this way.

“It is undeniable that there is some connection, but it is definitely not what you think. A devout scholarly heart and a righteous character are witnessed by Heaven and Earth. Even if I were to help the Yin family with all my might, I could not conjure up a Yin Zhaoxian who would be respected by the people and admired by the gods out of thin air.”1

As he spoke, a smile appeared on Ji Yuan’s face. He returned to the table, placed his left hand on the red invitation, gently opened it, and looked up at Yin Qing and Yang Ping.

“Your Highness, please don’t be nervous. Now that you are marrying Yin Qing, you will be my junior. I wish you a long and happy marriage!”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan stretched out his right hand, and a stream of light flew out from his sleeve, transforming into a wolf-hair brush at his fingertips. The brush tip was wet with ink amidst the shimmering light.

As the ink from the brush touched the red paper, a faint fragrance of ink wafted out. One by one, characters full of spirit appeared on the red paper as Ji Yuan wrote: “May you have a long and happy marriage; may your hearts be one forever.”

As the last word was written, a flash of light appeared on the invitation.

Ji Yuan handed the invitation back to Yin Qing.

“Keep this safe. If you ever take another wife, I won’t give you another one.”

“He wouldn’t dare!”

Princess Changping pinched Yin Qing hard, then stood up and gave Ji Yuan a deep bow.

“Since you are an elder, you can just call me Pingping or Ping’er.”

“Alright, I’ll call you that when it’s just our own family from now on.”

Yin Qing slapped his thigh and quickly pulled up the small jar of wine from under the table.

“Mr. Ji, these two jars are 20-year-old Qianrichun wine. It took a lot of effort to buy them from Yuanzipu. I know you like to drink it, so I brought them here especially to show my respect to you!”

Ji Yuan chuckled.

“The owner of this garden shop is shrewd. Twenty years ago, a 20-year-old Qianrichun was indeed rare. Now, who knows how many they’ve hoarded? Next time, look for one that’s over 30 years old. Well, your salary is enough to buy it anyway.”

Yin Qing exclaimed exaggeratedly.

“You can’t treat me like this, sir. I have to count every penny of my salary!”

…

Originally, Yin Qing and Princess Changping’s entourage were supposed to dine at a restaurant in the county, but they ended up having dinner at Ju’an Pavilion that night, with Ji Yuan personally cooking the dishes.

By the time they left, it was already dark. Although someone occasionally came by to maintain the Yin family’s old house, it was clearly not suitable for staying in, so they went to an inn in the city instead.

After leaving Tian Niu Lane, surrounded by the fragrance of jujube blossoms, Yang Ping leaned close to Yin Qing and quietly asked him:

“Dear fiancé, is Master Ji truly an immortal? Can he summon wind and rain, and grant immortality?”

“I don’t know, and I’ve never seen him perform such techniques. I only know him as an elder who always makes me feel at ease. Does it really matter if he can summon wind and rain or make people live forever? When I was a child, Mr. Ji said that immortality is never something you can ask for.”

After much hesitation, Yang Ping finally asked the question that had been lingering in her mind.

“Then, may I tell Father about this?”

Hearing this, Yin Qing’s expression did not change, as Yang Ping had worried it might; instead, he looked at her with a smile.

“Setting aside whether or not it would harm the country; if there really were an immortal who could help Dazhen, and the emperor was wise enough, the benefits would definitely outweigh the drawbacks. But you’ve asked the wrong person. You should ask Mr. Ji. What do you think his opinion is?”

Yang Ping immediately understood and all her worries disappeared.

After drinking sweet tea at Ju’an Pavilion, the fatigue from the journey vanished. Starting the next day, Yin Qing and his family began sending invitations to their paternal and maternal relatives, assuring them that they would not need to worry about the trip, as they would prepare carriages and boats.

On the fourth day, Yin Qing, having received a special message, took Princess Changping to Niu Kui Mountain. Ostensibly, it was for sightseeing, but in reality, they went to visit their good friend Hu Yun. This time, with Yin Qing’s prompting, Hu Yun revealed his true form to Yang Ping, letting her know that the so-called “red cat” was actually a fox spirit.

Yang Ping was even more excited to see the “fox spirit” than she had been in Ju’an Pavilion. Hu Yun had no reservations in front of Yin Qing. He used supernatural techniques to send the guards away, and showed off his monstrous power in front of Yang Ping. He even showed off his paper figure talisman like a treasure. He called the person he created from the paper doll talisman a “Shadow Spirit.”

That day, the guards responsible for protecting the princess and her fiancé were terrified. The group of highly skilled guards had actually lost track of the princess and her fiancé In a mountain that was said to have once been home to man-eating tigers. This was a grave offense.

Fortunately, Yin Qing and Yang Ping emerged from the mountain unharmed half a day later, putting the profusely sweating guards and servants finally at ease. Knowing that the couple would not punish them and would even cover up the incident, they felt even more relieved.

However, because of this incident, even after leaving Ning’an County and returning to the capital, the guards never took their eyes off of their two masters for a moment.

Once the year was up, the marriage of Yin Qing and Yang Ping became famous throughout the capital. On their wedding day, both the Yin residence and the palace were extremely lively.

In this festive atmosphere, Hu Yun accompanied Ji Yuan to the Yin family to offer his blessings. This time, however, he did not stay long, and returned to Ning’an County just three days later.

…

Time flew, but in Ning’an County, Ji Yuan didn’t feel as though time were rushing by, especially since he hadn’t chosen to sleep for extended periods of time.

This time, Ji Yuan stayed in Ning’an County for nearly three years, the longest he had ever stayed since coming to this world.

Nothing major happened during those years, and not many people came to bother Ji Yuan. The only exception was that the Wei family resumed their habit of sending tea. Every year when the new tea was harvested, they would select the best leaves and send them to Ju’an Pavilion.

In the blink of an eye, it was the second month of the year of Wuxu.2 On this day, Sun Yaya, who had grown a little taller, was still practicing calligraphy in the courtyard. Despite the freezing weather, the little girl’s hands and feet were warm, and her thoughts seemed to be clearer in the courtyard.

Ji Yuan was in his room, playing with several spiritual coins. These were the result of several sessions of careful refining. Their luster as more subtle and their Tao essence was more profound. They looked like larger copper coins, with a heavier feel.

“Knock knock knock…”

There was a knock on the gate. Sun Yaya quickly put her brush in the brush holder and ran to open the door.

With a creak, the door opened. Sun Yaya found an old man with a white beard standing outside, looking kind and gentle.

“You must be Sun Yaya?”

Sun Yaya frowned.

“Grandpa, who are you? I’ve never seen you before, why do you know me?”

“Hahaha, it’s possible to know someone even if you’ve never met them before…”

Ju Yuanzi looked at Ji Yuan, who had already walked out of the house, and cupped his hands in a gesture of respect.

“Mr. Ji, the time for departure has arrived!”

35th year of the 60 year cycle, Earth Dog

Chapter 458: Departing, Return Date Uncertain

Ji Yuan bowed to Ju Yuanzi in return, then gestured towards the courtyard.

“Fellow Taoist Ju Yuanzi, please come in.”

“Please excuse my rudeness, I would be honored!”

Ju Yuanzi replied politely. Sun Yaya stepped aside and he entered, the little girl closing the door behind him.

Upon arriving at the stone table in the courtyard, he naturally noticed the writing brush, ink, paper, and inkstone on the table, as well as Sun Yaya’s handwriting. Seeing this handwriting, Ju Yuanzi’s eyes lit up.

“This girl’s handwriting is excellent!”

He looked back at Sun Yaya, who had just closed the door. Ju Yuanzi asked Ji Yuan, who had already approached, in a low voice.

“Is Mr. Ji interested in taking her as his disciple?”

Ji Yuan shook his head.

“At present, I am only teaching her calligraphy. People always say that the path to immortality is coveted, but even that path is not perfect. Marriage, children, and being a good wife or mother are still the main expectations the Sun family has for her.”

Ju Yuanzi chuckled.

“If you find it inconvenient to take on a disciple, this girl can also join my Yuhuai Mountain Sect. I, Ju Yuanzi, am willing to make an exception and take on another disciple!”

Ji Yuan looked at Ju Yuanzi with his pale eyes and blurted out a single sentence.

“You wish!”

“Hahahaha……”

Ju Yuanzi burst into laughter, which puzzled Sun Yaya, who had just returned. She looked at Ji Yuan and asked him.

“Sir, what is this old man laughing at? Is he your elder?”

“Oh dear… I dare not accept that title!”

Ju Yuanzi’s smile immediately vanished. He could joke with Ji Yuan, but he dared not casually acknowledge any seniority.

Ji Yuan looked at the writing on the table. Sun Yaya’s handwriting amazed Ju Yuanzi; it was naturally excellent in the eyes of ordinary people, and could even be compared with the works of some famous calligraphers. However, in Ji Yuan’s opinion, her handwriting had hardly improved since last year.

There are many factors involved, including talent, the praise and flattery that come with fame, the resulting inevitable smugness and restlessness, other distractions, even limitations in stationery.

Ji Yuan liked the child very much, but just like her calligraphy, whether it was because her ambitions had not yet solidified or because her family had high expectations, it was not the right time to take her as a disciple. Besides, leading someone onto the path of immortality might not necessarily be a good thing for them.

Everyone said that immortals were the greatest, but in Ji Yuan’s opinion, wasn’t it also good to live a peaceful and happy life?

Moreover, to be honest, Ji Yuan had always had very strict, demanding requirements when it came to accepting disciples.1 Even someone as talented as Lu Shanjun had only been accepted privately so far. Of course, he had his reasons, but it also revealed something about him.

Seeing Mr. Ji looking at her handwriting, Sun Yaya subconsciously scratched her head. She knew that she hadn’t made much progress in a long time, but Mr. Ji always encouraged her to calm down and persevere.

“Teacher, is there something important you need to discuss? Should I go back first?”

Sun Yaya asked obediently. Ju’an Pavilion had always been quiet, but in the past few years, when some special people visited, Mr. Ji always told her to go home first. She supposed it would be the same today.

Sure enough, after asking, Sun Yaya saw Ji Yuan nod slightly. Then, she skillfully began to tidy up the things on the stone table. Unlike when she first came to Ju’an Pavilion to study, now Sun Yaya only needed to put away the papers she had written on, dispose of the ink, and take away some things that she felt she would need at home or the school. She already had several sets of other items such as inkstones, brush holders, and paperweights, so she didn’t need to take them all back.

Just as Sun Yaya finished dealing with the ink and was about to take some stationery back to the pavilion’s main room, Ji Yuan spoke up again.

“Yaya, pack everything up this time, and put it in your book pack to take home.”

“Hun?”

Sun Yaya was taken aback. She looked at Ji Yuan for a while, lowered her head and said “Oh”, before she slowly began putting everything into the small book pack under the table.

After putting in the last paperweight, Sun Yaya pursed her lips, looked up at Mr. Ji, and asked very carefully.

“Sir, can I still come tomorrow?”

Ji Yuan patted the little girl’s head.

“You don’t need to come tomorrow. Your teacher isn’t trying to get rid of you, but I am going on a long trip and it’s unclear when I will be back.”

Sun Yaya’s eyes, which had been slightly red, immediately softened down considerably.

“Then, after Teacher returns, can I still come to Ju’an Pavilion to practice calligraphy?”

Ji Yuan smiled.

“As long as you still want to practice calligraphy, of course you can. Okay, pack up and go home. Don’t come over tomorrow, I won’t be here.”

Sun Yaya nodded vigorously.

“When Teacher returns, I will definitely come to practice calligraphy again!”

“Haha, that’s kind of you. Be careful on your way back.”

Sun Yaya smiled.

“Teacher, I’ll just go as far as Tian Niu Lane. I still have to wait for Grandpa to close up stall before we go back together.”

With that, the little girl picked up her book pack and walked towards the courtyard gate. After opening the gate, she looked back at Ju Yuanzi and Ji Yuan.

“Teacher, I’ll be off then.”

“Go ahead, go ahead!”

Ji Yuan waved at her with his hand, and watched the little girl disappear out the door. Then he pulled the door lock from the outside to close the gate.

Ju Yuanzi remained silent throughout, only turning to Ji Yuan after the little girl had left.

“Mr. Ji, is this alright?”

Ji Yuan thought for a moment and then answered.

“Compared to other situations, it’s better to leave it at that for now.”

Ju Yuanzi nodded in agreement. Cultivators were extremely cautious when accepting disciples, especially those with profound cultivation. It was no exaggeration to say that potential disciples underwent rigorous trials, and many couldn’t even pass the first hurdle.

…

Sun Yaya walked through the alleys of Tian Niu Lane, carrying her little schoolbag. The neighbors along the way would greet the little girl, enjoying her sweet replies.

However, the little girl was clearly distracted today. When she passed the public wells, she didn’t stop to chat with the older women, but walked past with a preoccupied expression.

Soon, Sun Yaya exited Tian Niu Lane. Sun Fu, seeing his granddaughter coming from afar, quickly called out to her.

“Yaya!”

The little girl hurried toward the noodle stall, and naturally took off her book pack and placed it behind the cart. She then sat on a small stool, resting her chin on her hands, lost in thought.

Sun Fu was busy as usual, but he also turned to look at his granddaughter and casually asked her a question.

“Yaya, why did you leave Mr. Ji’s house so early today?”

Sun Yaya usually arrived at the perfect time, always before his usual closing time, but today there was still at least half an hour to go.

Sun Yaya replied listlessly.

“An old man with a white beard came to Ju’an Pavilion. The master told me to go home first, and said that he was going on a long trip and that I didn’t need to go tomorrow.”

Sun Fu had been nodding and listening, but then he suddenly paused, put down his tools, and squatted down in front of Sun Ya Ya to look at her.

“Mr. Ji said he’s going on a long trip? How long will he be gone? Did he say he’d be back soon?”

Sun Yaya looked up at her grandfather and shook her head.

“Teacher said he didn’t know when he would be back, and he even told me to take all the writing implements from Ju’an Pavilion home…”

Sun Fu noticed that his granddaughter looked disappointed, but he couldn’t think about that right now; he was preoccupied with other things. It took him a long time to come back to his senses and look at Sun Yaya.

“Yaya… Mr. Ji may be gone for several years, or even longer. Did you say goodbye to him properly?”

Since Ji Yuan had Sun Yaya pack away the writing materials and that she didn’t need to return tomorrow or the day after, it meant he was leaving soon.

Sure enough, the little girl trembled immediately upon hearing her grandfather’s words.

“Oh, several years… I’ll go find Mr. Ji!”

Sun Yaya jumped up from the small stool as she spoke, and rushed towards Tian Niu Lane, not even bothering to greet the neighbors she met along the way.

“Hey, Yaya, what brings you back?”

“Hey Yaya, slow down! Be careful not to fall!”

“It’s an urgent matter!”

“This child…”

She rushed to the depths of Tian Niu Lane and arrived at the gate of Ju’an Pavilion. Despite the great exertion, Sun Yaya was only slightly out of breath. Her gaze focused on the gate of the pavilion and saw that an old copper lock was already hanging on it.

“Mr. Ji… Mr. Ji…”

Sun Yaya called out to Ju’an Pavilion a few times, but received no response; the entire Ju’an Pavilion was quiet.

At that moment, tears welled up in the little girl’s eyes, but she held them back and shouted with all her might through the door into the small room.

“Teacher-! Even while you’re not here, I will practice my calligraphy diligently. When you come back, you’ll definitely praise my improved handwriting. You definitely will!”

Whoosh… Whoosh…

The wind around Ju’an Pavilion suddenly picked up, and the jujube tree in the courtyard swayed in the wind. Leaves drifted down one by one, something fell, carrying a fiery red glow, landing in front of the dejected Sun Yaya. The little girl subconsciously reached out and grabbed a few jujube leaves and the object with the fiery red glow.

“Huh…”

Sun Yaya held a large fruit in her hands, feeling its warmth. Upon closer inspection, she could see fiery radiance swirling around its surface, the light flickering.

Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi were walking together on the official road outside Ning’an County. As usual, Ji Yuan locked the inside and outside of the courtyard, took his bag, and left. He allowed some of the residents of the neighborhood to see him, but he did not show up at the exit of the city.

At this moment, Ji Yuan suddenly stopped and turned to look in the direction of Tian Niu Lane in Ning’an County.

“Mr. Ji, what’s wrong?”

Ji Yuan’s eyes flashed as he glanced at his sleeve. Just now, a phantom chess piece had appeared in his hand; it was Sun Yaya’s.

“It’s nothing. Let’s go, we don’t want to keep Mr. Qiu and the others waiting.”

As they spoke, clouds and mist rose beneath Ji Yuan’s feet. Ju Yuanzi flicked his sleeves and lightly ascended. The two of them soared through the clouds in the setting sun, leaving Ning’an County and heading to distant lands.

Chapter 459: The Peak Ferry Crossing of Yuelu Mountain

Ju Yuanzi had come to inform Ji Yuan. He had originally thought that if Ji Yuan still had some matters to attend to, he could ask Ji Yuan and stay at Ju’an Pavilion for a couple days. He believed that Ji Yuan would not have any objections. However, it seemed that Ji Yuan had anticipated this, and Ju Yuanzi set off immediately after arriving.

“Mr. Ji, several of my fellow Taoists and juniors from Yuhuai Mountain have already set off ahead of time and will meet us at Yuelu Mountain. There is an anchor point for the realm crossing ferry in the mountain range, called the Peak Ferry Crossing. Fellow Taoist Qiu and the others have already made preparations and selected the means of transportation. Once we arrive, we will take the realm-crossing ferry together at the Peak Ferry Crossing.”

Before leaving, Ju Yuanzi had explained their planned route from Yuhuai Mountain, and of course had asked Ji Yuan if he had any opinions. Ji Yuan, of course, left it to them to decide; what kind of plans could he possibly have?

Now, upon hearing Ju Yuanzi explain the details, Ji Yuan showed an expression of interest.

“Where is Yuelu Mountain? Which immortal sect owns the Peak Ferry Crossing? Is the ferry a flying boat or something else?”

Ju Yuanzi stroked his beard and replied.

“Yuelu Mountain is a little-known mountain on the border between the countries of Yunzhou and Zenan. There is a Taoist sect atop it called the Yuelu1 Sect. They are in charge of the Peak Ferry Crossing. However, the Yuelu Sect does not have its own realm-crossing ferry. Instead, other realm-crossing ferries will stop at the Peak Ferry Crossing from time to time, so I don’t know what we will be riding. Perhaps it will be a flying boat, perhaps a floating island, or perhaps some kind of enormous beast.”

“Oh… the Yuelu Sect doesn’t have its own ferry?”

Upon hearing Ji Yuan’s words, Ju Yuanzi dared not assume that he was ignorant, but carefully replied.

“Sir, you may not know this, but a realm-crossing ferry contains a dimension within itself. Its arrays and artifacts are all intricately interconnected. Putting aside its enormous size, it is an extraordinary magical treasure in itself. It can not only gather spiritual resources for the immortal sects, but also forge many good karmic relationships. In times of crisis, the realm-crossing ferry is a powerful tool… It is already excellent that the Yuelu Sect can manage the Peak Ferry Crossing by virtue of its geographical advantage. As for the realm-crossing ferry, they do not yet have that capability.”

Ju Yuanzi sighed. He remarked quietly to himself that even Yuhuai Mountain would find it quite difficult to create a realm-crossing ferry treasure, let alone the Yuelu Sect.

Yuhuai Mountain was not short of money, but it was not a sect or an immortal abode; it was a sacred land for shared cultivation. Aside from a few lineages, everyone was laid back and lacked cohesions. Sometimes that made it difficult to concentrate their efforts on major tasks, let alone refining an extremely energy-consuming and resource-intensive Relam-Crossing Ferry together.

“I see.”

Ji Yuan didn’t ask any more questions. Ju Yuanzi took charge of their flight, heading towards the northwest.

In the center of Yunzhou lay a vast swamp. Although it was nominally a large swamp, it was actually composed of many lakes, rivers, and marshes of varying sizes. It was inhabited by many highly intelligent animals, as well as some tribal human settlements. The country of Zenan was located south of this swamp; its name was simple and straightforward.2

From when they started their journey from Ning’an County, without deliberately rushing, the two riding the clouds finally approached Yuelu Mountain several days later. However, although the country of Zenan was nominally adjacent to the Central Great Swamp, it was still an entire separate country, so it was impossible for Ji Yuan to see the Great Swamp from where they were located.

Ju Yuanzi waved his sleeve, parting the clouds in the surrounding sky, and pointed to the mountain below.

“Mr. Ji, that is Yuelu Mountain. The summit is protected by an array; although it is possible to enter and exit, it cannot be seen from here.”

Ji Yuan looked out at Yuelu Mountain. The mountain was not particularly steep, but possessed a rather beautiful expanse of lush, verdant greenery and a landscape shrouded by deep mist. His gaze swept across it: he felt that the mountain was smaller than Niu Kui Mountain, but it was still a rather large mountain.

While Ji Yuan was observing Yuelu Mountain, Ju Yuanzi was already descending on the clouds. About a quarter of an hour later, the two of them stepped onto the ground at the foot of Yuelu Mountain.

“Sir, when on the grounds of any immortal abode or sect, unless there is an emergency, it is best to travel on foot, as a sign of respect for fellow immortals.”

“Haha, that’s natural. Besides, I personally prefer hiking in the mountains.”

“Then I shall consider myself to be accompanying Mr. Ji on your mountain tour. Shall we?”

Ju Yuanzi smiled and solemnly extended his hand to invite him onwards. Ji Yuan also gestured politely, and the two of them leisurely headed towards the mountains. They seemed to be strolling at a leisurely pace, but in reality, they were quite fast.

As it was currently morning, just after sunrise, the mist on Yuelu Mountain gradually dissipated. There were herb gatherers walking around, woodcutters chopping wood, and hunters carrying bows and spears in groups. It was a scene of harmony between humans and nature. It was impossible to tell that there was an immortal sect in the mountains.

Ji Yuan, in a blue robe, long hair tied up with a jade hairpin, and Ju Yuanzi, wearing a white robe with a long beard and an air of otherworldly elegance, admired the scenery of man and nature along the way. Some mountain travelers and even wild animals observed them in return.

Two woodcutters carrying firewood came down a small side path and happened to run into Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi walking deeper into the mountains. One of the woodcutters called out to them.

“Hey… gentlemen… the mountain path ahead is difficult to walk. If you want to enjoy hiking the mountain, this way is better.”

As he spoke, the woodcutter pointed with his axe to the road on his left, where the path was more open.

“Thank you for your kindness, but a little rough terrain won’t be a problem for us.”

Ji Yuan replied with a smiled, but the woodcutter shook his head.

“Sir, you are relatively young, so you should be fine. I’m talking about the old gentleman next to you. He’s quite old. What if something goes wrong if he goes deeper into the mountains? Besides, although Yuelu Mountain seems peaceful, there are still wild beasts in the mountains. There are definitely wolves, without even mentioning any other possible animals. If something really happens, do you think a god will come to save you or something?”

Ju Yuanzi laughed heartily and cupped his hands in respect to the woodcutter.

“Thank you for your concern. I am an old man blessed with good fortune, and always manage to turn bad luck into good. If I ever encounter trouble, I am sure that a true deity will come to my aid.”

The other woodcutter, like his companion, was both amused and exasperated. He pointed at Ju Yuanzi.

“Oh, you old man… Sir, aren’t you going to try to persuade him?”

The second half of the sentence was addressed to Ji Yuan, but Ji Yuan could only smile helplessly.

“There’s no way I can persuade him. You two should go down the mountain.”

The two woodcutters shook their heads. After all, it was someone else’s business, so they let it be. They then descended the mountain, following the path Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi had come from.

However, as they left, they would occasionally look back at the two figures walking further and further away.

“Hey, didn’t Uncle Chen and the others always say that there really are immortals in Yuelu Mountain?”

One of the woodcutters suddenly remarked. The other laughed.

“What? You’re not suggesting these two might be immortals, are you?”

“Of course not. I mean, could these two come seeking immortality? After all, this isn’t the first or second time this has happened.”

“Why do you care so much?”

The two walked and talked, carrying firewood on their backs. Before long, when they looked back, but Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi were no longer anywhere to be seen.

Meanwhile, as Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi ventured deeper into Yuelu Mountain, they encountered fewer and fewer ordinary mountain travelers, and occasionally sensed the presence of some spiritual animals watching them.

About half an hour after entering the mountains, at Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi’s pace, they had actually covered a distance that would normally take an experienced mountaineer a whole day to traverse.

As Ji Yuan could vaguely sense the spiritual energy of the array, he seemed to have a premonition. He looked up and stared at a certain spot to his right. With his eyes fully open, he could vaguely discern the outline of a mountain peak.

Rather, it was actually two mountain peaks colliding together, resembling an enormous “入” character,3 with the lower peak supporting the upper part of the upper peak, while the upper peak jutted out into the clouds.

“No wonder it’s called the Peak Crossing.”

Ju Yuanzi naturally noticed Ji Yuan’s actions. Upon hearing his words, he understood that Ji Yuan had definitely seen the true Peak Crossing. Although he could sense the changes in the formation and spiritual energy and even discern the direction of the energy flow, he could not see anything at this distance with just his eyesight alone.

‘As expected of Mr. Ji!’

Ju Yuanzi had always admired Ji Yuan, and just as he was about to suggest that they walk faster, a voice suddenly came from a distance to the side.

“Wait a minute… you two over there, please wait a minute…!”

The shout echoed slightly in the mountains. Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi looked in the direction of the sound and saw several people hurrying along a ridge. One of them was waving as he walked, heading directly towards Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi, in the same direction as they were heading.

“Please wait a moment, you two…”

As if afraid that Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi would simply leave, the people in the other party would occasionally shout. Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi could hear the anxiety in their voices.

“What should we do, sir?”

Ju Yuanzi asked, and Ji Yuan readily replied.

“Let’s wait and see. Perhaps they’ve encountered some difficulties.”

Seeing the two people standing still on the mountain path in the distance, unmoving, the group who were hurrying down the ridge and across the ravine felt much more at ease, but they did not slow down their pace.

As the saying went, “if you run your horse while gazing towards the far mountains, you will run your horse to death.”4 Even though the distance seemed short, it was actually quite far. In addition, the mountain road was rugged and had obstacles such as bushes and weeds. It took half an hour for the group to reach Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi.

There were six people in total: a middle-aged man led five young men and women, about sixteen or seventeen years old, all with youthful faces. They inevitably had some traces left from hiking the mountains on their bodies, such as mud or grass clippings, and some of them had been scratched by thorny branches.

When the middle-aged man saw Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi, two refined and dignified men, and especially an older man who walked through such a difficult mountain path yet remained spotless, he felt immediately reassured.

“You lot, don’t pant so much, come with me to pay your respects to the immortals.”

As he spoke, the middle-aged man took the lead in bowing deeply with his hands clasped in a respectful gesture.

“Greetings to the two immortal masters!”

The younger members of the group quickly followed suit and bowed deeply.

“Greetings to the two immortal masters!”

Ju Yuanzi and Ji Yuan looked at them but did not refute their words. The former stroked his beard and asked:

“Why do you call us immortals?”

“Oh haha, Immortal Master, you jest. Look at us mortals. I still have some martial arts skills, yet I’ve been reduced to this state…”

The man showed off his somewhat dirty clothes and then continued.

“Only an immortal could enter the mountain so leisurely without being touched by worldly grime. To be honest, we have been wandering around Yuelu Mountain for three days now. We humbly request that you lead us to the Peak Crossing! We beg you!”

The middle-aged man then performed a deep bow.

Just because the realm-crossing ferry was an immortal artifact didn’t necessarily mean that mortals could not ride it. In fact, some mortals could even take the ferry for free as long as they could give a plausible reason. But finding the immortal ferry was no easy feat.

Ji Yuan looked at them.

“Since you know about the Peak Ferry Crossing, we can take you there. Follow us.”

月 (yuè): moon, 鹿 (lù): deer

泽 (zé): pool/pond/damp, 南 (nán): south –> lit. “South of Swamp,” you can tell the author was getting real creative with country names LOL

入 (rù): to enter/to join. Thematically very appropriate for the anchor point of a realm-crossing ferry

Proverb about the futility of chasing or pushing too hard towards an unreachable goal. Edit: thank you Despyte for the improved translation and info

Chapter 460: The Lower Peak Market, the Sky-Devouring Beast

Seeing that Ji Yuan was so easy to talk to, the middle-aged man was so excited that he bowed continuously.

“Thank you, Immortal Master! Thank you, Immortal Master!”

The young adults standing nearby also joined the middle-aged man in expressing their gratitude.

“Alright, keep up, we’re not far from the Peak Crossing.”

With that, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi set off ahead towards the summit, while the group of people quickly followed behind them.

Those behind dared not get too close, but if they looked closely, they could see that the two in front of them were trekking along as if taking a leisurely stroll, their hands barely touching the ground, unlike the group behind them, who often had to use both hands and feet to clamber up.

Furthermore, weeds and vines acted like drifting catkins as Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi passed by, slipping past their clothes without a care. Of course, the people behind them were also unaffected by weeds, withered branches, thorns, and vines, but those things had previously caused them great suffering during their journey.

“Where did you learn about the location of the realm-crossing ferry’s anchor point, and why are you headed there?”

Ji Yuan turned around, looked at the six people who had been following closely behind him, and casually asked. Seeing that the middle-aged man was about to bow again upon hearing his question, he quickly added:

“When crossing mountains, you need to use both hands and feet, so don’t bother with bowing. If you fall, I won’t save you.”

“Yes, yes!”

The middle-aged man wiped his sweat with his sleeve and answered as he climbed the mountain.

“In reply to the Immortal Master, our Yan and Zong families were originally from the country of Langming in the Heng region.1 According to the elders, our ancestors suffered a great calamity and fled for several months. When they got lost in the deep mountains, they stumbled upon an immortal land, which turned out to be a realm-crossing ferry anchor point. Later, they found some work at the ferry crossing, and on the goods ferried across by the immortals, allowing them to survive. Some people disembarked from the realm-crossing ferry and found a peaceful place to live. We are the descendants of one of these branches…”

The man answered respectfully, slowly recounting his family background.

Ju Yuanzi stroked his beard and spoke.

“Then why go to the Peak Ferry Crossing now?”

“In reply to the Immortal Master, we are living quite well in the country of Zenan, but not long ago we received news at night that someone from our ancestors was able to enter the gates of an immortal sect a hundred years ago. Now they want to select outstanding talents from among their descendants to lead them onto the path of immortality. This is tantamount to a shortcut to heaven. The dozen or so descendants of the Yan and Zong families in the country of Zenan were excited when they heard. In the end, they selected suitable people, and I have brought these descendants to find the ferry crossing. However, even with the map from our ancestors, the ferry is still hard to find. If we hadn’t met the two immortal masters, we mortals would have had to wander around for who knows how long.

“Actually, our ancestors once left behind a token, which was bestowed onto him by the immortal of Yuelu Mountain many generations ago. Unfortunately, it has long been lost. It is said that some good-for-nothing ancestor who had accumulated a huge gambling debt stole the token from the ancestral hall and sold it.

“Since then, neither family has returned to Yuelu Mountain; or rather, nobody from either family has been able to successfully enter the Immortal Realm. This time, they only came to try their luck after receiving a message from our ancestors. Sure enough, our ancestors blessed us and their fortunes turned around.”

‘When one attains enlightenment, even his chickens and dogs ascend to heaven,’2 was Ji Yuan’s conclusion after hearing the middle-aged man’s words. Although the proverb differed from the actual reality of the situation, the ancestor’s fortune still generally benefited his descendants.

To accommodate for those behind them, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi deliberately slowed their pace. The group chatted as they walked, and after about two hours, the mountain path under their feet gradually widened, and the annoying thorns and vines almost disappeared.

The surrounding scenery became increasingly beautiful, and the air the middle-aged man and his companions breathed felt fresher. A soft, burbling sound came from up ahead, and a mountain stream, about the width of one person, appeared before them.

“Water!”

A boy behind the middle-aged man instinctively called out.

Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi deliberately slowed down and stopped by the stream, and the others behind them rushed to the bank.

“Drink water! Drink water!”

“This water is so clear!”

“It’s spring water, it’s fine, you can drink it!”

“Glug, glug, glug…”

The group didn’t care about anything else; they knelt by the stream, cupped their hands, and drank, or simply lowered their heads to drink.

“Wow, this water is so sweet!”

“Don’t just drink, fill your water casks!”

“Yes, yes, yes!”

The group drank their fill of water, then took off their bamboo tubes and began filling them with water from the stream.

“We’ve arrived.”

While the group was busy filling their bottles with water, they heard Ji Yuan’s soft voice and instinctively looked up. The hazy mist in the distance seemed to gradually dissipate, and a magnificent mountain peak that had previously been entirely invisible appeared in the distance.

A low peak supported a higher peak that slanted into the clouds, a shape of mountain unlike any other they had seen before, as if the high peak had fallen down from the sky and was now propped up by the lower lower peak.

There had been nothing there before reached the stream.

“Have you had enough to drink? If so, let’s set off.”

“Yes, yes, yes, by your will, Immortal Master. Let’s go, let’s go!”

The middle-aged man became even more excited. It was his first time seeing an immortal ferry crossing. If he was this excited, then the teenagers behind him were even more so. Although the two people they had been following him were also immortals, they were hiking just like them, walking along the same path, and there was nothing particularly supernatural about them.

After crossing the stream, the road became much easier to walk on, and they even encountered other people from time to time. Some were cultivators and others were ordinary mortals.

Upon arriving, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi parted ways with the middle-aged man and the five teenagers. They had already brought them here, and all that was left was for them to slowly climb the mountain. The path up to the summit was not steep, and there was a wide mountain path with some pavilions and shops on both sides along the way. The six of them could easily make their way up on their own.

About fifteen minutes after parting ways with the six people, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi had reached the top of the peak. The real ferry crossing was on the outer mountain peak, while on the inner mountain peak, a rather large complex of buildings had formed.

The market consisted of dozens of large buildings and many more smaller ones, including restaurants and inns, some run by mortals and others by cultivators. Some buildings are unremarkable, while others shone dazzlingly.

There were quite a few people in the crowd, and it looked very lively. Ji Yuan even saw a few creatures that looked like clearly supernatural monsters wandering around. Some mortals would instinctively avoid them.

Standing outside the market, Ji Yuan looked at everything in astonishment. Although he had thought he might see a similar scene after disembarking the flying boat at Nine Peaks Mountain, he had not expected it to be so bustling, practically like a real market.

“Hehehe, I didn’t expect that after so many years, the Peak Ferry Crossing would be so lively!”

Ju Yuanzi chuckled and said to Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, let’s go for a stroll too. In the cultivation world, such scenes can only be found at various ferry crossings.”

As he spoke, Ju Yuanzi unleashed a thin stream of light, and then he and Ji Yuan stepped together out into this “bustling market” belonging to the world of cultivation.

Even ordinary shops could stand out here. Excellent service was a must. No short-tempered mortal would dare lose their temper here. The food and products were made to the highest standard of quality.

Things that are considered treasures in the secular world, such as calligraphy and paintings by masters and objects carefully crafted by skilled craftsmen, also found a market here. After all, these were all things that embodied the spirit of their creators, and many of them possessed their own unique charm. Even cultivators sometimes found them interesting, like Ji Yuan had.

Therefore, there are also some discerning dealers who specialized in searching for things in the secular world that immortals might be interested in, and then sending them here.

If a mortal could obtain some wondrous item from an immortal using such goods, then they would truly strike it rich.

However, cultivators were not truly detached from worldly affairs. They also possessed gold and silver. Unless it was in truly exceptional circumstances, they would usually pay for such items with gold and silver, and would not casually give away items from the immortal path.

Besides gold and silver, which could circulate among mortals, immortals could barter. There were also hard currencies such as the Five-Wen Coin, essences of the five elements, and elixirs, which were all of such basic value that no one would refuse.

Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi walked through the bustling market. Those around them with a bit of sense would try to keep their distance.

One was dressed in a simple long robe and exuded an extraordinary air, while the other, with a white beard, white hair, and a youthful face, clearly belonged to another world at first glance. Yet, on a sensing level, they appeared to be just ordinary people, particularly coupled with their composed demeanor and a sense of wonder about their surroundings.

Without a doubt, they were almost certainly the kind of highly skilled immortal elders who rarely ventured outside. Such people possessed unfathomable cultivation and had all-seeing eyes that could see through the world. If they were of sound mind, everyone would respect them.

“Master Ju, Mr. Ji!”

Wei Yuansheng’s voice rang out, followed by Qiu Feng, Yang Ming, and the others from Yuhuai Mountain, who appeared in Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi’s sight. Wei Yuansheng even ran over, rushing to greet to the two of them.

“Mr. Ji, I didn’t know the world of cultivation could be so lively! I thought it was always like Yuhuai Mountain!”

“Haha, I was quite surprised too!”

As they spoke, Qiu Feng and the others approached and bowed to Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi. This time, Yuhuai Mountain had sent more than a dozen people, from the True Immortals to the disciples. Ji Yuan knew some of them, and others he had never seen before, but they all recognized Ji Yuan.

“Master Ju, Mr. Ji, since you have arrived so early, let’s take Xuanxin Mansion’s realm-crossing ferry. If they are not delayed along the way, we will arrive at the Peak Ferry Crossing in two days, and then it will take about two and a half months to reach the Heng Region in the Northern Territories.

“What do you think, Mr. Ji?”

Ju Yuanzi asked Ji Yuan. The latter nodded.

“Haha, you guys decide. I’m just a country bumpkin, I’ve never seen anything like this.”

Such words were best left unsaid. Even though Ji Yuan was easygoing, no one in Yuhuai Mountain dared to make fun of him.

Seeing Ji Yuan nod, Ju Yuanzi finally spoke and concluded.

“Since Mr. Ji has no objections, let’s take the Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship.”

At this moment, Ji Yuan couldn’t help but think of Old Dragon Ying Hong. If the Old Dragon were here, he would have been ridiculed as soon as he finished speaking. Sometimes, the people of Yuhuai Mountain were just too rigid.

Whoosh… Whoosh…

At that moment, the clouds and mist swirling around the mountain peaks in the sky were suddenly stirred up, and gusts of air formed a gale amidst the howling sound. However, by the time they reached the market, they had become a gentle breeze. Nevertheless, many people, including Ji Yuan, still subconsciously looked up at the sky.

A colossal creature was vaguely visible in the clouds.

“Awooooo…”

A long, distant cry rang out. It was the cry of an enormous monster with fin-like appendages on its sides and top. Judging by its size, it was practically a mountain, even larger than the towering peak of the ferry crossing.

“The Sky-Devouring Beast of the Weimei Sect is one of the most famous realm-crossing ferries!”

Ju Yuanzi watched the giant beast fly towards the outer peak ahead, and then whispered a brief explanation to Ji Yuan.

It could also be translated as “the Heng Continent,” or “this continent” but it is later mentioned that it’s part of the notoriously lawless Northern Territories. 恒 (héng) means permanent/constant/usual/ordinary

Basically saying that any idiot nobody can gain a high status and ascend to heaven by riding on the coattails of someone with enough achievements, in cultivation or otherwise (thank you Jaertin for the correction)

Chapter 461: A Mixed Bag

[T/n: note on chapter title]1

With such an enormous spiritual beast, or rather, celestial beast, even disregarding its other qualities, its sheer size alone gave off an unparalleled sense of oppression and destructive power. Moreover, it could fly, so besides its special innate abilities, it also must possess considerable spiritual energy and power.

Even with Ji Yuan’s current cultivation and composure, upon seeing the Sky-Devouring2 Beast, he couldn’t help but murmur.

“The Sky-Devouring Beast…it does look somewhat like a Kun3…”

Ji Yuan had never seen what a real Kun actually looked like, but with its appearance resembling some aquatic fish, its enormous size, and the name “Sky-Devouring Beast,” how could he not associate it with a Kun?

Speaking of which, in his past life, Ji Yuan had been bombarded with online advertisements for a “Raising Kun” game4 for a period of time. And to be honest, at certain angles and moments, the beast did look quite like some of the images on those websites.

Ji Yuan’s mutterings were overheard by Ju Yuanzi, who hesitated for a moment before asking him a question.

“Mr. Ji, what is a Kun? Is it also a giant monster?”

Ji Yuan nodded.

“I guess so. It’s a kind of big fish.”5

Many people around shouted excitedly. Most of them were mortals, but there were also quite a few cultivators. Even those who had seen the Sky-Devouring Beast before were still excited.

A short distance away from Ji Yuan and his companion, a group of people in front of a building with blue6 bricks and glazed tiles were so frightened by the Sky-Devouring Beast that they collapsed to the ground.

“Uncle, Uncle… what is that…?”

“I… I don’t know either!”

“So… so scary!”

“I was so scared I almost peed my pants!”

…

A young cultivator standing nearby was also shocked by the Sky-Devouring Beast as it passed by, but he was amused by their reaction.

“Get up, get up! Look at you all like this. This is a celestial beast, protected by immortals. It won’t hurt you.”

A young boy, still staring blankly at the cultivator, asked him a question.

“Wouldn’t this thing eat an entire city in one bite?”

“It can survive by absorbing spiritual energy. Even if it does eat, it only eats ferocious demons and monsters, not ordinary mortals.”

The young cultivator patiently explained. He was the one responsible for picking up these six people. He had been waiting for a while and was even thinking of going to the country of Zenan, but today he finally saw them arrive.

The six said that their ancestors had lost their token, and that they almost couldn’t make it in time, but that they had been brought in by a cultivator they met on the way.

People had been watching them from the side the whole time. Although their legs were still a little weak, everyone on the ground quickly stood up. After all, this was an immortal ferry, and they dared not act rashly.

“Ancestor, we…”

The middle-aged man was interrupted before he could finish speaking.

“Hey, how many times have I told you? I’m not your ancestor. I’m only a little older than you. I’m just here to pick you up on behalf of my uncle…”

The young cultivator was once again left at a loss for words. When this group of people first arrived, he went over to greet them, and they actually knelt down and kowtowed, shouting “Greetings, Ancestor!” which completely baffled him.

Upon hearing the young cultivator’s words, the middle-aged man realized he had made another slip of the tongue and quickly corrected himself.

“Yes, yes, Immortal Master, I wanted to ask…”

“Gurgle~”

Before the middle-aged man could even ask what he wanted to ask, his stomach seemed to have already spoken for him. Then the stomachs of the five teenagers next to him also rumbled.

“Gurgle~”

“Gurgle~”

…

“Uh, well, I just wanted to ask…”

“Hahahaha… Mortals are really interesting. So this is how a person’s stomach can rumble so loudly when they’re hungry. Hahahaha…”

Suddenly, exaggerated laughter came from the side. A boy who also looked like a teenager was holding a peach blossom branch in his hand, laughing heartily not too far away. The peach blossom branch trembled in his hand as he laughed.

The young cultivator next to the middle-aged man bowed and greeted him.

“Fellow Taoist, these are my junior uncle’s descendants. Please forgive them if they have been impolite!”

Then the young cultivator addressed the group of six.

“Hiking through the mountains must have taken a toll on your energy. You must be hungry. I’ll take you to get something to eat. Come with me!”

With that, the young cultivator took the lead and started moving. The other six naturally followed closely behind. They knew that in this kind of place, it was essential to stay close to the person in front of them. Who knew what might happen if they got lost?

The boy holding the peach blossom branch stopped laughing, and didn’t return the youg cultivator’s greeting. He just watched them leave, but his gaze was suddenly startled by something else, and he immediately turned his head to look in another direction.

In that instant, as if in a trance, the boy holding the peach branch seemed to see a vision: in the dim light, water rippled, then calmed, a bright moon appearing in the reflection. Then the vision dissipated, and his gaze fell upon a pair of pale, piercing eyes, looking right at him.

A group of cultivators were strolling towards the blue building with glazed tiles. The young man holding the peach blossom branch quickly averted his gaze and hurried away.

Ju Yuanzi’s gaze swept over the departing boy, then turned to the blue-brick building not far away.

“Mr. Ji, that place is where you check the ferries. Some ferries will send a message before they arrive, so people can know in advance. Others are fixed at a certain time of day for each of the four seasons. This place has changed a lot, but the building itself has stayed the same.”

“Okay, let’s go take a look!”

Having witnessed the Sky-Devouring Beast’s passage, Ji Yuan wasn’t in a hurry to go to the outer peak immortal anchor point to see the spectacle. With his divine eyes, he could easily see through the mist and make out the Sky-Devouring Beast hovering in the air. Instead, he was more interested in the other ferry vehicles. Here, he could identify them and make a “reservation.”

Before stepping into the blue-brick building, Ji Yuan’s gaze finally returned from where he had been staring into the distance. Even though he could no longer see the boy holding the peach blossom branch, it was as if his gaze were still following him.

As soon as Ji Yuan’s gaze shifted away from him, the boy, who who was winding his way through the distant streets, seemed to feel the pressure leave and breathed a sigh of relief.

‘My goodness, where did this old monster come from?’

On the other side, the young cultivator led the six people from the Yan and Zong families for a while until they arrived at a restaurant run by ordinary mortals. He looked back and glanced around, but didn’t see anything. Only then did he smile again and look at the people behind him.

“Come in and eat! I think the food and drinks here are delicious, and I’m sure they’ll suit your tastes too!”

The six looked up at the very upscale restaurant, hesitated for a moment, but still followed the young cultivator inside. After all, dining with an immortal shouldn’t mean they couldn’t pay the bill.

Perhaps they really were starving, because once the food was served, the six of them merely looked to the young cultivator for instructions and then began to wolf down their food at the eight-person table without any restraint, eating with gusto and exclaiming how satisfying it was.

“Judging by your hunger, the two fellow Taoists who brought you in must have secretly helped you, otherwise you probably wouldn’t have been able to make it to the summit.”

“Mmm, delicious…yes, yeah, slurp…well…”

“Eat your food in peace, swallow before you speak.”

“Alright… gulp, Immortal Master, you should eat too!”

“Okay, okay, I’ll eat too…”

The young cultivator politely extended his chopsticks, picked up a few pieces of vegetables and a piece of meat, put them in his mouth, then took a small sip of wine. He subconsciously looked back at the outside of the restaurant, a thoughtful expression on his face.

After a long while, when he looked back, some of the dishes on the table had magically disappeared.

“By the way, Immortal Master, was there anything wrong with that person just now? I saw you looking at him and then at us, and quickly led us away.”

After swallowing the chicken in her mouth and taking a sip of soup, a girl cautiously asked a question, which made the young cultivator glance at her again. He nodded and whispered.

“That’s true. This bustling Peak Crossing is actually a mixed bag. It’s safe most of the time, but you can’t be too careful. Although I couldn’t see through that young man just now, I knew he was definitely not human. I had a strange feeling the moment I met his gaze. Although it was fleeting, I’m not mistaken.”

Upon hearing that he wasn’t human, the six people slowed down their speech. Some even showed a hint of fear, naturally assuming something nefarious.

“Remember this: in places like these, those who wander around with the outward appearance of spirits or monsters are usually not going to do anything rash. On the contrary, those who have an exceedingly perfect human appearance might be hiding something terrible, and some of them might occasionally be mentally unstable…”

“Oh…”

“I see!”

“Alright, with me here, you don’t have to worry. Just follow me closely. We won’t leave the Peak Crossing before the ferry arrives. We’ll get on the flying ship to the Heng region, then head straight for Uncle’s place.”

…

“Looking around, this Sky-Devouring Beast is still the most imposing. If it opened its mouth to eat, how much would it swallow in one gulp?”

One of the Yuhuai Mountain disciples jokingly remarked as their contingent emerged from the blue-brick building. Wei Yuansheng chimed in.

“I have a large horse farm in my mortal home. I dare say that even a thousand horses there wouldn’t be enough for that Sky-Devouring Beast to swallow whole!”

Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi walked front and center. Although they didn’t speak, Ji Yuan was listening to the jokes and discussions of the people behind them. However, when they looked up, they encountered a group of women.

Some of the women carried swords on their backs, some held swords, some had fuchen7 in their hands. Some wore light veils, and some had their long hair tied up in a bun. They all had an ethereal, elegant air about them as they walked.

The woman leading the group carried a fuchen, her long hair reaching her waist, with red ribbons wrapped around her temples and hanging down to her feet. She was beautiful, and in fact possessed extremely high cultivation.

“Mr. Ji, these are all fellow Daoists of the Weimei Sect. It is rare for them to accept men, and they do not like to socialize in the cultivation world.”

Ju Yuanzi whispered to Ji Yuan, who nodded subconsciously, his gaze still lingering. However, before he could look away, the two groups brushed past each other without exchanging greetings.

As the people from Yuhuai Mountain gazed at the Weimei Sect contingent, the women ono the other side were watching them in return. Some of them glanced at the jade pendants on the waists of the Yuhuai Mountain group, already knowing which organization they belonged to.

“Grandmaster, they are all from Yuhuai Mountain. Hmm, that one with the strange eyes doesn’t have a jade pendant.”

A woman spoke to the female cultivator in front of her using a telepathy technique. When the latter nodded slightly, she keenly noticed that when the younger cultivator of the sect mentioned that his eyes were “strange,” the man in the blue robe glanced at her slightly, smiled, and then left with Yuhuai Mountain’s group.

Chapter 462: Terrifying Demonic Feather

After the people from Yuhuai Mountain left, the group of female cultivators from Weimei1 Sect, led by the elder at the front, stepped into the building with blue bricks and glazed tiles. However, the elder glanced back in the direction the Yuhuai Mountain cultivators had gone.

She hadn’t been able to discern the cultivation level of that man with the pale gray eyes; clearly, he couldn’t possibly be an ordinary person. It was as if he knew everything she had been saying in her telepathic messages.

In this situation, the elder of the Weimei Sect knew that it was probably not that the man was deliberately using a technique to probe her, but rather that he was someone whose cultivation had reached a level where his mind was clear enough and his spirit was pure enough that he could sense when there were conversations about him.

“Grandmaster, I never expected that the people from Yuhuai Mountain, who have rarely left their territory for hundreds of years, would send out so many people this time. There are even a few with unfathomable cultivation. I underestimated them before.”

The woman who had just spoken had obviously noticed Ji Yuan’s faint sidelong glance earlier, since he had been looking at her. Ju Yuanzi, who had been beside Ji Yuan, also possessed unfathomable cultivation, hence her current apprehensive admiration.

Hearing her grand-disciple’s words, the elder cultivator was slightly taken aback and turned to look at her.

“Do you know why they’re here?”

“Uh…Grandmaster, it’s the year of Wuxu, and it’s that time of year again, so naturally they are going to participate in the Cultivator’s Grand Assembly.”

The elder nodded in realization.

“Oh… another sixty years have passed…”

The group fell silent and slowly entered the building. The interior space was much larger than it appeared from the outside, likely due to the use of some kind of space-condensing formation.

The first floor was a spacious hall with various plaques hanging on the walls, some glowing and others dim. The plaques indicated the names of various flying boats, exotic beasts, and floating islands. Cultivators from Yuelu Mountain were in charge of overseeing the arrangements.

Upon seeing the distinctive female cultivators from the Weimei Sect enter, a cultivator from Yuelu Mountain hurriedly stepped forward to greet them.

“My surname is Li, and I am one of the officials in charge of the Peak Ferry Crossing on Yuelu Mountain. Thank you for your hard work, fellow Taoists of the Weimei Sect!”

“Hmm, Fellow Taoist Li, there’s no need for such formalities. Just tell us who wishes to ride the Sky-Devouring Beast, and whether there are any additional transport items.”

The people of the Weimei Sect were known for their taciturn nature and aversion to excessive contact with outsiders. As the person in charge, the cultivator surnamed Li was well aware of this, so he quickly took out a bundle of jade slips that were specially used for recording information. He swiped his finger to find the column for the Sky-Devouring Beast that had arrived today, and then pulled out the jade slip and handed it to the Weimei Sect cultivator with both hands.

“Fellow Taoists, please take a look. Everything is listed above. If there are no urgent matters, we hope that the Sky-Devouring Beast can stay at the Peak Crossing for two days so that anyone with a sudden whim to travel can also be transported. If you need a place to cultivate in seclusion, please come upstairs!”

“Alright, we understand!”

The people from the Weimei Sect had no interest in going up to the upper floors, and planned to turn around and leave. If they wanted to cultivate, wouldn’t it be better to use their own Sky-Devouring Beast?

However, just as she turned around, the female cultivator leading the group, a disciple of a certain generation, suddenly asked Cultivator Li a question.

“Fellow Taoist Li is the administrator of Peak Ferry Crossing, so you must be very familiar with all the powerful seniors who have come and gone. May I ask if you recognize either of the two highly skilled seniors who were with Yuhuai Mountain?”

After thinking for a moment, the Taoist Li replied.

“Of the two of them, the long-bearded old man is the Ju Yuanzi, True Immortal of Yuhuai Mountain. This senior rarely goes out. The earliest record of him in Yuelu Mountain dates back to six hundred years ago. His true age is unknown, but the depth of his cultivation is unimaginable.”

Age was not necessarily the standard for measuring cultivation level. For example, due to talent or other reasons, a sixty-year-old young cultivator might not have a lower cultivation level than a hundred-year-old cultivator. However, if one was old enough, then it was indeed a standard. After all, if one’s cultivation level was not sufficient for such an age, they would have died long ago.

After speaking about Ju Yuanzi, the Taoist Li thought of Ji Yuan and continued.

“There is another senior whom I have never met, but all the cultivators of Yuhuai Mountain treat him with great respect, even Ju Yuanzi. They all call him ‘Mr. Ji’. In my opinion, that senior is probably not a cultivator from Yuhuai Mountain, but he has a good personal relationship with the sect.”

“Alright, thank you for informing me, fellow Taoist!”

“You’re welcome!”

After asking their questions, the female cultivators of the Weimei Sect left, more decisively than many men. Once they were gone, the Li family administrator breathed a slight sigh of relief. The people of the Weimei Sect did not like to deal with outsiders, and outsiders who knew of the Weimei Sect’s habits were similar.2

It wasn’t that the people of the Weimei Sect are unreasonable. Among the orthodox cultivators of the immortal path, those who were truly unreasonable were actually a minority. However, there were quite a few who were stubborn or intolerant of any injustice. The Weimei Sect belonged to both the former and the latter categories. Their cultivation method emphasized tranquility, clarity, and a calm mind. Therefore, they sometimes seemed very indifferent. If opinions differed, the expressionless, nonchalant, yet unyielding female cultivators of the Weimei Sec were the most terrifying.

At the Peak Ferry Market, a group of cultivators from Yuhuai Mountain strolled around in groups. Ji Yuan, Ju Yuanzi, and Qiu Feng were traveling together. Qiu Feng, who had arrived earlier, was introducing some special buildings in the market to Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi.

These were mainly places run by cultivators, mostly selling various treasures and spiritual items. Some cultivators even used their unique expertise to identify spiritual items for companions, thus building good relationships and karma.

Some were market stalls, while others were standalone buildings, clearly beginning to develop a certain “brand effect”.

The market here was obviously much more lively and abundant than the small market on the flying boat at Nine Peaks Mountain that Ji Yuan had visited before, with countless strange and wonderful items to be found.

Ji Yuan even saw immortal cultivators selling all sorts of rare little spirits. These little spirits were all very weak, some of which could be easily killed by mortals if they weren’t careful, but each had its own unique uses. He passed by a stall where an old immortal cultivator was selling a kind of dark blue spirit.

Another was a tiny figure, about the size of a thumb, with a pair of transparent blue wings on its back. It was confined in a black metal cage, covered with a black cloth. However, thanks to a technique, people could see three of those tiny figures playing together inside, while the figures themselves could not see outside.

The stalls of cultivators differed greatly from those in secular markets in that the stall owners rarely hawked their wares. Instead, they focused on quiet cultivation at their stalls, and only spoke when interested fellow cultivators came to them.

Upon seeing Ji Yuan and the group of cultivators from Yuhuai Mountain stop, the old cultivator began to introduce his wares.

“Fellow Taoists, the creature in this cage is the Blue Dream Demon. It likes to steal dreams from people’s ears while they are asleep, also stealing some of their spirit. Some people wake up feeling listless even though they have gotten enough sleep; these demons may be the cause.”

“What other uses do they have?”

Ji Yuan asked. The stall owner thought about it carefully.

“If they wish, they can help people revisit their dreams, or repeat or continue the same dream, but they are also very fragile and are often crushed to death by people turning over in their sleep.”

Because these spirits were born from the interaction between the mind and dreams, the majority of their body would quickly dissipate and disappear after death, leaving only a few traces of blood. Some people would wake up in the morning to find bloodstains on their pillows and mats, but no injuries on their own bodies. This might have been because they crushed the spirit to death that night.

“Well, fellow Taoists, are you interested in buying them? I’ll also include the cage. If you can give me an ounce or two of Wood Element Essence, or some kind of Rejuvenating Pill to replenish my vital energy, that would be fine too.”

Qiu Feng couldn’t help but interject.

“These kinds of spirits are of little use to us, so why would we buy them? But this thing looks rather special.”

As Qiu Feng spoke, he picked up a golden-red feather from the stall. It was about the length of his forearm. After he infused it with spiritual energy, it emitted a faint heat.

“I don’t know what kind of demon left this feather. It doesn’t possess any discernible demonic aura. I found it in the Eastern Sea years ago. Occasionally, it will emit heat, but only to the extent that you can activate it now. It can neither be used to refine spiritual artifacts, nor can it aid in cultivation. Perhaps it has other uses, but I don’t know of them.”

Ji Yuan hadn’t paid much attention to the feather at first, until Qiu Feng infused it with spiritual energy, activating its heated current. An extremely discomforting sensation emanated from the feather, startling Ji Yuan so much that he almost took a few steps back. Only his strong mental fortitude allowed him to stay still.

‘Are you kidding me… No demonic aura? It’s overflowing with demonic energy!’

Ji Yuan had already opened his divine eyes wide, staring intently at the feather in Qiu Feng’s hand. The feather that Qiu Feng and the stall owner were chatting about so calmly was emitting a terrifying demonic aura, black and red in color. It was very faint, but very terrifying.

Ji Yuan had rarely felt genuine fear in recent years.

If a single feather could evoke such a reaction, Ji Yuan could hardly imagine what kind of demon its owner would have provoked had it been present. Could it be…?

As he pondered, Ji Yuan couldn’t help but look up at the sky, as if he could see the sun through the perpetual clouds above the summit.

“Mr. Ji, Mr. Ji?”

“Huh?”

Ji Yuan snapped back to reality. The demonic aura emanating from the feather had distracted him, causing him to drift into a daze, a rare occurrence for him.

Ju Yuanzi and Qiu Feng were among the few cultivators of Yuhuai Mountain who were relatively familiar with Ji Yuan. Seeing that Mr. Ji was staring blankly at the feather, they knew it was likely extraordinary, but the stall owner didn’t know that.

“Um, Mr. Ji, this feather looks quite nice. Would you like to buy it as a decoration?”

Ji Yuan was taken aback by Qiu Feng’s question, but then smiled and replied.

“That’s right, but it’s not for decoration. Since this feather can absorb spiritual energy, I am not convinced of its uselessness, and want to go back and use spiritual energy to investigate whether it has other uses. By the way, fellow Taoist, how much does this feather cost?”

The old cultivator glanced at the three of them and held up two fingers.

“Two catties of wood element essence, or two and a half catties of condensed essences from the other five elements, or pills that replenish vital energy, such as the Rejuvenating Pill, will also be acceptable!”

“So expensive? Didn’t you say it was useless?”

Qiu Feng couldn’t help but ask, and the old cultivator smiled.

“Things are valued for their rarity. I can’t do anything with it, but I also can’t find its origin, either. Besides, these two fellow Taoists are highly skilled, so perhaps they have some kind of method or technique to use it.”

“You…”

Qiu Feng wanted to speak, but Ji Yuan stopped him.

“Fellow Taoist is right, and I will not haggle. However, I do not have any Wood Element Essence or any pills to replenish vital energy. Would these be acceptable?”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan took out two newly refined spirit coins from his sleeve.

As soon as the spirit coins appeared, the surrounding spiritual energy subtly stirred. A deep brass-colored light flowed along the edge of the spirit coins, and a Taoist aura condensed and did not dissipate.

The old cultivator subconsciously moved closer.

“May I take a closer look?”

“Take it.”

Ji Yuan handed over one of the spirit coins. The old cultivator took it and used his senses to inspect it, gradually uncovering its mysteries, but still feeling somewhat unsatisfied, as it was not entirely clear.

“This item is called a spirit coin, it’s something I carefully refined. It has many wonderful uses. Are two such coins worth the price of the goods?”

Qiu Feng stared at the coins, his hand instinctively rising.

“Mr. Ji, I have Wood Element Essence, I have it! These spirit coins, why don’t you…”

“I’ll sell it, I’ll sell it! I only want these two spirit coins. I don’t even want the Wood Element Essence now, I just want the spirit coins!”

The stall owner, being a cultivator, was now rather impatient, watching Qiu Feng warily, fearing that the spirit money he was about to obtain would change hands.

“Haha, alright, here you go too, please keep it safe.”

Ji Yuan handed over the other coin, then reached out and took the feather.
