T/n: dun dun DUNNNN

The water vats they are referring to are enormous, jar-shaped storage vats of varying size, averaging at about 4.5ft/1.3m in diameter. A number of them can be found in the Forbidden City, and were used to hold water in case of a fire. They are also referred to as “Menhai” (門海, Gate Sea), “Auspicious Vats” (吉祥缸), or “Taiping Vats” (太平缸, Peace Vats).

Chapter 469: The Evil Creature That Should Have Been Extinct

Content Warning: Entomophobia (many crawly bugs). See end of chapter for a summary.

In the storm far behind the flying boat, the crimson dragon was darting wildly through the gale-force winds, even actively approaching the lightning bolts that struck down from the sky.

The red dragon’s body was now covered with a dense sheet of strange insects. Some appeared soft and intertwined, stuck to the dragon’s scales, while others resembled beetles and were constantly tearing at him. Anyone who saw this up close would definitely feel their skin crawl.

“Roar…”

Flames burst from the crimson dragon’s mouth and continuously overflowed, attempting to burn the countless strange insects on his body.

“Boom…”

Lightning struck down from the sky, pouring down on the crimson dragon. The lightning bolts struck down scores of the strange insects, which fell sporadically, but those were few compared to the number on the dragon’s body.

Whoosh whoosh whoosh whoosh…

From the turbulent and murky seawater, countless strange insects broke through the surface and surged up, aiming to ensnare the red dragon soaring in the sky.

The red dragon was truly terrified. As a dragon, and a young and vigorous one at that, he rarely experienced fear. Although he might fear stronger cultivators, that fear was more of awe, unlike the trembling terror he was experiencing now.

These countless strange insects that clung to his body were not afraid of the dragon’s might and kept biting at his dragon body, trying to kill him by gnawing away at him. They also slowed down the dragon’s speed, making it impossible for him to dodge further attacking insects.

The draconic power remained abundant, but the dragon’s body was becoming increasingly heavy. These strange insects seemed intent on dragging the red dragon down into the murky, desolate ocean currents.

“Roar…”

The red dragon struggled to fly, not daring to enter the water. These things had emerged from down below; once caught in their trap, there was no way to shake them off. Only the heavens knew how many more were below; going in would be certain death.

It was at this moment that the panicked red dragon saw the celestial light ahead. Regardless of the differences between immortals and monsters — the dragon race was different from other monster races, after all — he immediately cried for help.

“I am the Crimson Dragon under the Dragon Lord’s command! Immortals ahead, save me! Immortals, save me! Roar… Roo…”

Although the red dragon called for help from the flying ship ahead, he did not believe that the other party would immediately intervene. However, he still held a sliver of hope, and he flew towards the flying ship with all his might.

Even if those immortals didn’t come to my rescue, they might be able to share some of the burden.

Just as that hope arose…

“Shiiing…”

A clear, sharp sound of a sword being drawn rang out.

With a “whoosh,” a dazzling sword light overwhelmed the thunder, illuminating the entire region of the ocean. A sharp sword intent suddenly appeared, and then the sky and sea split in two, one to the left and one to the right…

This sword cleaved the air front of the flying ship and the seawater, gale, torrential rain, and lightning behind it, creating a deep, trench-like passage. In an instant, the flying ship and the dragon were clearly visible to each other.

‘An immortal has intervened!’

Overjoyed, the dragon unleashed his monstrous power and flew towards the flying ship. On the deck, at the stern of the flying ship in the distance, many immortal cultivators also clearly noticed the red dragon’s condition.

The once fiery red dragon was now almost entirely black, save for his head, which was constantly spewing flames. As he drew closer, a closer look revealed that he was covered in strange insects.

Anyone with trypophobia1 would definitely find this unbearable.

“Immortal Master, save me…!”

The dragon roared. The Green Vine Sword had already flown back to Ji Yuan’s side; after all, immortal swords were not convenient for killing insects.

Just as Ji Yuan was about to try a different approach, a cultivator nearby spoke up.

“Fellow Taoist, I’m here to lend you a hand!”

“Me too!”

At the same moment the voices sounded, four or five beams of light shot out. Some were spiritual artifacts, some were talismanic lights, and one was a flame transformed into rings of fire that shot into the distance.

The beams of light did not fly towards the Crimson Dragon, but rather shot directly towards the sea surface. The moment they touched the seawater, they transformed into a massive talisman of light, covering a large area of ​​the sea. The strange insects below could not escape the range of the light talisman at all.

The remaining flames and magical light struck the dragon’s body, but the dragon knew this was an attempt to help, and neither dodged nor evaded.

“Boom…”

“Whoosh whoosh whoosh…”

Fire and lightning intertwined on the dragon’s body, and a spiritual artifact swept across the densely packed area of ​​strange insects, like a steel brush scraping off their scales and fins.

“Roar…”

A dragon roar rang out again from the red dragon’s mouth again, this time much louder. Even though being grazed by the cultivators’ techniques and spells was painful, he was feeling increasingly lighter than before. His flying speed was increasing as well, allowing him to approach the flying boat more quickly.

About twenty breaths later, the dragon had crossed a hundred feet away from the flying ship. A cultivator from Xuanxin Mansion made hand seals and cast a technique to disperse part of the flying boat’s array, and the dragon took the opportunity to quickly fly inside.

“Everyone, make way so that this dragon can land!”

The dragon did not transform into human form, though it was unclear whether he was unwilling or unable. Thankfully, his speed noticeably slowed slowed down after entering the array, and the surrounding immortal cultivators all moved aside to make room on the stern deck.

“Bang…”

The twenty-foot-long red dragon crashed onto the deck. His scales, covered in slime and rainwater, scraped against the deck with a screeching sound, sliding over several dozen feet before coming to a stop.

Several Xuanxin Mansion cultivators glanced in Ji Yuan’s direction; he was why they had readily opened the formation. Since Mr. Ji was here and had taken action immediately, it didn’t matter if they let the red dragon in.

After the Crimson Dragon entered the flying ship’s formation, the turbulence on the surface of the desolate ocean subsided considerably, and quickly returned to normal. Apart from the fierce storm and turbulent waves accompanied by strong winds, there was no remaining trace of those strange insects.

“Huff… haah… huff… haah…”

The Crimson Dragon collapsed on the deck, his breathing like a draft of wind, carrying a fishy smell and a burning sensation.

This was the first time many of the cultivators had seen a flood dragon, or seen one from so close up. After all, dragons were generally considered powerful monsters, and given their pride, such an opportunity was truly rare. Therefore, a number of cultivators gradually gathered around the dragon’s body, including some bold mortals.

The dragon’s eyes were slightly closed, his whiskers twitching occasionally as he calmed his mind and recovered his strength. After a long while, he opened his eyes slightly, his amber eyes scanning the surroundings. His mouth opened and closed, making sounds.

“Many thanks to all the immortals for your help, otherwise I might have been caught off guard and drowned today…”

The Crimson Dragon was much calmer now, but thinking back on what just happened, the he was still filled with lingering terror.

Ji Yuan, who had somehow appeared in front of the Crimson Dragon, gazed at him calmly and asked:

“What were those strange insects? How could a dragon like you, capable of flight and swimming, get entangled like this?”

Judging from the results of the cultivators’ attacks, those strange insects, though disgusting, were not too powerful. They were all killed or swept away. Even if a dragon could not completely eliminate these things, it would not be rendered unable to escape.

The Green Vine Sword did not conceal its presence at this moment, and hovered diagonally behind Ji Yuan. Based on his intuition, the Crimson Dragon judged that this was the immortal cultivator who had first used his sword to divide the sea and sky.

“Replying to the immortal, I did not want to waste too much energy on the fierce winds, so I originally swam into the currents of the desolate ocean, intending to cross this area… As that time, it was night, and the storm was raging. The bottom of this desolate ocean is pitch black, and the currents are turbulent. Those strange insects were originally lying on the seabed, no different from rocks and mud and seemingly lifeless. After I disturbed them…”

At this, the Crimson Dragon felt a little scared. After calming himself down, he continued.

“It was like a swarm of locusts bursting from their nest; they all came to life and frantically clung to me, as if the entire seabed were teeming with these things. If I hadn’t clenched my teeth and sealed all my orifices in time, they probably would have burrowed into my eyes, ears, nose, and mouth…”

“I was caught off guard and was sucked in by these strange insects. I felt incredibly heavy, and my monstrous power and spiritual energy kept leaking out of my body. I couldn’t even swim fast. What was even more terrifying was that there seemed to be an endless number of these strange insects…”

Upon hearing the Crimson Dragon’s words, some of the immortal cultivators present could imagine the scene vividly: his four claws had originally been pressed to his belly, twisting as he swam underwater, then suddenly he was covered in insects that clung to his entire body, biting and burrowing into him—it was truly terrifying.

“Do you recognize what kind of insect this is?”

Ji Yuan pointed to the side, where a cultivator had used a spiritual weapon to grab some of the things, which were now encircled in a spiritual light.

The strange insects varied in size, with the smallest being no larger than the thickness of an average pinky, with the largest as thick as a forearm. There were probably more than thirty of them, and they came in more than a dozen different types. Some resembled worms but had hard, ring-like markings. Others resembled beetles but had densely packed mouthparts. Others still looked like leeches. In short, many of them were quite disgusting.

Although there seem to be many kinds, Ji Yuan could tell with just a sniff that these insects all smelled very similar, practically the same kind of creature.

The Crimson Dragon’s gaze swept over the cultivator’s spiritual light, and its dragon pupils narrowed slightly.

“There are many evil creatures in this sea, they are born and die every day. I don’t recognize this one. If I had known that such a thing existed here, I would never have swum here, not even under pain of death!”

The immortal cultivators nearby were also whispering among themselves, their conversation revolving around the strange insects and the red dragon on the ship. Some were even speculating about why these insects were not afraid of the dragon’s might.

“Dragon Corpse Insects! These are Dragon Corpse Insects!”

Ju Yuanzi, who had just emerged from the cabin, saw the strange insects and immediately gave the answer.

“This insect breeds from the corpse of a dragon. Most of them are not afraid of the dragons’ might. It is a very evil creature. If any dragon clan in the four seas encounters them, they would surely exterminate them!”

Old men were treasure troves of knowledge. Ju Yuanzi had cultivated for over eight hundred years. Even though he was a reclusive hermit from Yuhuai Mountian, he was definitely knowledgeable and experienced. As soon as he spoke, Ji Yuan naturally had no doubts.

Upon hearing this, the Crimson Dragon reacted even more violently.

“What?! Dragon Corpse Insects? Weren’t these evil creatures supposed to be extinct?! Impossible, impossible!”

The dragon’s roar boomed loudly, causing the surrounding immortal cultivators to frown repeatedly, while some daring mortals who were watching the spectacle were so frightened that they covered their ears and collapsed to the floor.

T/n: so many creepy crawlies…

Summary for our entomophobic friends: JY cleaves the sea and sky in two, clearing the path for the dragon, who’s covered in bugs. Other cultivators join in, and the dragon successfully lands hard on the ship, free from the bugs, which vanish. We learn from Ju Yuanzi that those bugs are Dragon Corpse Insects, and come from (you guessed it) dragon corpses, but the red dragon says that they should be extinct.

I agree that this would be trypophobic, but honestly it’s more entomophobic.

Chapter 470: Ocean Pavilion’s Mysterious Mirror

Ju Yuanzi glanced at the dragon.

“What, you think I would lie to you? Those things have been occasionally found even in some major rivers and waterways on land, and some have even become quite powerful and transformed into human form.”

Ji Yuan understood Ju Yuanzi’s unspoken implication: it would be difficult to truly eradicate the dragon corpse insects. After all, the world was so vast, and even though the dragon race was powerful, it was impossible for them to keep watch over every corner. Moreover, it was possible that those that had become powerful can hide away.

Of course, if a Dragon Corpse Insect truly became powerful, given the strength of the dragon race, it would immediately sever all ties with its past, live as an ordinary monster, and try its best not to appear in front of the dragon race.

Even if all the powerful ones were wiped out, and all the ones hiding outside were killed, there would never be a shortage of people with ulterior motives in the world. There might very well be someone who would cultivate something as disgusting as the dragon corpse insect.

Ji Yuan could understand the unspoken meaning behind these words, and he believed that many in the dragon race could also think of these possibilities. But what could he do? As long as the dragon race could not control everything, such things were inevitable. Therefore, he could only issue a strict order after eliminating all the dragon corpse insects he could find.

Upon hearing Ju Yuanzi’s words, the red flood dragoncalmed down and began to ponder carefully.

Ji Yuan remained silent for a while before asking.

“You say you are an aquatic creature under the Dragon Lord, which Dragon Lord would that be?”

Chi Jiao dared not delay in responding to Ji Yuan’s words and quickly replied.

“The situation was urgent just now, so I lied and said I was a dragon under the Dragon Lord’s command. In reality, I only live in the ocean region belonging to Dragon Lord Guang Sheng in the Outer Eastern Sea, and am not under the Dragon Lord’s command. Please forgive me, Immortal Master…”

The immortal cultivator before him with the unusual look in his eyes seemed to know the Dragon Lord. He couldn’t continue to lie at this time, or it would be bad if it truly reached the Dragon Lord’s ears.

“Oh… Dragon Lord Guang Sheng…”

Ji Yuan had never heard of this name before, but since he dared to use the title “Lord” in his name, he must be a true dragon.

“Mr. Ji, what should we do about these dragon corpse insects? There are obviously quite a few of these things under this desolate ocean current.”

The people of Yuhuai Mountain knew about the relationship between Ji Yuan and the Dragon Lord of the Tongtian River. Dragons might not get along with each other, but the Dragon Corpse Insects were absolutely hated by all dragons in this world.

Ji Yuan glanced at the red flood dragon beside him.

“You should rest for a while and then leave on your own. This is no small matter for your dragon race. Notify the appropriate people accordingly. As for the dragon corpse insects in the ocean currents below…”

“I hope you will not take action against these dragon corpse insects, Immortal Master, as this is a matter for my dragon clan. After I return, my dragon clan will use this place as a starting point to carefully investigate the source of these evil creatures. For now, let them stay below.”

The red dragon’s words made sense, so Ji Yuan nodded.

“Alright, then we won’t interfere.”

The red flood dragon glanced at the cultivator who had captured more than a dozen dragon corpse insects. the latter understood the dragon’s intentions and waved his hand, incinerating all those strange insects to ashes, so that the dragon would not think that he wanted to cultivate these things.

Although it had been attacked before, it was still a living flood dragon, and in truth, the dragon was not seriously injured. At most, it was quite frightened. After resting for only a quarter of an hour, the dragon did not linger any longer and took off into the air, flying off into the distance. This time, it was willing to endure the strong winds to fly high enough.

After the red flood dragon left, Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship did not continue moving, but instead remained stationary.

Now that they had already saved the red dragon, they might as well do the dragon race a favor by keeping an eye on the dragon corpse insects down below. They could make up for lost time later.

About three days later, the roars of dragons echoed from the distance, followed by the appearance of light on the horizon, which calmed the chaotic gales.

A colossal old yellow dragon appeared, accompanied by more than a dozen flood dragons. The old yellow dragon was roughly several hundred feet long, with long whiskers that stretched for hundreds of feet. The other dragons looked like small mudfish1 in comparison.

“The Dragon Lord really came himself! It seems the Dragon Clan is indeed very concerned about the Dragon Corpse Insects.”

An old cultivator who had joined the fight immediately after Ji Yuan made a move remarked. Another elder beside him immediately echoed the sentiment. These two old men had been by Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi’s side for the past three days; their intuition told them that the expert who had caused the galaxy to fall that day must have been one of them.

Ji Yuan glanced at the old man who had spoken, then looked back at the enormous yellow dragon. Wasn’t it obvious? To the dragon race, these insects were nothing more than grave robbers; anyone would hate them to the core.

Moreover, judging from previous experience, the dragon corpse insects didn’t just eat dragon corpses. Given the opportunity, they wouldn’t be picky eaters.

Even before the true dragon and the flood dragons arrived, a booming voice already rang out.

“Thank you all for your help, but this is a matter for the dragon race. You outsiders should leave immediately!”

Rumble rumble rumble…

The booming sound of his voice was like thunder, making the waves in the surrounding sea even more turbulent.

At this moment, the old dragon spoke directly, and his voice, carrying the power of a true dragon, made all the passengers on Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship feel intense pressure.

However, as a true dragon, speaking like this to immortal cultivators could already be considered quite polite.

The two administrators of Xuanxin Mansion were also on the stern deck fo the flying ship. Upon hearing this, they looked at Ji Yuan and asked him.

“What does Mr. Ji think?”

Ji Yuan watched from afar as the yellow dragon and the flood dragons drew ever closer.

“We had no intention of getting involved in the first place. Since they don’t want us to get involved either, there’s no need for us to stay. We should focus on continuing on.”

“That’s right, resuming our travels is the priority.”

“Absolutely! Absolutely!”

Several people nearby echoed in agreement. The dragon clan clearly did not want the matter to become public, and everyone on the flying ship understood this.

Thus, the flying ship shone brightly, and a gentle wave slowly rose from under its hull. It carried the flying ship away and back to its original course without making a sound, thus responding to the old yellow dragon’s request with concrete actions, as if to say:

‘We left at your request, so you can’t say we were impolite, can you?’

A dozen or so breaths later, the yellow dragon led a group of dragons to this desolate region of the ocean currents. The red flood dragon stayed beside the yellow dragon. Looking at the fading celestial light in the distance, the yellow dragon spoke.

“This must be the place. I can already smell that nauseating stench. It really is Dragon Corpse Insects. You all, swim downstream with the ocean current and search. I’ll deal with this place first!”

As he spoke, the yellow dragon suddenly flew downwards, bursting through the sea surface with a “bang” and diving into the ocean.

The seabed in this area suddenly lit up in the very next moment, with only a blurry silhouette of a dragon visible in the center.

“Boom…”

In an instant, silver serpents danced on the seabed, lightning flashed everywhere…

On the flying ship, Ji Yuan and the others looked back into the distance, wondering how the yellow dragon would deal with it, when suddenly they saw the entire seabed light up. Then, a pervasive aura of thunder and lightning spread. Under the destructive power, the currents of this desolate ocean seemed to surge.

‘As expected of a true dragon, what a grand gesture!’

Ji Yuan’s thoughts wandered. He considered that, although his old friend lived in inland waters, he was still part of the Eastern Sea. He should know this yellow dragon. Perhaps he could ask the Old Dragon about the follow-up later. He believed that such information should be readily available at least among the true dragons of the Eastern Sea.

…

A few days later, the sky began to clear. One night, the flying boat left the sea and took to the air again. They had left that region of desolate ocean currents and strong winds and returned to the normal expanse of ocean.

That morning, the passengers woke up to find that they had left the dark, desolate ocean current region.

The immortal cultivators were fine, but the mortals and even some spirits who were bored couldn’t wait to go up to the deck to breathe fresh air.

There was a thin layer of mist suspended around the flying ship, but as the sun rose higher, the mist gradually dissipated, revealing the sea below.

“Everyone, the Ocean Pavilion’s Mysterious Mirror is just a hundred li ahead. Although we are traveling on a realm-crossing ferry, we should not miss this rare and magnificent spectacle, if time permits. If you have any relatives or friends still resting in your cabins, please be sure to wake them up and either bring them to the Mirror Pavilion to the upper deck. Otherwise, you will regret it for the rest of your life!”

Aside from those who lived on the ship, most people couldn’t frequently take the realm-crossing ferry, and some might only ride it once in their entire lives, so missing out on some beautiful scenery could truly be a lifelong regret.

Soon, many people reached the Mirror Pavilion inside the main cabin, while even more more went to the deck and peered out from the ship’s railing.

“Whoosh… whoosh…”

The Yin-Yang sails billowed, emitting soft whistling sounds. The people on board felt their bodies being pulled slightly backward, while the speed of the flying boat had increased considerably. It streaked across the sky with a golden trail of light, heading towards the distant, faintly visible mountain range in the sea.

The first thing that caught the eye were two crescent-shaped island mountain ranges, one large and one small, standing opposite each other in the sea.

On the larger one stood a towering peak, and the side facing the boat was a steep cliff, smooth as if it had been cleaved by a heavenly axe.

With Ji Yuan’s supernatural eyes, he could see a flow of spiritual light on the islands, indicating the presence of a cultivation sect. Even more striking was the “mirror” between the two crescent-shaped islands.

Calm and deep blue, yet faintly shimmering with iridescent colors, it must possess some special quality at play, which was why Ji Yuan could see it so clearly. Even before they approached, it already displayed its exquisite beauty.

The flying boat quickly approached the two crescent-shaped mountain ranges, allowing the people on board to get a clearer view. Large characters had been carved on the outermost cliffs, their sharp edges and strong sword intent clearly indicating that they had been left by a master swordsman.

“Ocean Pavilion, Mysterious Mirror.”

As Ji Yuan murmured these words, he could feel the powerful sword intent emanating from the carved characters, concentrated and unyielding, striking and awe-inspiring.

“Everyone, please refrain from making loud noises. This is the Ocean Pavilion’s Mysterious Mirror, a place of quiet cultivation. It is already a great kindness that we are allowed to enjoy the beautiful scenery of the Ocean Mirror. Please do not offend anyone.”

An administrator from Xuanxin Mansion transmitted messages mentally throughout the flying ship. After giving instructions, he activated the spiritual array to guide the flying ship slowly downwards. Soon, the bottom of the ship broke through the sea surface, passed the edge of the cliff, and sailed into the “Mirror Surface”.

The water was crystal clear, the sea surface was utterly still, and the light seemed to penetrate directly to the seabed, but all that could be seen was unfathomable depths. Sunlight streamed through, creating a kaleidoscope of colors within.

Even Ji Yuan was somewhat stunned, not to mention mortals and some of the spirits. He could imagine that it would be even more beautiful at night than during the day.

“Well, we have already been delayed for a few days. Let me ask you all first. If you are not in a hurry, we will wait until after tonight to leave. If you are in a hurry, we will continue on our way shortly.”

“No rush, no rush!”

“That’s right, no one will mind being short a day or two.”

“Yes, yes, let’s stay another day!”

Just as Ji Yuan had expected, the people on the ship all expressed their willingness to wait a little longer.

Chapter 471: If This Works, I’ll Call You Master

The Ocean Mirror between the two crescent-shaped islands was actually quite large. Xuanxin Mansion’s realm-crossing flying ship resembled a sesame seed in a bowl of water.

The ship did not continue sailing but instead stopped at the center of the “mirror”. Aside from the few days it had lingered in the currents of the desolate ocean region, this was the first time that the flying ship had stopped on its planned route since it had set sail from the Peak Crossing.

The flying ship was was already very stable in rough weather, but the sea here was even calmer. The wind stopped automatically within three zhang of the ocean surface, which reminded Ji Yuan of Dingfeng Island,1 which he had created during his cultivation to provide shelter for fishing boats at sea.

However, Dingfeng Island was far inferior to the calm and tranquil sea before him. Of course, to be frank, Ji Yuan could also say that Dingfeng Island was more in harmony with nature than the Ocean Mirror.

After the ship anchored, one of the administrators of Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship took off from the deck and flew towards the larger island. It seemed he was going to make contact with the people from Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion.2 He returned quickly, apparently not even having had a cup of tea, but was otherwise completely calm.

On the third floor of a restaurant on the deck, Ji Yuan and the Yuhuai Mountain contingent occupied three tables, each laden with fragrant dishes and wine.

The group enjoyed the beautiful scenery outside while eating, which added a unique flavor to the meal. They found it quite pleasant.

The food on this table was paid for by the people of Yuhuai Mountain. The dishes they ordered were not cheap, but they tasted very good. This meal alone improved Ji Yuan’s mood. In terms of his life philosophy, he had always been a person who was easily satisfied.

“Hey, someone’s fishing over there!”

Wei Yuansheng had just swallowed a bite of crispy pork when he suddenly saw a small boat on the crystal-clear sea in the distance. A man with a fishing rod stood on board.

Shang Yiyi also glanced over, then looked at the clear, transparent surface of the sea nearby.

“Are there fish here?”

The water here was so clear. As the saying went, “where the water is too clear, there are no fish.” Although the depths of the water were obscured by the changing light, it also gave the impression that there were no fish here, otherwise they would have been easily visible.

“Who knows, there might actually be fish.”

“Really? The fish beneath this mirror-like sea must be extraordinary, right?”

“Who knows…”

Ji Yuan held up his cup and looked down, then glanced thoughtfully at the person sitting on a small boat in the distance holding a fishing rod.

It seemed that he, Ji Yuan, also had a fishing rod, but he hadn’t used it for a very long time.

At that thought, Ji Yuan quickly picked up more food and ate for a while, seemingly refined but actually at an extremely fast pace. Then he left the table, saying that he was going to find the people of Xuanxin Mansion to ask something.

The most convenient way to find someone from Xuanxin Mansion was naturally to go to the mast, but Ji Yuan thought it would be better to go to the flying ship’s administrators instead.

A moment later, in a courtyard on the second deck, Ji Yuan explained his intentions to the administrator surnamed Du from Xuanxin Mansion. The latter’s expression turned slightly strange after hearing his explanation.

“Mr. Ji, you also want to go fishing?”

Ji Yuan smiled and nodded.

“Yes, the people at Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion didn’t say fishing was forbidden, did they?”

“Uh, they didn’t say so, but…”

If it were someone else bringing up this matter, Administrator Du would have either ignored it or politely found an excuse to send them away. But Ji Yuan was different; even if it might be a troublesome matter, he had to give it a try first.

“How about this, sir: please wait a moment while I go and ask the people at Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion. If there are no problems, you can go fishing later.”

“Oh, that would be wonderful, thank you so much, Administrator Du!”

Ji Yuan’s fishing addiction had kicked in, and he didn’t mind the trouble he was causing. After all, when he used to fish, he already basically knew everything that was in the rivers and lakes, which made fishing quite boring. But here, he couldn’t see anything under the surface, which made fishing much more exciting.

“No trouble at all, it’s just a errand. Please wait a moment, sir.”

With that, the administrator went up to the deck with Ji Yuan, and then flew alone to one of the crescent-shaped mountain ranges, while Ji Yuan stood on the ship’s railing to watch.

This time, the wait was much longer than before. Previously, it felt like he had just gone to make a brief appearance and returned immediately, but now, after waiting for quite some time, Administrator Du still hadn’t returned.

Furthermore, Ji Yuan also saw two cultivators riding the wind flying over the Mysterious Mirror Ocean and approach the fisherman’s small boat. They seemed to be talking to each other. The group of people frequently glanced towards the flying ship.

The group on the small boat finished their conversation and flew back to the island. A while later, Administrator Du of Xuanxin Mansion returned to the flying ship. As soon as he landed on the deck, he cupped his hands in greeting to Ji Yuan.

“I have fulfilled my mission. Sir, please feel free to cast your line. Would you like me to prepare a small boat and fishing rod for you?”

“No need, no need, I’ll just fish from this ship. As for the fishing rod, I have my own. Thank you for your help, Administrator Du!”

Ji Yuan quickly returned the bow and expressed his gratitude.

“Haha, Mr. Ji, you’re too kind. I’ll take my leave now. Please feel free to come and find me if you need anything.”

“Alright, take care, Administrator!”

Administrator Du nodded and slowly walked away. However, as soon as he rounded a building on the deck, he noticeably slowed down and looked back at Ji Yuan. Just then, he saw Ji Yuan flick his sleeve, and a lush green bamboo fishing rod flew out from it.

Every fisherman needed bait. Ji Yuan had taken out his fishing rod, but he didn’t know what to use as bait. After all, he had no idea what was down below. Ordinary rice grains certainly wouldn’t work.

After thinking it over, Ji Yuan took out a jujube pit from his sleeve.

Normal jujubes would not have almond like kernels inside their pits. Instead, they would have something like a soft membrane wrapping around tender insides.3 However, Ji Yuan’s pit came from one of Ju’an Pavilion’s jujubes, so naturally it was a little different.4

However, Ji Yuan did not intend to use the kernel inside the jujube pit as bait. Instead, he inserted the fishhook into the jujube pit so that the aroma of the jujube kernels could be slightly released.

“That’s about right!”

After doing all this, Ji Yuan gently flicked the fishing rod, and the fishhook with the jujube pit attached was cast far away.

The deck of the flying ship was still quite high above the water, but the fishing line on Ji Yuan’s fishing rod stretched on and on, as if it would never reach the end. Then, with a “plop,” the hook went into the water.

Ji Yuan smiled slightly, then sat sideways on the gunwale, holding the rod in one hand and looking at the mirror-like surface of the water. His gaze swept across the distance, where the fishermen on the small boat seemed to be watching Ji Yuan’s actions as well.

After the hook entered the water, the fishing line continued to extend, reaching a depth of thirty zhang, which Ji Yuan considered an appropriate distance.

This fishing line was made by silkworm farmers, who took silkworm sacs, kneaded them together, stretched them, and dried them in the shade. Originally, its thickness would have been about equivalent to a thin bamboo toothpick from modern times, but this single silkworm thread was hundreds or thousands of times thinner than that.

The fishing line, which Ji Yuan had refined, appeared to be only about a couple zhang long, but in reality, through continuous transformation and refinement, it ultimately had become as thin as silk. Ji Yuan had never attempted to measure its full distance. Although he nurtured it frequently, he rarely had the opportunity to use it.

Therefore, a fishing line of thirty zhang was merely a drop in the ocean for Ji Yuan’s emerald green bamboo fishing rod.

As for the strength of the fishing line, that depended on the fishing rod itself, which was like a spiritual artifact. It also depended on the user, Ji Yuan, who used pure spiritual power to sustain it; as long as he kept feeding it spiritual energy, the line would not break. If all else failed, he he could use the methods he had learned while going fishing in his previous life.

A little while later, Wei Yuansheng quietly ran up to Ji Yuan alone. Like an ordinary curious boy, he glanced at Ji Yuan’s fishing rod and then at the fishing line in the distance. He couldn’t see a float,5 so he turned back to Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, are you fishing?”

“Of course.”

Wei Yuansheng scratched his head.

“But are there any fish down here?”

Ji Yuan pointed to the small boat in the distance.

“Why would he be fishing here if there weren’t any?”

Wei Yuansheng looked into the distance.

“It’s been quite a while, but I haven’t seen him catch anything.”

“Even casting a net takes time, let alone fishing.”

As Ji Yuan spoke, he seemed to be able to sense the slight ripples underwater through the fishing line and hook. Although the surface of the water appeared as calm as a mirror, and although Ji Yuan could not feel anything except the current, the ripples in the water were definitely caused by some living creature disturbing the flow, meaning that something had passed near his hook.

“There really are fish!”

Ji Yuan smiled and waited patiently for the fish to take the bait.

On the small boat in the distance, the cultivator holding the fishing rod kept an eye on Ji Yuan in addition to his own fishing rod. After all, it was just the two of them fishing there. However, he knew very well that the person on the flying ship was just playing around.

After watching for a while, he suddenly saw Ji Yuan on the flying ship stand up from his seated position, as if he were about to raise his fishing rod.

“Haha, if you truly can catch one, I’ll call you Master!”

The cultivator’s mocking expression almost immediately vanished and his eyes bulged.

At the edge of the flying ship, Ji Yuan suddenly lifted the emerald green fishing rod, the fishing line shimmering with light as Ji Yuan pulled it taut.

“Splash… splash…”

The calm mirror surface was shattered as a creature underwater, biting the hook, dragged the fishing line around. The violently churning water below affected the surface, creating shimmering ripples on the mirror-like sea.

“There really is a fish! Sir, you caught one! You’re amazing!”

Yuansheng shouted excitedly, attracting the attention of many people around him. Ji Yuan, however, was too preoccupied with gripping his fishing rod to pay attention to anything else. The fishing line was being stretched continuously by something underwater, and he could only reel it in occasionally.

‘What is that? It’s incredibly strong!’

Ji Yuan was, after all, a cultivator with decent cultivation. Although he didn’t focus on body refinement, his physical body had been tempered by spiritual methods over time, and with the support of his spiritual power, he boasted considerable strength. Yet now he felt a sense of strain, indicating that the fish below was indeed no ordinary fish.

“Fellow Taoist! This fish cannot be pulled forcefully, otherwise even if the fishing line of the spiritual artifact is not broken, the fish will be crippled. We must let it drag the boat along!”

The fishermen over there couldn’t help but shout. If Ji Yuan’s spiritual artifact broke and the fish got away, it would truly be heartbreaking and a pity. But if Ji Yuan’s spiritual power was strong and the spiritual weapon was sturdy, then the fish would definitely be crippled, which would be a waste.

“Hey fellow Taoist, quickly take to the air and swing along the fishing line!”

However, no matter how anxious the fisherman was, Ji Yuan remained calm and focused. Only his arm swayed left and right in the direction of the fishing rod’s movement, while the line occasionally flashed with a subtle spiritual light.

While he had struggled before, Ji Yuan quickly found a pattern. The fishing rod trembled gently after each pull and release, then emitted a faint, thread-like wisp of lightning.

This lightning was the fruit of Ji Yuan’s long-term struggle against the tribulation lightning. None of the wisps would make the fish down below detect anything unusual, but little by little it would gradually weaken and begin to feel numb. The force with which it pulled on the rod in Ji Yuan’s hands would weaken quite bit in a short period of time.

A moment later, Ji Yuan changed his offensive strategy, focusing more on reeling in the line than letting it out.

“Hey, fellow Taoist, you are wasting such a precious resource!”

The cultivator on the other side grew anxious and took to the air, riding a gentle breeze towards the large ship.

“Tao…”

Before he could even finish speaking, Ji Yuan’s expression hardened, and with a flick of his right sleeve, he suddenly pulled up the fishing rod.

“Bang…”

A spray of water shot into the sky, rising three zhang high, and an enormous golden fish, along with a fishing line radiating spiritual light, was pulled from the water. Countless droplets of water shimmered with flowing light, like dazzling fireworks.

The cultivator stepped onto the breeze, his mouth slightly open, simply inhaling.

“Gasp… Master!”

Chapter 472: What? Eat It?

The cultivator’s mutter of “Master” was purely a subconscious slip, but he was close enough for Ji Yuan to hear him. Ji Yuan glanced at him, but didn’t pay any attention, and continued to focus on the big fish in front of him.

“Splash, splash splash splash…”

Even though Ji Yuan had already lifted the big fish out of the water, its violently thrashing tail still pulled up a lot of seawater. Streams of light surrounded the big fish, also pulling in more water, as if the light and water were stuck to the fish’s tail, making it impossible to lift it up as it put on a final struggle.

Through the splashes, the surrounding seawater even seemed to change its patterns of light and darkness. Ji Yuan was caught off guard and, on his first attempt to lift the rod, failed to lift the fish completely out of the water. Instead, it bounced back into it.

“Splash…”

The big fish darted back into the water, and a strong force traveled up the fishing rod, causing Ji Yuan’s body to sway forward. The fishing line loosened instantly, allowing the big fish to drag the hook and swim deeper into the sea.

The other cultivator, who was still riding the breeze, was quite anxious, but he fell silent. His intuition told him that the green-robed cultivator with the emerald fishing rod in front of him could definitely handle this situation, and perhaps might even possess quite a bit of knowledge about the spirit fish beneath the Mirror Ocean.

Ji Yuan had never lacked patience. He relentlessly played with the fish, remaining motionless for half an hour, gradually increasing the intensity of the lightning technique. Once hooked, the fish wouldn’t simply be able to spit it out.

“You think you can just escape after swallowing this jujube pit?”

Ji Yuan chuckled, his hands moving with controlled tension. The emerald green fishing rod curved and twisted, and the spiritual light on the silk fishing line subtly reflected the surrounding flowing light. Each slight glow was accompanied by a flash of lightning energy.

This method of electrofishing1 rendered the originally powerful golden fish’s struggles weak and ineffective. Combined with the characteristics of the fishing rod in Ji Yuan’s hand and his own skills, he became increasingly adept at handling the fish’s thrashing.

Moreover, having learned from the previous experience, Ji Yuan handled the approach more methodically this time.

Finally, just as the big fish once again used the force of its tail to bounce itself back to the surface, Ji Yuan suddenly increased his strength, instantly pulling the fishing rod into a crescent shape. In addition to gripping the fishing rod tightly with his right hand, he also flicked the fishing rod lightly with his left.

“Thunk-“

A crisp sound echoed from the point of contact between Ji Yuan’s left middle finger and the fishing rod. A distinct ripple of light vibrated across the rod, extending down along the fishing line to the large fish below.

“Whoosh…”

The connection between the golden fish’s tail and the mirror-like seawater below was severed in an instant. Ji Yuan’s eyes lit up, and he swung the rod again, sending the several-foot-long golden fish flying into the sky.

Then Ji Yuan quickly transferred the fishing rod to his left hand, raised his right arm high into the air, and flicked it towards the sky. All the onlookers could see the illusion of a large sleeve reaching for the sky. In the next instant, the enormous fish in the sky was caught and disappeared into Ji Yuan’s sleeve, completely severing its connection with the Mirror Ocean below.

“Mr. Ji, where’s the fish?”

Wei Yuansheng looked at the empty fishing rod, puzzled. Ji Yuan’s movements were too fast, and he hadn’t seen it clearly, but it seemed that Mr. Ji had taken it away with a flick of his sleeve.

“It’s here.”

Ji Yuan swung his right arm and flung the golden fish onto the deck, simultaneously covering its body with a film of water.

“This…isn’t a fish? No, I mean, this isn’t an ordinary fish?”

Wei Yuansheng, having embarked on the path of cultivation, quickly realized the supernatural nature of the golden fish. Ji Yuan nodded in agreement, but before he could speak, the man who had been fishing earlier arrived within range of the flying boat on a gentle breeze. His voice rang out before he even landed on the deck.

“Naturally, it is no ordinary fish. This fish is unique to the Mirror Ocean and is called the Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon.2 Although it is a creation of water element essence, it is incredibly strong and resilient. In the Mirror Ocean, its power is inexhaustible, making it extremely difficult to catch.”

Ji Yuan looked to the side. The man quickly cupped his hands and bowed to Ji Yuan.

“Master, you came prepared. I don’t know what kind of spiritual power you used, but you managed to fish this golden sturgeon out of the water in less than half an hour. Last year, I caught one and had it drag a small boat across the Mirror Ocean for nine days and ten nights before I finally won the battle and managed to drag it out of the water!”

As he spoke, the man looked closely at Ji Yuan’s fishing rod and noticed a strange core on the hook, from which a faint fragrance wafted.

“Oh, the Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon, what a lovely name!”

Wei Yuansheng nodded from the side, and squatted down to examine the several-foot-long fish closely. He noticed that although the fish was not moving at all, it was still opening and closing its mouth.

Ji Yuan frowned as he looked at the man in front of him. He had sword-like eyebrows, bright eyes, and a dignified appearance. He did not have a beard or a hair bun, and wore a slightly loose blue robe, which looked quite decent.

“Fellow Taoist, you can’t just call someone ‘Master’ so casually.”

“I understand. Of course, one cannot call someone ‘Master’ casually. Therefore, my words are in no way casual! I am Lu Min, a cultivator from Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion. I wish to become your disciple!”

The newcomer suppressed his smile and bowed deeply to Ji Yuan with great solemnity. However, this time Ji Yuan simply stepped aside and dodged him.

“Since you are a cultivator from Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion, can you truly accept someone as your master so casually without breaking any unspoken rules? Even if your sect agrees, I, Ji Yuan, still would not agree.”

The cultivator laughed happily.

“As you say. It’s not that I don’t want to become your disciple, but that you won’t accept me as your disciple. Don’t go back on your word!”

Ji Yuan was both annoyed and amused.

“Don’t worry, I won’t go back on my word!”

Lu Min visibly breathed a sigh of relief; it would be incredibly foolish for him to have gained another master just because of this.

“That’s good, that’s good. By the way, may I ask your esteemed name, fellow Taoist? Could you tell me how you caught it? This fish is a water spirit, capable of using the shimmering light of the Mirror Ocean as its spiritual technique. To catch it, you can only wait until it is exhausted and cannot further exhaust your strength.”

Ji Yuan bowed slightly.

“I am Ji Yuan, and I belong to no sect or school.”

Then Ji Yuan pointed to the fishing line.

“I used lightning energy to paralyze its body, preventing it from gathering the spiritual power of the Mirror Ocean water. Finally, I detonated the lightning energy within its body by snapping the rod, and then fished it up.”

“Lightning energy?”

The visitor paused for a moment.

“Lightning energy is useless against golden sturgeon; it will only provoke its ferocity. What kind of lightning technique did you use?”

Ji Yuan didn’t want to explain too much, so he just replied casually.

“You can believe it or not.”

“I believe you. You’re qualified to be my master when it comes to fishing, so I’ll naturally believe you. Hey, how about you teach me a little bit of that lightning technique, and I’ll tell you how to refine this Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon into Yin water in exchange?”

Ji Yuan was indeed somewhat interested in how to refine the golden-scaled fish, but he had no interest in teaching the lightning technique. Moreover, Ji Yuan had other ideas than refining the golden-scaled fish into Yin water essence.

Ji Yuan had been fishing for a while, attracting a lot of attention. Now, they all gathered around. Some even recognized the fish.

“I have heard that beneath the Mirror Ocean lies a kind of Yin Water essence, which transforms into a golden-scaled fish, an extremely rare creature.”

“That must be this fish, right?”

“Most likely!”

“There shouldn’t be any normal fish under the Mirror Ocean, right?”

“That’s not necessarily the case…”

While onlookers commented on the fish’s appearance, Ji Yuan reached out and grabbed the fish’s lips, lifting the large fish up and examining it closely. Then he asked the cultivator from the Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion a question.

“This fish was caught in the Mirror Ocean, and is considered property of Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion. Should I pay a certain fee for it?”

If someone were to catch a fish that looked extraordinary right there in someone else’s backyard, they should at least ask about it, instead of just taking it away.

“Oh, no need for that. Since you’ve been allowed to fish, there’s no reason to ask for payment after you catch something. Fellow Taoist, please consider my suggestion. I always keep my word and am extremely sincere!”

Ji Yuan nodded, but ignored Lu Min and instead smiled at Wei Yuansheng beside him.

“We’re in for a treat tonight.”

The smile on Wei Yuansheng’s face had only just appeared when he was immediately startled back by a loud shout from the side.

“What!! Eat it!? Fellow Taoist… no, Master! Can you take me with you?”

The first half of the sentence was one of utter astonishment, but the second half took a sharp turn, becoming gentle and full of anticipation.

“I’ve never seen or tasted this kind of Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon before. Can I have a taste?”

Ignoring the man’s intent, Ji Yuan picked up the fish and strode away with Wei Yuansheng, heading towards the entrance of the flying ship’s inner cabin.

Perhaps the greatest value of this fish lay in refining it into Yin water essence. However, upon seeing this fish, Ji Yuan was reminded of the water essence fish soup from years before. He still fondly remembered the taste of that fish soup. This one was so big; it would definitely be delicious braised or grilled.

Besides, Ji Yuan was now confident that he could cook the fish without having it disintegrate or get mushy, ensuring it retained its shape and didn’t turn into a bowl of soup.

Ji Yuan ignored everyone else, but they wouldn’t leave. This flying ship belonged to Xuanxin Mansion, and this territory belonged to Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion. He couldn’t very well drive them away.

It has to be said that Lu Min was indeed quite thick-skinned. He just stopped talking and smiled when Ji Yuan and Wei Yuansheng looked at him. But no matter how much Ji Yuan frowned or how much Wei Yuansheng looked at him with disdain, he just wouldn’t leave.

He followed them all the way to Yuhuai Mountain’s inn. Ji Yuan and Wei Yuansheng stepped inside, but when Lu Min, who was following behind, tried to go in, Wei Yuansheng, who had already entered, slammed the courtyard gate shut with a loud bang.

As soon as the gate closed, the courtyard’s array activated, leaving Lu Min stranded outside.

“Hey… Fellow Taoist Ji… young Fellow Taoist… Wait! Fellow Taoist Ji, I can become your disciple! Let me have a taste too!”

Lu Min knew himself too well. If he were to catch a golden-scaled fish, even if he could, he certainly wouldn’t be willing to eat it. But he wouldn’t feel bad about eating someone else’s fish, and would be full of anticipation.

琉璃 (liúli): colored glaze used in traditional Chinese architecture/glass object, 癸 (guǐ): 10th heavenly stem/ancient Chinese compass point, 水 (shuǐ): water/to swim –> 癸水 (guǐshuǐ) together means “menstruation,” but this actually refers to the monthly cycle, and thus to the Moon/Yin energy water. Then we have 金 (jīn): gold, 鳞 (lín): scales, 鲟 (xún): sturgeon.

Chapter 473: A Masterpiece of Culinary Skill

Lu Min wanted to eat fish, but he was turned away. After lingering outside the courtyard for a while, he realized that this was not a viable solution and decided to go find someone from Xuanxin Mansion.

In less time than it took to drink a cup of tea, Lu Min found one of the administrators of Xuanxin Mansion and asked him to go with him to Yuhuai Mountain’s inn. However, the administrator looked at him with a very strange expression.

“The inn where Mr. Ji is staying?”

“That’s right, the inn where Mr. Ji is staying. I would like to trouble Administrator Du to do me a favor and put in a good word for me. Tell him that I, Lu Min, am not an impolite person, and that everything before was a misunderstanding. Please allow me to try the dishes made with the golden-scaled fish. I will pay for it myself.”

Administrator Du grimaced.

“I can’t help you with that. Xuanxin Mansion only controls the realm-crossing ferry service to open a convenient route for fellow Taoists and passengers, but that doesn’t mean that fellow Taoists on the boat necessarily follow our orders. Since Mr. Ji has turned Fellow Taoist Lu away, don’t even think about eating fish. You can fish for another year or two, and maybe you’ll catch one.”

Lu Min scratched his head.

“It might not happen in a year or two. Even if it did, although I am an elder of the Mirror Ocean, I may not be able to make that decision. And even if I could, I wouldn’t be willing to eat it…”

Administrator Du smiled.

“Then I’m afraid I can’t help you. To tell you the truth, we would never offend Mr. Ji. Please leave.”

Lu Min was politely escorted out of the immortal cabin’s courtyard by the people of Xuanxin Mansion. After standing there dumbfounded for a while, he realized that he truly had no chance today. However, he still subconsciously returned to the courtyard of the inn where Ji Yuan and Yu Huaishan were staying.

As soon as he arrived, he could already smell the faint fragrance emanating from inside.

This inn was built as a standalone courtyard house, and naturally, it was fully equipped with all the necessary facilities, including a kitchen.

Ji Yuan was cooking inside the inn’s kitchen. The ingredients he prepared included the secret seasoning from Taoist Qingsong from Cloud Mountain Temple, as well as fresh jujube blossom honey from Ju’an Pavilion.

Ji Yuan first stir-fried the seasonings with oil, using his strong sense of smell to distinguish the subtle changes in the aroma of each ingredient, and then poured in all the chopped fish.

“Sizzle sizzle…”

A fragrant aroma rose up from the oil. The fish was about to melt into liquid when Ji Yuan immediately grabbed a small bottle from the table and poured in Ju’an Pavilion’s jujube blossom honey.

As soon as the honey was released, its sweet fragrance flowed over the fish, turning into a pale yellow glow that spread throughout the entire large pot. It gradually seeped into the melting fish, instantly making the fish appear distinct and separate.

“Sizzle sizzle…”

Ji Yuan smiled as he smelled the aroma. He hadn’t expected his cooking skills to improve so much. He then casually touched a dish of old-fashioned sauce on the table, spreading the sauce into a thin film that covered the entire pot. He stirred it a few times, then put the lid on.

After completing this step, Ji Yuan calculated that he only needed to wait another half hour before the fish was ready.

“Puff puff puff puff…”

Incessant simmering sounds came from inside the pot, and a unique aroma continued to waft through the air.

Ji Yuan glanced back and found that all the cultivators from Yuhuai Mountain were gathered at the kitchen door. Some of the younger disciples were swallowing their saliva. Even Ju Yuanzi’s eyes were filled with anticipation.

Ji Yuan’s smile grew even deeper. Sure enough, even those who cultivate immortality, those considered gods in the eyes of mortals, still had desires.

“Everyone, please don’t crowd around the door. Quickly prepare the tables and chairs. The fish will be ready in no time. Let’s try this golden-scaled fish made from the essence of Yin water and see what it tastes like.”

Without further ado, everyone immediately got to work. If there weren’t enough tables and chairs in the courtyard, they moved some from the house. They even took out various jade dishes, jade bowls, jade chopsticks, and other items from their own personal storage devices.

Because there was only one pot of fish, the people outside actually used spiritual techniques to conjure a large round table so that everyone could sit at it.

Half an hour later, Ji Yuan lifted the lid of the pot. The fish and meat in the pot were vibrant red, with a faint spicy aroma and a hint of sweetness. Just from the smell, Ji Yuan knew that this pot of fish had reached the peak of his culinary skills to date.

The golden sturgeon was not actually a real fish, or at least, not a real living creature. Although it resembled one, it was only in appearance. It did not have a complete set of internal organs, it did not eat, drink, excrete, or defecate, and it was purely formed from concentrated essence of Yin water. Therefore, after Ji Yuan removed the scales, the whole pot of fish meat could stay, and nothing needed to be thrown away.

As for these scales, Ji Yuan kept them for now.

Seeing that everyone outside was so eager, Ji Yuan quickly picked up a piece of fish with his fingers and put it in his mouth to taste. Instantly, a fresh, fragrant, sweet, and spicy flavor filled his mouth. It was so delicious that Ji Yuan almost swallowed his own tongue.

“Hahahaha… a skill close to the Tao, a skill close to the Tao!”

Laughing loudly a few times, Ji Yuan carried the pot out. This technique he had learned from Cloud Mountain Temple was quite convenient.

Although there was a spiritual array separating them, some of the fragrance from the inn courtyard still managed to escape. The fragrance wasn’t strong, but it was extremely unique, seemingly possessing a peculiar ability to make those who smelled it constantly salivate.

Lu Min waited outside for a long time, but no one came out to invite him. Instead, he swallowed his saliva countless times.

“Damn it! I have cultivated for over three hundred years, and this is the first time I’ve felt this agony. I can’t stay here any longer!”

Taking a deep breath of the aroma, Lu Min turned and ran, not daring to linger for even a moment longer.

Once Lu Min left, Ji Yuan, who was chewing a large piece of fish, couldn’t help but shake his head and smile. Ju Yuanzi next to him had just swallowed a mouthful of fish meat and skin and laughed.

“As a cultivator, this person is truly unique. I haven’t met anyone with such a thick skin in centuries.”

Ji Yuan looked at him.

“You must have spent more than 90% of those centuries cultivating in the mountains. The world is vast and full of wonders.”

Ju Yuanzi chuckled.

“Mr. Ji is right, I… Hey, wait a minute, don’t take all that big piece of fish skin by yourself, give me some!”

Faced with such delicious food, even the old immortal Ju Yuanzi couldn’t resist. Seeing that Guan He had actually torn off a large piece of fish skin, he couldn’t help but reach out with his chopsticks to intercept it, startling Guan He into quickly demonstrating his “respect for elders.”1

This pot of fish was delicious, both the meat and the bones were excellent, but the best part was the fish skin, which resembled a solidified layer of fat and was covered in the seasonings. It was best when it was wrapped around some fish meat as well. It was truly a delicacy that even gods would find irresistible.

This golden sturgeon wasn’t small, but there were still quite a few people eating it. Additionally, cultivators could go for long periods of time without eating and then eat large quantities at once, so the fish was no match for the onslaught of chopsticks. The whole pot of fish was quickly emptied.

Then Ji Yuan unleashed his ultimate weapon: a large pot of rice, topped with the remaining broth containing bits of fish.

Ji Yuan felt the meal was worthwhile. Not only did he witness the pinnacle of his culinary skills and taste the delicious golden sturgeon, but more importantly, when else could he witness this group of “immortals” frantically shoveling rice into their bowls, and even scrambling to get more?

“Phew…”

Yang Ming was the last to put down his rice bowl, letting out a long sigh of relief. He couldn’t even remember the last time he had eaten so heartily; it must have been more than two hundred years ago.

Everyone looked at each other, and after holding it in for a moment, they all burst into laughter.

“Hahahaha…”

“Hehehehe…”

“Interesting, interesting…”

Everyone felt that being with Ji Yuan often brought a unique sense of life, but it was not vulgar at all. Upon reflection, it even gave them a unique sense of enlightenment on their path of immortality.

…

The Mirror Ocean was indeed more beautiful at night than during the day. All the passengers returned to the deck once again to appreciate this rare sight.

Lu Min lay on a deck at the bow of the ship, sighing. Although he had just run away from Yuhuai Mountain’s inn, he could no longer forget the aroma he had smelled. He could only imagine in his mind what the fish behind that fragrance tasted like.

“Sigh…”

Just as he sighed again, Ji Yuan’s voice suddenly rang out from the side.

“Fellow Taoist Lu, don’t think about things that can’t be forced. As a token of my gratitude to Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion for allowing me to fish, I’ll give you this.”

Lu Min immediately straightened up and caught the item Ji Yuan threw to him with both hands. He opened his palms to find that it was a fruit pit, though he didn’t know what kind of fruit it was.

“It was precisely this bait that I used to lure the golden sturgeon. As for the lightning technique, you can forget about it.”

Lu Min quickly stood up and bowed to Ji Yuan, who was standing ten steps away.

“Thank you for the bait, fellow Taoist. I will certainly make good use of it! Uh, may I ask another question? The fish meat that’s was cooked smells so good, what did it taste like?”

Ji Yuan smiled.

“Fresh, fragrant, spicy, sweet, and perfectly seasoned—it was simply delicious.”

With that, Ji Yuan turned and left.

It would have been better if he hadn’t asked. Ji Yuan had answered, but Lu Min felt even more upset. He secretly decided that next time he caught a golden sturgeon, he would definitely cook it and try it!

With this thougght, he looked down at the fruit pit in his hand, sniffed it, and a faint spiritual fragrance wafted out. Lu Min frowned and carefully examined the fruit pit from top to bottom, scanning it inside and out with his spiritual energy, but found no trace of any binding spells.

‘So, this fragrance and its unique spiritual energy are condensed and preserved thanks to the fruit pit itself?’

Lu Min had seen many fruits imbued with spiritual energy, but after a while, their spiritual energy would dissipate, and the fruit would rot. This fruit pit had clearly been away from the tree for quite some time, yet it still possessed such rich and pure spiritual energy, and there was even a faint flow of heat circulating inside. Moreover, it had not been tampered with using any supernatural methods. Why was that?

Lu Min’s thoughts gradually shifted away from his craving for fish. He felt as if he had seen a similar description somewhere before. After a long while, he suddenly shuddered and blurted out a word.

“Spiritual root!”
