Chapter 474: Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing

The fruit pit in his hand suddenly seemed to grow heavy. Lu Min pinched the small, hard pit and carefully brought it closer to smell it. Sure enough, it couldn’t be described simply as spiritual energy.

‘Could this really be the pit of the Spirit Root Fruit?’

Although Ji Yuan was no longer in sight, Lu Min still glanced in the direction Ji Yuan had gone.

‘So Mr. Ji used this fruit pit to fish before?’

No wonder the notoriously picky Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon took the bait so quickly; if it were the pit of a Spirit Root Fruit, it would indeed attract them.

The current situation was enough to show that there was still some spiritual energy within the fruit pit.

“Use it for fishing? Don’t be ridiculous!”

Although Lu Min really wanted to ask Ji Yuan for details, he also felt that he might have left a bad first impression on the other party, so he dared not bother him too much.

Putting away the fruit pit, Lu Min took to the air and flew towards one of the crescent-shaped islands.

The pit of a spiritual root fruit and the seed of a spiritual root fruit were two different concepts. Spiritual root fruits were often unique, and even if they were to propagate, they couldn’t sprout from just any pit.

Even so, Lu Min still felt that the fruit pit in his hand was very precious. At the very least, it should be able to help him figure out what was so special about the original fruit. After all, spiritual root fruits were not merely entities that contained spiritual energy.

Lu Min looked back at the Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship in the night sky. The surrounding Mirror Ocean seemed to reflect the stars, and the flying ship shone with magical light, like the bright moon. It was quite beautiful.

“Ji Yuan… indeed, this generation of cultivators is full of hidden talents!”

…

For the passengers on Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship, the starry night view of the Mirror Ocean did not disappoint, but it was not breathtakingly stunning either.

The main reason was that due to one specific technique cast by a certain master on this ship, the passengers had already witnessed the spectacular sight of a river of stars falling. Although the beauty of the Mirror Ocean was magical and gave them the feeling of the starry sky above touching a river of stars below, it was still not as incredible as sailing directly through the galaxy.

This was the first time that the cultivators in charge of Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship had seen such calm passengers on such a night; even some of the mortals on the flying ship were able to remain relatively calm.

Aside from Lu Min, few cultivators from Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion appeared until the next day, when the boat resumed its journey and slowly ascended out of the Mirror Ocean and into the sky.

For Ji Yuan, Lu Min was just a minor episode in his cultivation and life. One would always encounter all sorts of passersby, but the Mirror Ocean was quite special.

For a while after the flying ship took off, Ji Yuan remained by the side of the ship, gazing down at the calm, glassy surface of the ocean below. The Mirror Ocean was not cut-off from the surrounding sea, aside from the crescent-shaped islands on both sides, but the two waters did not merge.

The clarity and ever-changing light of the Mirror Ocean could not be explained by a mere description of a mysterious natural phenomenon. Although there were array formations and barriers set up in the waters surrounding Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion, the Mirror Ocean itself, as the central location, had no trace of any barriers, which was inherently illogical.

Ji Yuan squinted at the ever-changing sea. The entire ocean surface was as calm as a mirror, not even ripples in the wind. Perhaps not even a real mirror could not be as smooth as the Mirror Ocean.

‘It’s probably not that the Mirror Ocean lacks barriers, but rather that the Mirror Ocean itself is an extremely rare and powerful barrier, and this Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon is merely the essence of Yin Water born within that restriction.’

If that were the case… Ji Yuan looked at the crescent-shaped islands on both sides of the Mirror Ocean, at the ocean pavilions with their shimmering lights, and then at the cliff face in the other direction. Although he could no longer see them, he still vividly remembered the inscriptions on the cliff that contained a strong sword intent.

“If that’s the case, then this Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion is probably quite extraordinary, and as for the barrier of the Mirror Ocean…”

Ji Yuan murmured, and said nothing more, but he firmly kept this thought in his mind.

…

As they crossed vast oceans, braving storms, harnessing the power of the Sun and riding the starlight and moonlight, Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship made four stops in total, starting from the Peak Crossing and approaching the Immortal Port, the destination where Ji Yuan and the others were preparing to disembark.

Besides Jingxuan Sea Pavilion, the other three were all immortal ports, each under the jurisdiction of various immortal forces. They stopped for only one day in each, with passengers boarding and disembarking at each one.

In general, although the various celestial ports were hidden from mortal view similarly to those regions hidden deep within the mists, they were much more lively than how old-timers like Ju Yuanzi remembered. Some markets had emerged, with many mortals engaging in business for their livelihoods, injecting considerable vitality into the markets within each port.

Starting in the middle of the fourth lunar month, the view beneath the flying ship was once again that of a continuous landmass, officially marking its entry into the Heng Region of the Northern Territories. By the end of the fourth lunar month, they had finally arrived at their destination: Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing.

On this bright and sunny day, the deck of the flying ship was crowded with people. Ordinary mortals were packed together, especially in the area where the gangplank was usually placed, while the areas occupied by immortal cultivators, spirits, monsters were relatively spacious.

Like other immortal ferry crossings, Ruan Mountain Crossing was surrounded by some illusion barriers, and was shrouded in mist year-round. However, from the inside, the mist was very thin, enough for mortals disembarking to clearly see the mountain path.

From a distance, Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing resembled a giant, flat-topped mountain. If it were named according to the characteristics of the region, it would definitely have been more appropriate to call it “Flat-Topped Ferry Crossing.” The mountain peak boasted dense buildings and a flow of people, making it seem even more lively than the previous immortal ferry crossings.

Unlike the previous ports, the buildings here stretched across the entire port, with even some taverns and treasure shops next to the mooring points.

While Ji Yuan and the Yuhuai Mountain group were still watching the sight, Administrator Du from Xuanxin Prefecture had already approached.

“Ruan Mountain Crossing is a ferry crossing under the jurisdiction of Nine Peaks Mountain. As the hosting immortal sect of this Cultivators’ Grand Assembly, it is natural for Nine Peaks Mountain’s Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing to be bustling. In fact, some independent cultivators and other spiritual beings have been gathering here for over ten years, just to wait for the grand gathering of immortals this summer.”

Ji Yuan nodded to indicate that he understood. Although the news of the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly theoretically circulated among the immortal cultivation sects, it would eventually spread out over time. It was not surprising that some well-informed cultivators would know about it now. However, not just anyone could enter the actual venue.

Even so, a thriving market environment had emerged on the outskirts. All beings had desires, regardless of whether they were supernatural, and there would always be curiosity and things they wanted to see and obtain. This ensured the existence of the market. Many cultivators and even spirits and monsters would travel great distances to try their luck, hoping to find what they wanted, or perhaps to encounter some good fortune or “immortal guidance.”

The flying ship was slowly descending. The people on the deck were discussing the sight with excitement or apprehension. There were other flying ships moored in the clouds, and even a small island suspended in the air, connected to the port by several long suspension bridges, a sight which amazed Ji Yuan.

After the ship came to a stop, several gangplanks floated up and connected in the air, eventually forming three bridges that were firmly anchored between the flying ship and the shore. Passengers who had been waiting for this moment began to disembark in an orderly manner, while many people in the port watched Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship from afar.

Ji Yuan and the group from Yuhuai Mountain disembarked a little later. Before they left, both administrators of Xuanxin Mansion were present to bow and bid them farewell.

“Mr. Ji, fellow Taoists of Yuhuai Mountain, we shall meet again. If any of you travel on this ferry from Xuanxin Mansion again within ten years, we will still be the ones in charge of the ship!”

The two administrators’ expressions were calm, but their sincerity was palpable. They were administrators of the flying ship, and had been in charge of this ferry for many yeras, so of course they were not eligible to participate in the Cultivator’s Grand Assembly.

Ji Yuan and the other cultivators from Yuhuai Mountain returned the bows.

“If we have the chance, we will definitely meet again!”

“Indeed, we who cultivate immortality seek the Tao and freedom. Though we dare not claim to truly be immortal, we are certainly worthy of witnessing the passage of time. We will surely meet again!”

“Take care, everyone!”

“Farewell!”

After exchanging goodbyes, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi took the lead and led the Yuhuai Mountain contingent off of the flying ship, onto the gangplank, and then into the Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing.

In the port area, some people watched Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship gleam in the sunlight. Some independent cultivators even set up a table with their friends in the port’s restaurants and other spaces to watch the immortal ships flying in from all directions. The passengers disembarked from the floating island, enjoying the spectacle and comparing their abilities.

“What kind of immortal sect owns this realm-crossing ferry? Its radiance is truly extraordinary.”

“Fellow Taoist, you may not know, but this is the Yin-Yang Flying Ship of Xuanxin Mansion. The sail is a huge Yin-Yang sail crafted by Xuanxin Mansion. It can gather the essence of the Sun and Moon and absorb the power of Yin and Yang. It is truly remarkable.”

“I see.”

“Hey, look, there are a few tall, earth-yellow monsters. They look like Earth Yellow Stone Spirits?”1

“Haha, how interesting, they’re imitating people by wrapping a few pieces of cloth around their lower bodies.”

Among the independent cultivators, there were indeed some highly skilled individuals, but the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly sometimes took into account an individual’s background. Social classes existed everywhere, even among immortals, demons, gods, and monsters. Unless one’s cultivation was high enough to break through the limitations, most of the independent cultivators can only watch the excitement.

“Gentlemen, have any experts disembarked?”

“There were some with obscure auras, but they all seemed to be independent cultivators. I didn’t see any cultivators from famous immortal sects disembark.”

“Ah, there! Over there, someone on the flying ship is bowing to them.”

Looking in the direction the cultivator who had just spoken was pointing, they saw the second gangplank of the flying ship, where Ji Yuan and the group were walking into the port.

“Are the first two mortals?”

“Haha, of course not. It’s just that our cultivation is too shallow to see the true nature of these experts. Fellow Taoist Zhao, you must be able to see a glimpse of their true nature… Fellow Taoist Zhao?”

As the most powerful independent cultivator present, Taoist Zhao who was known to be at the cusp of the Return to Origin Realm, also shook his head.

“Even I can’t discern anything about these two. If I met them on a mortal street, I would just think they were ordinary people with refined bearing.”

I’m pretty sure that in Chinese philosophy, yellow is the color of earth

Chapter 475: Encountering an Old Friend in a Foreign Land

At this moment, Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi, who were regarded as experts by onlookers, also had very complicated and subtle feelings.

Ji Yuan sensed that the day of the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly was finally approaching. He was fine on the Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship, but after disembarking, a sense of anticipation and excitement gradually intensified.

It was a prestigious gathering of cultivators, after all. Would he encounter a few True Immortals? Would he run into the author of “A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds”?1 How many wondrous and extraordinary techniques would he witness? He hoped to encounter everything he desired.2

As for the people of Yuhuai Mountain, their feelings were equally complicated. They had missed the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly several times, and in the past, the cultivators from their mountain sect had even caused trouble during the discussions of the Tao.

The immortals like Yang Ming, Qiu Feng, and other masters from Yuhuai Mountain were juniors in front of Ju Yuanzi, and had never participated in the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly before. Even they were filled with excitement and trepidation due to past experiences and unfamiliarity, not to mention how their disciples were feeling.

As for Ju Yuanzi, having lived for so many years, his cultivation was at the pinnacle of those of Yuhuai Mountain. His state of mind was completely different. Past incidents and the unknowns of the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly were nothing to him. His heart was completely serene. Ji Yuan’s calm was superficial; Ju Yuanzi was truly calm.

“Mr. Ji, let’s go and pay our respects to Nine Peaks3 Mountain first. They will arrange accommodation for us. Of course, if we are not satisfied, we can also find our own accommodation outside.”

Ji Yuan had no objection to Ju Yuanzi’s suggestion and nodded in agreement.

The group lingered on the market at Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing, then walked toward a very conspicuous building at the ferry crossing. A red banner floated on top of the building, with the words “Dengxian4 Pavilion” written on it.

The onlookers noticed Ji Yuan and his group walk toward the building, being greeted by cultivators from Nine Peaks Mountain at the entrance. They knew that these were people from true orthodox immortal sects, the kind who had invitations to the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly.

A moment later, a cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain dressed in a blue robe led Ji Yuan and the group from Yuhuai Mountain out of Dengxian Pavilion. With a wave of his sleeve, he released a huge willow leaf, which enlarged and transformed into the size of a small boat in front of them.

“Fellow Taoists, please board the Green Leaf Boat. Our Taoist temple of Nine Peaks Mountain covers ground both within and around Ruan Mountain. You may not be familiar with the route, so I will escort you to the mountain to rest.”

“Yes, thank you!”

Ju Yuanzi said politely and stepped onto the green leaf first, followed closely by Ji Yuan and the rest of the group.

The people from Nine Peaks Mountain mistook Ji Yuan for a cultivator of Yuhuai Mountain. Ji Yuan didn’t explain anything, to avoid them asking him for an invitation separately, which he didn’t have. He would have to find the flying ship administrator who invited him back then, so it was best to avoid trouble.

However, Ji Yuan had barely stepped onto the Green Leaf Boat when he heard someone calling him from afar.

“Mr. Ji… Mr. Ji… Is it Mr. Ji?”

The voice sounded familiar, yet strangely unfamiliar, which was quite peculiar. Ji Yuan’s hearing was incredibly sharp; he could remember almost any sound he heard.

He could even distinguish every dog’s bark from memory, let alone human voices. He had never encountered a sound that was both familiar and unfamiliar.

Ji Yuan frowned and turned to look in the direction of the voice. Suddenly, his eyes lit up. A man radiating spiritual energy was walking briskly towards him. He wore a pale yellow robe, a small hairpin grown holding his hair bun, and his expression was full of surprise.

The cultivators from Nine Peaks Mountain and the people from Yuhuai Mountain naturally stopped what they were doing at the sound of these calls and looked at the newcomer.

Soon, the man reached Ji Yuan, extended his arms in a gesture of respect, and bowed ninety degrees, giving Ji Yuan a deep greeting.

“Greetings, Mr. Ji! I never expected it to be you. My master said you definitely wouldn’t come here, hahaha, he’ll be so happy to see you!”

Hearing this, Ji Yuan’s heart stirred, but he did not show it on his face. He nodded slightly at the man who spoke but did not say anything.

The man smiled, put straightened from his bow, and spoke.

“Sir, you probably don’t recognize me anymore, do you?”

“Haha!”

Ji Yuan smiled mysteriously. He indeed was reminded of someone. In this whole world, excluding the use of supernatural techniques, there was probably only one person whose voice he could recognize but not remember. And the person in front of him clearly did not use any supernatural techniques.

“Your Majesty has certainly acquired a fine new appearance!”

The middle-aged man was slightly taken aback, then his smile widened.

“As expected of Mr. Ji, you recognized me without me even having to prompt you. I, Yang Zong, am truly impressed! However, please don’t call me “Your Majesty” again, you might dare to call me that but I do not dare to accept such a title!”

Ji Yuan felt inexplicably relieved at seeing Yang Zong as a middle-aged man. He was truly afraid that a little kid wearing a bib would pop out, which he, Ji Yuan, would find hard to accept.5

Shaking off the amusing thought in his mind, Ji Yuan glanced around. Since Yang Zong was here, the old beggar shouldn’t be too far away, but he didn’t see him. The other man probably hadn’t seen him either, otherwise he would have definitely come over.

“Where is your master?”

“Master is at Nine Peaks Mountain. My senior brother and I were just strolling around the market at Ruan Mountain Crossing when we unexpectedly ran into you, sir. I, Yang Zong, will never forget your great kindness. Without you and my master, I would not be here today!”

This old emperor had indeed undergone a complete transformation. He had gained profound enlightenment in the face of life and death, and his personality was now completely different from before. In addition, he had been reborn with a lotus root body, so it was no wonder that Ji Yuan’s ears couldn’t distinguish the sound of his voice.

At the thought that the other party had reconstructed his physical body using lotus root, Ji Yuan couldn’t help but examine Yang Zong from top to bottom, wondering with a wicked sense of humor whether he often lost his hands and feet.6

“Uh, Mr. Ji, and who is this?”

Ju Yuanzi didn’t know who the man in front of him was, or why Mr. Ji called him “Your Majesty”.

Ji Yuan smiled and waved to Yang Zong, whose appearance was now completely different than it had been in the past.

“Fellow Taoist Ju, this man is none other than the former Emperor of Dazhen, Emperor Yuande, Yang Zong.”

“Hmm? Emperor Yuande?”

“Emperor Yuande!”

“What!?”

“Didn’t Emperor Yuande pass away?”

Ju Yuanzi and the others from Yuhuai Mountain exclaimed in astonishment. Meanwhile, the cultivators from Nine Peaks Mountain nearby remained calm and unhurried, simply observing the scene before them intently.

Yang Zong quickly bowed to the crowd.

“Yang Zong greets all the esteemed elders. You are all right, Emperor Yuande has passed away. Now, I am merely Yang Zong!”

After his initial surprise, Ju Yuanzi’s eyes suddenly flashed, and a possibility suddenly occurred to him: the physical body could be reshaped, just as the universe could be recreated.

‘What a grand act!’

“Junior brother… junior brother… why were you running so fast? I can’t keep up with you!”

A voice familiar to Ji Yuan came from behind. A boy who looked to be about the same age as Wei Yuansheng came running up in a hurry, clutching a steaming lotus leaf bundle in his hand.

He had grown taller and his clothes had grown neater, but when Ji Yuan saw him, he knew he was still the same little beggar from back then.

“Junior brother, if you run off again, I won’t bring you along next time…”

The boy stopped halfway through his sentence, swallowed the food in his mouth with a “gulp,” and stared wide-eyed at Ji Yuan.

“Xiaoyou, don’t you recognize me?”

“Mr. Ji! You’ve come to the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly too? That’s wonderful! Now my junior brother and I have another acquaintance!”

As he spoke, Lu Xiaoyou handed the lotus leaf package in his hand to Ji Yuan.

“Sir, please try these hawthorn glutinous rice balls, fresh out of the pot!”

Ji Yuan didn’t stand on ceremony. He smiled, reached out and picked up a rice ball, then put it in his mouth and chewed it.

“Fellow Taoists, I think it would be best if we settled in at Nine Peaks Mountain, and then you could catch up later.”

The cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain finally spoke up. It was quite common to encounter acquaintances during the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly. Many cultivators were long-lived, and it was not uncommon for old friends to meet again after decades or even centuries.

“Mr. Ji, you go first. My senior brother and I will go back to find our master and tell him that you have arrived.”

Ji Yuan nodded and stepped onto the Green Leaf Boat. Then, the cultivators of Nine Peaks Mountain used spiritual energy to control the boat and ascend into the sky. Although it was moving forward, its altitude was constantly increasing.

Ji Yuan turned to look in the direction the flying boat was heading. With his divine eyes wide open, he could vaguely see nine enormous mountain peaks suspended in the clouds and deep mist.

Below, at Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing, Lu Xiaoyou and Yang Zong maintained their respectful bows in farewell until they could no longer see Ji Yuan. Then they finally withdrew and straightened.

“Master will be very happy to know… right?”

“Yes, or rather, it’s very complicated…”

“Sigh!”

These two fellow disciples had gotten to know each other quite well over the years, and they couldn’t help but sigh. Of course, overall, their master would definitely be happier.

The Green Leaf Boat was quite fast, and in a short while it had already moved far away from Ruan Mountain Crossing. After passing through the hazy, misty clouds, Ji Yuan and the others beheld the true gate of Nine Peaks Mountain.

Nine enormous mountain peaks stood in a row, varying in height but roughly the same size. They were surrounded by green hills and clear waters, yet they seemed to float above the clouds. Wispy white clouds still gathered around their summits. Some peaks were even illuminated by rainbows, and birds flew as immortals roamed freely. This was clearly the abode of immortals.7

“Fellow Taoists, this is the gate of our Nine Peaks Mountain sect. The nine peaks stand together, visible in the clouds, both present and illusory. Each of you will receive a token upon arrival. Without the token, you cannot enter the mountain gate, so please be careful not to lose it.”

This cultivator had always been impeccably polite, but at this moment, he could not hide the pride he felt as a disciple of Nine Peaks Mountain.

The Nine Peaks Sacred Land possessed endless wondrous methods, and was also the hosting venue for the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly, a truly prestigious event. Cultivators had weak desires and could not be swayed by worldly wealth and status, but this prestige was hard-won, and anyone would feel immensely proud.

This was also Ju Yuanzi’s first time visiting Nine Peaks Mountain, and it was the first time that Nine Peaks Mountain had hosted the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly.

Swish! Swish! Swish!

A series of streaks of light flashed by, coming from several young cultivators who were flying on their swords, accompanied by laughter and cheerful chatter as they went.

“Hahahaha…I’m the fastest!”

“Your sword-flying is as slow as a tortoise’s!”

“I’ll catch you!”

“Don’t run!”

As their voices faded into the distance, the beams of sword light sometimes moved straight and sometimes spiraled, leaving behind tangled trails that showcased the wondrous power of sword control. Just watching the agile sword-riding techniques, Ji Yuan felt ashamed of his own inferiority.

‘These are all wondrous and sacred sights; this trip was definitely worthwhile!’

Chapter 476: Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern

Although Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi knew from their previous discussion that some powerful figures might not be particularly interested in the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly, it was undeniable that the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly was a true grand gathering of immortals.

Various immortal abodes, sacred lands, and sacred caverns had arrived. Some came early, some came late, and some, like Yuhuai Mountain, arrived precisely on time. In addition, there were also scattered independent cultivators, spirits, monsters, and other cultivators who came from all over the world. As a result, the area around Nine Peaks Mountain was quite densely populated with cultivators.

Ji Yuan had heard a joke in his past life that if you tossed a coin into the air in the capital and missed catching it, you had a high chance of hitting a low-ranking official. Now, it would be that there was a higher chance of hitting an immortal in and around Ruan Mountain, and perhaps even the entire country where Ruan Mountain was located, than anywhere else.

Ji Yuan smiled knowingly as he watched the cultivators chasing after each other on their swords. Wei Yuansheng, Shang Yiyi, and other young disciples of Yuhuai Mountain were also eager to try, but unfortunately, Wei Yuansheng’s cultivation was still shallow, and he only knew the basics of various sword techniques. He didn’t know any sword-flight techniques. Guan He, Shang Yiyi, and the others could barely fly, but that was all they could do.

“I really want to do that too!”

Wei Yuansheng muttered, then quickly stopepd when he saw Qiu Feng looking at him.

Seeing Ji Yuan watching the young juniors flying after each other on their swords, Ju Yuanzi smiled and asked.

“Does the master think those young fellow Taoists are exceptionally talented?”

Different perspectives lead to different understandings. Ju Yuanzi was considering the aptitude and innate talent of the young cultivators who were passing by.

Hearing this, Ji Yuan shook his head. His smile remaining unchanged as he replied.

“I’m not judging them by their inherent talent, but rather by the enjoyment they find in it. We cultivators seek the Tao, to put it bluntly, to experience the wonders of enlightenment and to live freely and unrestrained. If we only sought longevity, how boring would it be to live a life of emptiness!”

Ju Yuanzi stroked his beard and gazed thoughtfully in the direction those cultivators had flown. He wholeheartedly agreed with the so-called wonders of enlightenment and freedom. Years of cultivating in the mountains had allowed him to experience the wonders of enlightenment, surpassing most worldly pleasures, and true freedom was the desire of every immortal cultivator.

“Sir, you truly are worthy of the title.”

These two simple statements from Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi, if they had been spoken between junior cultivators like Wei Yuansheng, might have been interpreted by their elders as excuses for their playfulness and neglect of proper cultivation. However, when uttered by Ji Yuan and Ju Yuanzi, they carried a profound meaning to all the cultivators present.

This was not only because of the two individuals’ identities, but also because the subtle, logical reasoning in their words. Even if it was largely psychological, it made people think more deeply.

Even though the cultivator of Nine Peaks Mountain was focused on controlling the Green Leaf Boat, he also kept an eye on the movements of the two “old immortals” behind him. As the hosting cultivator, it was highly beneficial for him to hear a few profound words from these masters. According to the experience of some fellow disciples, profound truths were often hidden in simple details.

“Fellow Taoists, please concentrate and clear your minds. Ahead lies the restricted area of ​​Nine Peaks Mountain’s Void Wheel Light Array, the location of the illusory barrier. Passing through it will feel like crossing to a different realm. Beyond that lies the gate of Nine Peaks Mountain.”

As he spoke, the cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain made hand seals and tapped repeatedly in the void in front of him. A burst of spiritual light rose from the Green Leaf Boat, carrying everyone diagonally upwards. The light of the boat gradually became ethereal and blurred, merging with the illusory light and color surrounding Nine Peaks Mountain, and then flew into it.

What had previously appeared before them as nine towering, colossal mountain peaks was now obscured by a hazy mist, with only shifting light and swirling gray fog, creating an empty landscape devoid of sky and earth.

After about four or five breaths, their vision cleared. The surrounding light and color seemed to have been filtered, becoming clearer and sharper, and the spiritual energy grew more abundant.

The nine enormous peaks of Nine Peaks Mountain came into view, far larger than they had been before. Ji Yuan and his group on the Green Leaf Boat were only at the outer edge of the peaks, which seemed to stretch to the heavens and down to the earth, immeasurably vast.

Ji Yuan opened his supernatural eyes wide and cast his gaze downwards.

Now, the colossal peaks of Nine Peaks Mountain no longer seemed to float mid-air above the clouds, but rather seemed to descent down below, clearly rooted and grounded. he could even make out smaller peaks below the clouds. Although these seemed smaller in comparison to the nine giant peaks, the might in fact be precipitous cliffs as well.

“Isn’t this Ruan Mountain?”

The cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain turned around and nodded to Ji Yuan.

“To reply to Immortal Master Ji, this place is now the territory of our Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern. It is no longer in the mortal world, so it is naturally not Ruan Mountain!”

This sentence was said with pride. To live a carefree life beyond the mortal world, to establish a sacred cavern and a sacred land of cultivation in one’s own domain… what a magnificent achievement on the immortal path! How many immortal abodes and sects in the world had such a foundation?

Ji Yuan was truly shocked. This was the first time he had entered a real sacred cavern realm. Even without mentioning the magnificence of the nine giant peaks, the mountains below the clouds and the implications of what could lay beyond them gave him much to ponder.

“Fellow Taoists, let’s head to Xianlai Peak now.”

With a sudden burst of speed, the Green Leaf Boat carried everyone toward the nearest mountain peak. The surroundings were serene and tranquil, with swans soaring overhead in formation and the green leaf clearing a path through the clouds.

Although Xianlai Peak appeared to be steep and precipitous, its massive size actually made it a place with gentle slopes suitable for building habitations. The Green Leaf Boat landed on the eastern side of the mountain, where several elegant pavilions had been constructed atop a protruding rocky promontory. Similar architectural complexes could be found throughout Xianlai Peak.

As the Green Leaf Boat landed, the people on board disembarked one after another. The cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain stepped forward and welcomed them inside.

“This is the Jade Bamboo Garden, built with spiritual bamboo. It was built on a large boulder, facing east, welcoming the morning light at dawn without obstructing the stars and moon at night. Are you satisfied, fellow Taoists of Yuhuai Mountain? If not, we can find another place.”

“No need, this is fine. It’s just a place to stay, no need to be picky.”

Upon hearing Ju Yuanzi’s words, the cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain bowed slightly.

“Thank you all for your understanding, fellow Taoists. Please rest for a while. I have already sent a message to the sect, and other fellow disciples will bring you your tokens and registration. I still need to return to the Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing to stand guard, so I won’t disturb you any longer!”

“Alright, fellow Taoist, farewell!”

“Please go ahead, Fellow Taoist.”

Ji Yuan suddenly asked a question.

“Are we limited to staying in Jade Bamboo Garden or Xianlai Peak?”

The cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain laughed.

“Senior Ji is too considerate. As long as you do not do anything disrespectful to Nine Peaks Mountain, you can go anywhere. If there are any restricted areas, there will be barrier arrays to prevent you from going there. I know that you fellow Taoists have profound cultivation, but please do not trespass if you encounter any barriers.”

“Of course!”

Ji Yuan was satisfied with the answer, and the cultivator from Nine Peaks Mountain did not linger any longer. He bid them farewell and flew away on the green leaf.

Not long after, several other cultivators from Nine Peaks Mountain, who were in charge of guest affairs, came and asked the group from Yuhuai Mountain to leave their names on the register. They also presented them with tokens and informed them that in addition to being able to fly out of the Nine Peaks Mountain gate on their own, they could also head to the locations guarded by several of the host cultivators on Xianlai Peak and ask them to for an escort out of Nine Peaks Mountain to Ruan Mountain Crossing, or to any other suitable place. This was clearly intended to provide a convenience for those cultivators who were not proficient in flying techniques.

Once everything was settled, only Ji Yuan and the Yuhuai Mountain contingent remained at Jade Bamboo Garden. They each took turns choosing their own pavilions and rooms and wandered around the area.

It had to be said that Nine Peaks Mountain was indeed superior to Yuhuai Mountain, at least in terms of its atmosphere of grandeur.

After tidying up his meditation pavilion, Ji Yuan walked to the end of the rocky promontory on the cliffside and looked down at the distant, misty landscape.

‘I wonder if anyone lives and thrives within this Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern, or if it’s some kind of small world?’

Just then, a hearty laugh echoed from the sky.

“Where under the heavens can you not run into people you know?1 I never expected someone as easygoing as you, Mr. Ji, to join in the fun of the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly, hahahahaha…”

Ji Yuan turned his head and saw someone arriving on white clouds. It was Lu Niansheng, the old beggar, who was still wearing his tattered clothes. Lu Xiaoyou and Yang Zong were not with him.

“Then you were mistaken, Mr. Lu. I enjoy joining in on fun more than you might assume. Even when ordinary people get married and hold a banquet, I would still go and get a cup of wine and a taste of the celebration. When I heard about such a lively place as the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly, how could I not come?”

As they spoke, the old beggar landed beside Ji Yuan. The two bowed to each other in greeting, and the former also bowed to the Yuhuai Mountain cultivators, who were watching from afar, in a gesture of greeting.

With greetings complete, the old beggar turned back to face Ji Yuan, a smile on his face.

“Mr. Ji, you’ve met Yang Zong, haven’t you? What did you think of him?”

Ji Yuan answered very sincerely.

“A complete transformation, truly extraordinary!”

“Haha, thank you for the compliment, sir!”

Ji Yuan knew the old beggar’s temperament and his current thoughts very well, so he added a heartfelt sentence with great seriousness.

“This is not flattery, but the truth. This method is truly miraculous, deserving of the title of ‘recreating the universe`!”

Ever since Yang Zong had been reborn, the old beggar had been eagerly looking forward to this day. It seemed that only a word of praise from Ji Yuan could make his life complete. Now that Ji Yuan had scratched his itch, he felt extremely pleased.

“Hahahaha… Sir, you flatter me, you flatter me!”

Even when flattered, one must be discerning. Even had it been a prince or nobleman praising him to his face, the old beggar would still scoff. But Ji Yuan’s two sentences were very pleasing to the ear, making the old beggar feel even happier than when he had arrived.

“By the way, since you enjoy watching a good show, sir, why don’t you come with me to a certain place? I was originally planning to go by myself, but since you’re here, we can keep each other company. Let’s go!”

“What’s this good show?”

Ji Yuan asked in confusion, but the old beggar had already taken off on a cloud, so Ji Yuan had no choice but to follow suit.

“Hahahaha… What other kind of excitement could there be at the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly? Of course, it’s immortals arguing — no, immortals battling! Fellow Taoists of Yuhuai Mountain, Mr. Ji and I are going out for a stroll. You can all cultivate here. See you later…”

Before he could finish speaking, the white cloud had already vanished in a flash of light, leaving only a long trail of sound echoing through the mountains.

Ju Yuanzi stood on his meditation pavilion, looking at the disappearing light and shaking his head. This old beggar had profound cultivation, but apart from Ji Yuan, no one else seemed to exist in his eyes.

Chapter 477: Immortals Discuss the Tao

Since they hadn’t invited anyone from Yuhuai Mountain, Ju Yuanzi and the others didn’t bother to shamelessly join them. They simply watched the disappearing light vanish into the distance.

Above the white clouds, Ji Yuan stood with the ruddy-faced old beggar. Coincidentally, the old beggar was more familiar with Nine Peaks Mountain than the people of Yuhuai Mountain, and Ji Yuan had some questions he wanted to ask him.

“Mr. Lu, you are quite familiar with Nine Peaks Mountain, aren’t you?”

The old beggar looked at the magnificent mountains around him and answered with a chuckle.

“I’m not exactly familiar with the sect, at most we know each other. But of course I’m definitely more familiar with Nine Peaks Mountain than those guys from Yuhuai Mountain.”

“Oh.”

Ji Yuan nodded, his gaze sweeping towards the mountain path beneath the distant clouds and mist.

“How large is this Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern? Are there any mortals living within it?”

“I don’t know exactly how big it is, but of course there are some mortals. Not only are there mortals in Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern, but there are also mortal kingdoms, and more than one. Of course, since it is an immortal sacred cavern, there are no demons or monsters; at most, there are some spirits and deities that Nine Peaks Mountain has permitted to reside here.”1

Ji Yuan let out another long “Oh”.

“So, this Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern is quite harmonious?”

Although the word “harmonious” had somewhat obscure connotations, its literal meaning was quite obvious. Hearing Ji Yuan’s words, the old beggar scoffed.

“Then you must be mistaken, sir. It’s not harmonious at all. Mortal kingdoms fight each other, and there are even ancestral feuds. Years ago, Nine Peaks Mountain would try to intervene and help form a unified dynasty, but what was the result?”

When the old beggar asked that question, Ji Yuan subconsciously replied.

“A complete collapse?”

“Indeed, the world was in chaos, dynasties were falling apart, and before the split, there were countless calamities. In the end, a situation was formed where several countries confronted each other in conflict, which was actually better than how things were before. Nine Peaks Mountain tried several times and found that unless they kept themselves in check, history would eventually come to the same end, and it would also damage the Tao heart and vitality of some immortal cultivators. So they simply stopped caring.”

Although he had guessed this possibility, the old beggar’s words still made Ji Yuan think deeply.

“Mr. Ji, don’t think too much about it. Nine Peaks Mountain will take care of its own affairs in the Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern. Let’s go watch the show.”

As soon as he finished speaking, the old beggar cast a technique to speed up his flight. The white clouds beneath his feet almost turned into a streak of white light, instantly carrying him and Ji Yuan away. They headed directly towards the Void Wheel Light Array of Nine Peaks Mountain. Clearly, this so-called “show” was outside the sacred cavern.

The light ahead shifted, and the old beggar’s flight did not stop. Ji Yuan’s right hand, hidden in his sleeve, had already gripped the token given to him by Nine Peaks Mountain, while the old beggar did not seem to produce any spiritual artifacts or tokens. He directly rode the cloud and crashed into the great formation.

Just like when they arrived, the light changed and the fog swirled thick. After a few breaths of alternating between reality and illusion, the white cloud carried Ji Yuan and the old beggar out of the Nine Peaks Sacred Cavern and flew against the wind into the high sky of the outside world.

Ji Yuan subconsciously glanced back. Sure enough, he could still see the nine enormous mountain peaks standing in the clouds. However, he now understood that without the right method, even if ordinary people could fly to this high altitude, they would not be able to touch the realm of Nine Peaks Mountain and would only encounter a mirage-like scene.

“Sir, hold on tight, we’ll be there in two or three days!”

“It’s that far?”

Ji Yuan was stunned for a moment. Two or three days? You old beggar, why didn’t you make that clear beforehand? How far would it be if it took several days to fly there?

“Where exactly is it? The Cultivators’ Grand Assembly is about to begin at the height of summer. We won’t be too late, will we?”

The old beggar shook his head.

“No, no, it’s only the end of the fourth lunar month. Besides, Mr. Ji, you rarely go out and don’t know how some things might happen. Do you really think the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly hasn’t started yet?”

Hearing the old beggar’s words, Ji Yuan realized what was going on, and nodded.

“The gathering of immortal cultivators is for the people, not for the sake of a meeting; it’s for discussing the Tao, not for deliberating on politics. So it must have already begun… In that case, are you and I heading to the ‘venue’?”

“Hahahaha…Brilliant, brilliant, Mr. Ji, your use of the word ‘venue’ is perfectly appropriate! Indeed, you and I are heading to the ‘venue,’ hahahaha…”

Perhaps because he was in a great mood, the old beggar’s hearty laughter carried far and wide. Many cultivators in the surrounding air could hear it, and even some sharp-eared people in the Ruan Mountain Ferry Crossing could vaguely hear it. They all figured that a master was probably passing by.

…

In a desolate coastal mountain range, there stood a steep, flat-topped peak several hundred feet high. No matter how experienced or agile the mountain climber, they could not ascend it. Even martial arts masters with high skills would find it extremely difficult to climb.

On this flat-topped peak, two groups of people sat on the ground.

On one side were a dozen or so cultivators dressed in a variety of colors and appearing rather casual. Their appearances revealed a mix of people of all ages and genders.

On the other side were a dozen or so cultivators, all dressed in white robes and wearing gold-trimmed filigree hairpin crowns. They, too, consisted of people of all genders and ages.

“It’s agreed: first, no damaging the mountain’s terrain; second, no swearing; third, we shall only reason with each other, and avoid resorting to violence.”

A cultivator from the casually dressed group spoke up, and people on both sides nodded in agreement.

“That’s only natural!”

“That’s right. We’ve agreed on three rules beforehand: we will only discuss the Tao.”

“Yes, yes, yes, a calm and peaceful mind is the key to cultivating one’s mental state for us cultivators!”

The cultivators on both sides either nodded or smiled, their expressions as gentle as the refreshing mountain breeze around them.

“In that case, shall we begin?”

“Well, as the saying goes, there’s no time like the present. Waiting until the height of summer is too long. Today is just right.”

“Exactly! Exactly!”

The elders on both sides stroked their beards in agreement, and then respectfully invited each other to begin.

“Fellow Taoist, please go first?”

“Ah, Fellow Taoist, please go first!”

“Fellow Taoist, please go first. We cultivators of my Great Wind Valley are more patient. We’ll listen first.”

“Fellow Taoist, you are mistaken. My Qianyuan Sect is most skilled in cultivating our inner strength and mind, and our patience is stronger. Fellow Taoist, please go first!”

The elders on both sides stroked their beards, exchanging gentle smiles, and remained silent for a while.

“I……”

“Then…”

The two spoke almost simultaneously, only to interrupt each other’s words, so they gestured to each other once again.

“Fellow Daoist, please!”

“Please, fellow Daoist, go ahead!”2

“Hohohoho…”

“Hahahaha……”

The cultivator of the Qianyuan Sect no longer stood on ceremony. He simply spread his hands and cast a technique to conjure a pattern of light and color, depicting swirling clouds, torrential thunder and rain, gentle breezes, and biting cold winds…

“In the past sixty years, the Taoists of Great Wind Valley all said that the wind is unpredictable and must be controlled by imitating the seasons and the changing weather. One must use force to attack the heavens and earth, and use gentleness to soothe all things. Do you claim that anything other than this method is a heretical path?”

The people from Great Wind Valley nodded.

“Indeed, that’s correct. But your fellow Taoists from the Qianyuan Sect believe that the wind has no constant force and water has no constant shape. The correct path is to control the wind with the mind and the law. Otherwise, don’t you claim that it cannot be called controlling the wind at all?”

“Indeed!”

The cultivators on both sides exchanged smiles.

“Unfortunately, my Qianyuan Sect has investigated the theory according to the ideas of you fellow Taoists, but the outcome was not as successful as our Qianyuan Sect’s method of control!”

“Haha, what a coincidence! My Great Wind Valley has also verified the methods of you fellow Taoists, and it’s utter nonsense!”

The elder from the Qianyuan Sect twitched his lips and took a deep breath.

“Fellow Taoist, isn’t the term ‘nonsense’ too harsh?”

“Oh, indeed it is. Then let me put it another way: how about ‘useless’?”

…

Although cultivators were often quite carefree, when disputes arose over the Tao, especially during a friction between two different lineages, they would argue about even the smallest things until they were cleared up and a winner was determined. If arguing were useless, they might have to resort to using their own spiritual techniques to prove their true strength.

Before Ji Yuan and the old beggar had even approached the southeastern coast, the old beggar’s beam of light had to slow down, because a strong gale and lightning were already brewing.

“Whoosh…whoosh…whoosh…”

“Boom…”

Leaden clouds rolled across the sky, obscuring the sunlight, while lightning rippled through the endless dark clouds.

The raging wind kept changing direction. Ji Yuan even saw a piece of dead wood, as thick as a thigh, being swept high into the sky one moment, then swept far away the next, and then blown back a moment later. It was more thrilling than flying a kite.

“Boom…”

A bolt of lightning struck, nearly hitting Ji Yuan and the old beggar. The old beggar remained motionless on his cloud, waving his hand to distort all the surrounding lightning in other directions.

“Mr. Ji, it seems the ‘discussion’ phase is almost over. Alas, we are too late. The most interesting part was this ‘discussion’…”

Seeing the old beggar’s mischievous expression, Ji Yuan couldn’t help but laugh and point to the sky.

“Watch out for lightning strikes!”

“Crack… Boom…”

“Boom…”

“Boom…”

Before the words had even finished leaving his mouth, several bolts of lightning struck down with a clear purpose towards the white cloud where the two were standing — or rather, towards the old beggar.

“Huh!?”

The lightning was too fast and too strange. Without any warning, the old beggar was caught off guard and had no time to dodge. He could only hurriedly gather his spiritual and reach out to hold back the sky.

“Crack…”

“Boom…”

“Whoosh…”

Bolts of lightning bathed the old beggar, making him appear as if he were bathed in light. Ji Yuan, standing nearby, felt a jolt of fear and quickly raised his sleeve, absorbing all the lightning into it.

The old beggar floated on the cloud for a long time without saying a word, then turned to look at Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, do you have some issues with this old beggar?”

“Uh, if I said it was an accident, would you believe me?”

The old beggar looked at Ji Yuan very seriously, while the latter, unusually, showed a hint of embarrassment and spread his hands.

“It really was an accident. I had suffered some lightning injuries before, and then used that opportunity to practice my lightning techniques. Perhaps something has changed.”

The old beggar’s lips twitched. ‘Ji Yuan, you’re here to show off, aren’t you?’

Chapter 478: The Host Suffers the Most

Even though the old beggar always secretly competed with Ji Yuan, and was indeed struck by lightning because of Ji Yuan’s words, he still trusted Ji Yuan completely. With his level of cultivation, he could naturally see that Ji Yuan was a truly sincere seeker of the Tao and would not use any tricks to harm him. Besides, with his own cultivation level, he wouldn’t be affected by a few ordinary lightning strikes.

So the old beggar felt that Ji Yuan truly did not mean it, but this unintentionality was still very unsettling. It wasn’t that he was upset about being “ambushed,” but rather that Ji Yuan hadn’t actually cast a technique when he pointed to the sky.

What did this mean? It meant that a mere thought could trigger celestial phenomena. This wasn’t simply a matter of spoken word becoming law. After all, this heavenly lightning had not been cast by Ji Yuan. At least the old beggar himself admitted that he could not do that, which made him very upset.

Therefore, the old beggar changed the subject.

“Mr. Ji, were you previously injured by lightning? What caused the injury? Did you encounter some kind of enemy?”

Ji Yuan’s background was mysterious. Over the years that the old beggar had been reshaping Yang Zong’s body, he would occasionally disguise himself and inquire about Ji Yuan under a fake name, but without exception, no one had ever heard of him. Instead, they would ask the old beggar who this master was. If he was familiar with the ones asking, the old beggar would talk about his experiences in Dazhen. But he would not mention the the other adventures much.

Now, hearing what Ji Yuan said, he became curious again.

“I didn’t encounter any enemies, but I had some insights from discussing the Tao with someone. The results of my refining attracted a lightning tribulation. I didn’t want my hard work to be destroyed, so I intervened in the tribulation and was injured. I only recently recovered.”

Ji Yuan didn’t explicitly say that he created the “Wonderful Method of Heaven and Earth”. His relationship with the old beggar was alright, but nowhere near as close as his relationship with the Old Dragon. Strictly speaking, he had only had a few interactions with the old beggar, so of course he wouldn’t reveal everything.

However, it was hard for the old beggar not to overthink Ji Yuan’s words.

Having gained insights from the Tao, the items he refined attracted a heavenly tribulation? And this heavenly tribulation even damaged Ji Yuan’s physical body?

The old beggar felt that he could not continue with this topic, at least not for the time being.

“Mr. Ji, are you alright now?”

“Well, this was actually a blessing in disguise. I’ve filled in some gaps in my shallow understanding of lightning techniques.”

“Well, that’s good. The dark clouds are covering the sky and the wind and thunder are raging below. It must be that the two sides had a misunderstanding and are now fighting. Let’s hurry over so we don’t miss the rest of the show.”

The old beggar changed the subject, channeling his spiritual power to propel the white clouds beneath his feet, increasing his speed once again as he descended amidst the storm and lightning.

Ji Yuan didn’t say anything more, but used his supernatural eyes to observe the wind and lightning at this moment. Just like that withered tree that had previously been blown about in the gale, the wind and thunder at this moment were clearly controlled by two sources.

As the saying went, “when soldiers come, generals will block them; when water floods, earth will dam it.”1 Even the clash between water and fire was a common occurrence in spiritual battles. This was the first time Ji Yuan had ever seen a competition to control the wind, which explained the strange gusts.

“Whoosh…whoosh…”

The coastal mountains could already be seen in the distance. The old beggar pointed in that direction and spoke.

“The place those guys originally planned to stay at is over there, but it might not be there anymore. We need to find wind in the right direction to go there. Where the wind is strongest and most chaotic, that’s where we’ll be sure to find them.”

As the white clouds flew over the cliff, they saw towering waves rising.

“Crash…bang…”

The giant wave crashed against the cliff, and was lifted dozens of feet high by the gale, leaving the area below the cliff covered in foam and water.

“Splash…”

“Boom…”

Ji Yuan looked out at the distant sea, which looked like a scene from an apocalyptic disaster movie. Thunder rumbled and the sea churned, with a force even greater than the gale-force winds that had previously disturbed the desolate ocean currents.

“Over there.”

Ji Yuan pointed to the southeast region of the ocean, and the old beggar’s white cloud continued its journey without slowing down, heading directly towards where Ji Yuan was pointing.

About half an hour later, the pair who had traveled a long way to see the spectacle finally saw the main characters.

The cultivators of Qianyuan Sect and Great Wind Valley were suspended in the sky. The younger cultivators stood on spiritual artifacts, while the higher-level cultivators rode the raging winds. The experts on both sides constantly formed hand seals to control the wind, using the force to lash out at each other. The sea below constantly erupted with waves, as if trapped in a violently shaking bucket.

While exchanging blows, the two sides continued their verbal sparring.

“Come, come, fellow Taoists from Mad Wind Valley, aren’t you proud of your ability to control the wind? How come you can’t even handle our Qianyuan Sect’s minor wind-controlling techniques?”

“Fellow Taoists of Qianyuan Sect, we are Great Wind Valley, not Mad Wind Valley.2 Unlike you, who claim to cultivate your inner peace and mind, yet your temperaments are so violent. Haven’t you seen that we have already taken control of your winds? How could you possibly be suppressing us? Are you suggesting we should whip you with our gales?”

“You’re talking nonsense! How can you say you’re leading us around? If you’ve got the guts, blow us away, blow us into the sea!”

“Just you wait, I’ll make sure you can’t stay in the sky any longer!”

The two continued to unleash their techniques. In Ji Yuan’s eyes, the surrounding area was no longer a howling gale, but instead like countless dragons of wind dancing and ferocious beasts roaring, their tails sweeping and their claws tearing at each other.

“Whoosh…whoosh…”

“Bang……”

“Bang……”

“Bang……”

The collision of wind currents produced a tremendously powerful sound.

“What? Aren’t you going to deal with us yet? Weren’t you going to blow us into the sea? Fellow Taoists, don’t worry about our safety. Our Qianyuan Sect’s water control techniques are quite good; we won’t drown!”

The old cultivator from Great Wind Valley laughed in fury.

“Hahahaha… I was wondering what your Qianyuan Sect was all about, talking about self-cultivation and so-called ‘controlling the wind with your mind’. Turns out it’s all about influencing other people’s minds with empty words. I was wrong before, it’s not that you’re useless, it’s that you’re downright despicable and shameless!”

“Fellow Taoist, you’ve gone too far! Be careful you don’t get struck by lightning!”

“Boom…”

“Boom…”

“Boom…”

Several bolts of lightning rained down towards Great Wind Valley’s position in an instant. Most of them passed by very close without hitting anyone, but they still caused the cultivators of Great Wind Valley to subconsciously take action to resist, and for a moment the wind became somewhat unstable.

“You old bastard, you’re playing dirty tricks!”

“A contest of spiritual power should be all-encompassing. Although it’s about controlling the wind, if a few claps of thunder scare you into losing control, what kind of mastery of wind control would that be? Or is it a lack of mental fortitude? Come and cultivate in seclusion at my Qianyuan Sect for a few decades, and I will certainly give you a warm welcome!”

“You, you, you! This is outrageous! ‘Winds Rise and Clouds Surge’!”

Normally, immortal cultivators wouldn’t shout out the techniques they were using in battle, unless they were truly angry and wanted to make a show of it.3

“Whoosh…whoosh…crash…”

The dark clouds in the sky were stirred into huge vortexes by the gale, just like the lightning that Ji Yuan encountered back in the tribulation. It looked terrifying.

The surrounding sea was swept up by the gale, forming huge waterspouts near the two combatant factions. These waterspouts connected the sky and the sea, with lightning constantly striking them, creating rolling bolts of lightning.

“Let me show you Qiantuan Sect people what true wind control is. The wind carries the power of the heavens, sweeping away clouds in a single strike! Fellow Taoists of Great Wind Valley, follow my method! Fellow Taoists of the Qianyuan Sect, you’d better hold on! If you can’t, you’ll be headed into the sea!”

As the old man from Great Wind Valley roared, echoing throughout the heavens and earth, the Qianyuan Sect stopped their boasting. The wind was clearly getting out of control, so they could only keep making hand seals and casting techniques.

In the distance, Ji Yuan, though his expression remained calm and composed, was actually stunned. This wind manipulation was simply divine, and frighteningly exaggerated. The slight arrogance he had gained from his past achievements vanished at this moment. There would always be people more capable; he should be more humble.4

“Wow, that’s something. Mr. Ji, What do you think?”

“Their wind control techniques are nothing short of divine. This is no longer just about controlling the wind; it’s about how all techniques follow the movement of the wind, combining wildness and subtlety. Amazing!”

“Yeah, it’s really impressive. Let’s stand further back a bit, the commotion is getting a bit loud.”

As he spoke, the old beggar rode the cloud higher into the sky and retreated even further away. The two main casters were no longer visible to the naked eye, but with his divine sight, Ji Yuan could see the entire process of the battle in full detail.

“Won’t something bad happen?”

Ji Yuan asked with a hint of concern. The old beggar smiled and shook his head.

“No, no, even if something happens, it wouldn’t be anything serious. It’s quite normal for discussions to turn into battles during the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly. Everyone knows their limits…”

“Boom…”

“Bang…”

“Boom…”

The wind whipped up the dark water, carrying thunder and lightning. Giant whips of wind, thunder, and water lashed wildly, and the surrounding sea was swirled up, as if being churned by Sun Wukong’s golden staff, creating a violent upheaval.

The old beggar hadn’t finished speaking when he added: “Um, well…”

As it turned out, although Ji Yuan and the old beggar arrived a little late, they weren’t too late. The battle of power continued for two more days after their arrival, with countless changes occurring in the movement and path of the winds, which broadened Ji Yuan’s horizons and made him truly realize what it meant for the wind to be unpredictable.

On the third day, things finally took a turn for the better. It wasn’t that the two sides were tired or had reconciled, but rather that a peacemaker had arrived. This peacemaker was naturally not Ji Yuan or the old beggar, but the host, Nine Peaks Mountain.

Three streaks of light appeared from the horizon, simultaneously transmitting their voices across the sea.

“Fellow Taoists, please stop! Fellow Taoists, please stop!”

“Stop fighting! Fellow Taoists, let’s not ruin our harmony. The Cultivators’ Grand Assembly hasn’t even started yet!”

“What if a mortal’s ship were to capsize while passing by? And the aquatic creatures in the sea would also suffer. How would we explain this if the Dragon Clan of the Northern Sea came to demand an explanation?”

After spouting a string of profound principles, the three streaks of light used their supernatural powers to prepare for defense, carefully approaching the vicinity of both sides. After much persuasion, they finally managed to gradually calm the wind.

The old beggar chuckled and turned to Ji Yuan.

“It seems we don’t need to take action. Heh, Nine Peaks Mountain is still full of pride, but they’ll soon find out why immortal sects that have hosted the Cultivators’ Grand Assembly once usually don’t host it a second time.”

Ji Yuan was still immersed in the profound experience of countless wind control techniques when he heard the old beggar’s words and couldn’t help but laugh.

“I can understand that somewhat now.”

The cultivators of Nine Peaks Mountain finally managed to persuade both sides to calm down. Their anger had subsided after having been vented these past few days; one side had grasped the wonder and power of the other’s wind control, while the other side felt somewhat uneasy for still not being able to defeat the other.

Chinese idiom meaning “adapt to changing circumstances”

狂风 (kuángfēng): gale/squall vs 大风 (dàfēng): gale. Not much difference in terms of the English translation together, however when you separate the characters, you get 狂 (kuáng): mad/wild/violent vs 大 (dà): great/eldest. It’s an excellent pun, keeping the original meaning while changing the connotation.

T/n: honestly the best explanation for the dumb shonen trope of shouting out attack names: it’s for intimidation purposes!
