Happy Autumnal Equinox!

Not me realizing that I didn’t put a footnote for this guy in the last chapter… welp buckle in folks. The Golden Crow, or 金烏 Jinwu, is a specific Chinese version of the mythological three-legged crow that is nearly ubiquitous in East-Asian mythologies. Three-legged crows always represent the sun, whether carrying it or being a manifestation of it. According to the Chinese myths, there were originally 10 golden crows that would travel around the earth carrying their respective suns one at a time on a roster each day, but one day they all came out at the same time, causing the world to burn. Hou Yi, the celestial archer, shot down all but one of them. (T/n: I’m sure that last golden crow is perfectly happy with this turn of events. Nope, definitely no resentment here. I have absolutely no idea what could possibly prompt it to become demonic. None at all. 😐 )

T/n: “but which kind of football, Mango?” I hear you ask. And yes, I did explicitly translate this word-for-word. It’s the soccer kind. Or you can just ignore me and assume American Football. Sure, there’s small differences in width and total area, but at this point it really doesn’t matter. The room is big.

Chapter 495: The Increasingly Unfathomable Man

As the old beggar said, this thread was neither aligned with metal, water, wood, fire, nor earth; it did not belong to any of the five elements.

The vast majority of things in the universe fell under the Five Elements. Every blade of grass, every tree, every bird and beast, and even wind, clouds, thunder, and lightning had an elemental affiliation. Saying something didn’t belong to the Five Elements was akin to saying that the thing wasn’t material.

But with the old beggar and Ji Yuan’s keen eyesight, they couldn’t be mistaken.

Since the scroll had been tied with this thread, could it also be something special?

With this thought, Ji Yuan picked up the scroll beside him and slowly unfurled it.

However, the contents disappointed Ji Yuan and the old beggar. It was a landscape painting with the inscription “Great Beauty of Mountains and Rivers,” and the name of the painter was none other than the old emperor’s name, Chu Ze. Obviously, it was unlikely to have any supernatural properties, otherwise the old emperor would be a master that even Ji Yuan and the old beggar could not see through.

Ji Yuan refocused his attention on the fine gold thread in his hand, looked at the old emperor beside him, and asked:

“Your Majesty, where did you obtain this gold thread?”

Before today, the old emperor would have thought it was just an ordinary thread woven with gold, and would not have remembered anything about it. He could only look at the old eunuch beside him.

“Gong Shun, do you know?”

The old eunuch couldn’t really remember clearly either. There were plenty of gold threads in the palace, how could he possibly remember a specific one? But since the old emperor had asked, he could only bite the bullet and answer. After thinking for a moment, he stepped forward and spoke.

“Your Majesty, there are many gold threads in the palace. The eunuchs in charge of purchases bring some back from the manufacturing workshop every now and then. These gold threads may have been made by skilled craftsmen in the manufacturing workshop.”

“The manufacturing workshop?”

The old beggar narrowed his eyes and smiled.

“Haha, this thread is definitely not something an ordinary person could make. It’s not made of gold…”

At this point, the old beggar suddenly remembered something and turned to face Men Yutong, who had been standing to the side.

“Imperial Preceptor Men, may I borrow your scroll again?”

“Yes!”

Men Yutong took out the scroll from the hidden pocket in his sleeve and handed it to the old beggar with both hands. Seemingly understanding the old beggar’s intentions, Ji Yuan also handed over the golden thread in his hand.

The old beggar grasped the golden thread and gently wrapped it around the scroll a few times before tying it tightly. Once the golden thread had been tied, the original subtly supernatural features of the Xiezhi scroll did not disappear, nor did they change due to the golden thread, but rather it gave Ji Yuan and the old beggar a subtle sense of rightness.

“So that’s how it is. This thread was originally used to tie the Xiezhi’s scroll.”

Normally, Ji Yuan and the old beggar would have left after not finding anything in the Imperial Treasure Vault. However, the main reason they had stayed was that Ji Yuan still had a faint, inexplicable feeling.

Upon finding the golden thread, Ji Yuan’s feeling vanished, and he suddenly realized that there was nothing left to search for.

In fact, the Xiezhi’s scroll itself only exuded a slightly supernatural aura, only about equivalent to that of a talisman, and could easily be sealed. Whether or not this golden thread was there had little impact. However, the current situation made Ji Yuan realize that the two originated from the same source.

As for the gold thread, it didn’t seem to be any kind of supernatural artifact at all; it was simply made of a special material. It was more like something the painter had casually used to tie the scroll.

Ji Yuan removed the gold thread again, returned the Xiezhi scroll to Men Yutong, and then addressed the old emperor.

“Your Majesty, may we take this golden thread?”

Even though he knew the golden thread was extraordinary, the old emperor understood he had no use for it. However, giving it to Ji Yuan and the old beggar was an important favor, so he had no objections and quickly replied.

“I have already said that whatever the two immortals take a fancy to, you may take it. This item is no exception; it is just a golden thread.”

“Thank you, Your Majesty!”

Ji Yuan cupped his hands in a respectful gesture towards the old emperor. Although it was only a slight bow, it was the first time he had ever bowed to the old emperor, which made the latter, who had long held the throne, feel a little excited.

“You’re too kind, Immortal Master. By the way, I have already instructed the imperial kitchen to prepare a banquet for dinner. Why don’t you two stay and dine with us? What do you say, Imperial Preceptor?”

Men Yutong quickly added.

“Yes, yes, why don’t you two immortals dine in the palace?”

“Haha…”

Ji Yuan smiled.

“You two need not be nervous, I will not leave yet. However, we will skip the meal in the palace. I have promised to personally cook a meal for the Qiao family’s children to repay them for slaughtering those two old chickens.”

The old beggar, without any regard for appearances, unceremoniously reached into his collar to scratch the itch on his back, also declining.

“Hey, Mr. Ji is going to cook, how could this old beggar not go and taste it? Mr. Ji, what are we waiting for? Let’s go, or it’ll get dark soon.”

Quite a few things had happened today. It was already late in the afternoon, and though it hadn’t yet grown dark, the old beggar was indeed very curious about Ji Yuan’s culinary skills.

Without the consent of the old emperor or the Imperial Advisor, Ji Yuan and the old beggar departed. The old emperor opened his mouth, but in the end, ultimately did not ask whether they could go together.

Ji Yuan and the old beggar had already left. Qiao Yong, who stood beside the emperor, hesitated for a moment before speaking.

“Uh, Your Majesty, this humble subject… shouldn’t this humble subject return?”

The old emperor was taken aback upon hearing this and looked at Qiao Yong.

“Good Minister Qiao, you’re still here? Hurry back! Remember to treat the two immortals well for me. Oh, and whatever ingredients they need, have the imperial kitchen send them over directly! Don’t just stand there, don’t keep your guests waiting for their host, go quickly!”

“Yes, yes, yes, Your Majesty, this humble subject shall obey the decree, I will go immediately!”

Qiao Yong apologized and quickly left. The old emperor and his entourage also exited the imperial study at the same time. However, they only spotted Qiao Yong leaving quickly. Ji Yuan and the old beggar were nowhere to be seen.

“Imperial Preceptor, what do you think of my response?”

Standing outside the imperial study, the old emperor frowned slightly as he watched Qiao Yong’s departing figure, and turned to Men Yutong beside him.

“Your Majesty’s actions and demeanor were measured and appropriate; no one could have done better than Your Majesty.”

After thinking for a moment, Men Yutong continued.

“Your Majesty, I shall take my leave now.”

“Imperial Preceptor, won’t you stay for a meal?”

Men Yutong sighed and raised the scroll depicting the mythical Xiezhi in his hand.

“Your Majesty, this painting harbors some secret, and my cultivation is not yet sufficient to bear such a mystery. I cannot keep it, nor dare I. I should hand it over to the two immortal masters.”

Previously, when Ji Yuan and the old beggar performed their techniques at the Heavenly Master’s Office, Men Yutong was actually a little worried that the two experts would take the scroll directly. However, immortals of the righteous path indeed would never do such a thing, so they returned it to him afterward, only reminding him to keep it safe.

But now, no matter how much Men Yutong thought it over, he felt something was wrong. He felt he dared not keep the painting any longer, so he himself thought of handing it over to the two masters. Perhaps the two masters had already foreseen this, but had not said anything before.

“By the way, Your Majesty, the two immortals also mentioned something else earlier, about the Pozi Mountain Range in Bianrong Prefecture…”

…

While Ji Yuan was cooking at the Qiao family’s residence, the immortal master from the Heavenly Master’s Office in the capital and the eunuch who had delivered the imperial decree had already set off. On the way, they happened to meet the person sent by Bianrong Prefecture to report.

Before the meal was ready, the Imperial Preceptor Men Yutong arrived at the Qiao residence and solemnly left behind the Xiezhi’s scroll. Ji Yuan was in the kitchen and did not come out, so it was the old beggar who went to the front yard to accept it.

As Ji Yuan cooked, in addition to making dishes from Ji Province, he also attempted to recreate the fish dish he had made on Xuanxin Mansion’s flying ship. Although he did not have any Glazed Water Golden Scale Sturgeon, with the help of the jujube blossom honey and Ji Yuan’s cooking techniques, even an ordinary grass carp was cooked to perfection. He even removed the small bones to make it easier for the children to eat, and the taste was naturally excellent.

During this time, Ji Yuan generously took out one of his Thousand-Dou Jars and poured wine for everyone. Although it was only the white Thousand-Dou Jar, the wine inside was still very beneficial, and the spiritual energy it contained was easier for people to absorb.

When the night fell and all grew quiet, in the same courtyard in the guest wing, Ji Yuan held the golden thread in his hand. The old beggar held the scroll of the mythical Xiezhi. The two sat opposite each other at the stone table in the courtyard.

“Mr. Ji, it’s better if you keep this Xiezhi painting. This old beggar just hopes you can tell me the deeper meaning behind it.”

The old beggar pushed the scroll in front of Ji Yuan, who unfolded it on the table. Without any spiritual energy or power infused into it, it looked just like an ordinary painting, except that the being depicted in the painting was rather fierce and strange.

“Mr. Lu, it’s not that I’m unwilling to talk about it, but I don’t know what the deeper meaning of this painting is. As for the Xiezhi, the Golden Crow, and the Bifang, I feel there’s a lot to say, but I can’t quite put my finger on it.”

This was Ji Yuan’s honest opinion. After all, he hadn’t specialized in this field in his previous life. He had some recollection of it and could say a few things, but he couldn’t go into great detail. However, the old beggar couldn’t help but interpret his words differently.

“Alright, it seems it’s best not to talk more about it. This old beggar will go rest now. You should also go to sleep early, sir.”

The old beggar didn’t insist. He stood up, dusted himself off, and nonchalantly turned and went into the guest room opposite.

Although he didn’t learn much today, he vaguely felt as though he were facing an ancient secret. Even someone like the old beggar, upon reflection, was deeply shaken.

It was obvious that this was an extremely important secret, so it was perfectly normal that Ji Yuan was reluctant to elaborate.

As the door closed, the old beggar peered through the slowly closing crack towards Ji Yuan, who was sitting at the stone table in the courtyard. Bathed in moonlight, dressed in a blue robe, Ji Yuan seemed to be coated with a layer of light as he held the golden thread in his hand, lost in thought as he gazed at the scroll on the table.

‘The longer I know you, the less I understand you, Ji Yuan. Who exactly are you?’

However, even though Ji Yuan’s origins were unknown, someone like the old beggar, who had clear enlightenment and a deep connection to the divine, trusted Ji Yuan’s character implicitly. He knew this man’s path was undoubtedly righteous.

T/n: I have a very big soft spot for outside-POV chapters with mysterious protagonists. It’s just so dang fun to feel like a god in the narrative

Chapter 496: What a Treasure!

Ji Yuan glanced at the room across the courtyard. The old beggar had already closed the door. A cultivator like the old beggar surely wouldn’t pretend to close the door and then secretly observe from behind it.

Who created this painting of the mythical beast Xiezhi? And what was the material of the golden thread in his hand? Why was it being used to bind a painting so casually? Was it really not some kind of spiritual artifact?

The golden thread slowly floated up from Ji Yuan’s hand. He used his spiritual power to gather the surrounding spiritual energy and concentrate it on the golden thread. The influx of spiritual energy caused the golden thread to emit a faint golden light, but it wasn’t because the thread had activated any mysterious formation; at most, it could only be considered the special material’s reaction to spiritual energy.

As Ji Yuan’s thoughts changed, the suspended golden thread also changed shape in various ways, sometimes straight, sometimes curved, sometimes like a snake swimming through the air, sometimes intertwined and knotted, and then easily untied.

This situation made Ji Yuan mutter to himself.

“It is very easy to control with my mind; I can change it at will with almost perfect precision.”

Although the art of manipulating objects was a very basic method in the path of immortality and even in various other cultivation paths, it was also the most varied. The most representative example was the art of sword control, which had long been a well-known orthodox method of the immortal path, and had even become a fundamental technique for many immortal cultivators.

This was because, while learning to control objects was not difficult, mastering it was not easy, and developing more ingenious, subtle uses and techniques was even more difficult. It required a high level of skill from the caster, and equally high receptiveness in the objects being controlled.

The golden thread in Ji Yuan’s hand was indeed miraculous, changing almost at will with perfect precision.

‘I wonder how strong it is?’

At this thought, Ji Yuan did not hesitate. He grabbed the floating golden thread with both hands and then pulled it forcefully to either side.

Despite his refined and elegant appearance, Ji Yuan was actually a cultivator who had been tempering himself with spiritual energy for many years. He stood at the perfection of all Five Elements, and had been passively tempered by Heavenly Tribulation Lightning. His own inherent strength was already extraordinary.

“Thwang…”

The thread made a rustling sound as the threads moved, but it was exceptionally strong.

Ji Yuan’s mind stirred, and his spiritual energy surged. With this, the force with which he pulled the thread increased a hundredfold, then a thousandfold in an instant.

‘Indeed, quite resilient…’

“Snap…”

Before Ji Yuan could even process the thought, the thread snapped.

‘It’s broken…it’s broken!?’

Ji Yuan was clearly stunned for a moment; this was quite different from what he had imagined…

Looking at the two sections of the golden thread in his hands, Ji Yuan couldn’t help but twitch his lips. What should he do?

He tentatively tried to put the broken ends of the thread together, and the two threads automatically reconnected. When he pulled them slightly, he couldn’t even feel that the golden thread had ever snapped at all. Even the feedback from his spiritual power was perfectly clear, as if it were a perfect whole.

“Interesting!”

Ji Yuan tossed the golden thread slightly into the air, letting it float. Then, Ji Yuan stretched out his right hand to the side, and the Green Vine Immortal Sword, which was connected to his thoughts, immediately flew to his side. Ji Yuan grasped the hilt of the sword at just the right moment.

“Shiing—”

“Clack!”

The clear sound of the sword ringing out and the crisp sound of it returning to its sheath rang out almost without interruption. Sure enough, the golden thread in the air had broken into two sections. However, this time it was not broken in the middle. One side occupied more than 90% of the length, while the other side had only a short section of thread.

Ji Yuan grabbed the two sections of thread again and carefully brought them together.

The sword intent inflicted by the immortal sword was difficult to remove. Normally, things severed by the Green Vine Sword could not be easily reassembled. However, as soon as the two sections of thread approached each other, they immediately reassembled, as seamlessly connected as before.

“Interesting!”

Ji Yuan rubbed his chin with his hand, glancing sideways at the thread, as if considering something. After a moment, his eyes flashed and he made a decision.

First, he reached out and broke off another section of the thread.

Then, in the next moment, Ji Yuan slightly opened his mouth, and with a breath, a stream of reddish-gray smoke flowed directly onto the short end of the rope.

When the True Fire of Samadhi appeared, although there was no change in the temperature of the courtyard, the balance of Yin and Yang was instantly disrupted, and even the flow of spiritual energy was repelled.

The old beggar, who had been lying quietly on the bed, sat up almost immediately. If the sound of the sword ringing out had felt like a flash of silver light on a mental level before, then the feeling at this moment was like a raging sea of ​​fire descending.

The old beggar looked towards the wooden door. Just what exactly was Ji Yuan up to outside? He seemed to be using some kind of powerful fire-controlling technique.

Inside the courtyard, under the scorching heat of the True Fire of Samadhi, the thread remained unchanged, which amazed Ji Yuan and put his mind at ease.

The True Fire of Samadhi’s energy paused briefly, then a stream of genuine reddish-gray True Fire was released.

“Whoosh…”

The flames reached the end of the rope, and within a few breaths, as if the intense heat of the True Fire of Samadhi was too overwhelming, the originally thin thread, which resembled a woven strand of whitish-gold, was now a dazzling, shimmering fiery-gold.

The True Fire of Samadhi was extremely dangerous, but while Ji Yuan’s control over it was not yet completely effortless, it was far superior to before. At least, he could now perform other techniques within the fire.

Under the scorching heat of the True Fire of Samadhi, Ji Yuan once again used his mind to control the object. The thread was pulled and its length was extended, just like stretching a rubber band. However, a rubber band would become thinner when stretched, while this one seemed to remain unchanged, or at least change was not so obvious.

A few breaths later, Ji Yuan withdrew the True Fire of Samadhi, only to be dumbfounded again. The end of the rope hadn’t reverted back to its original state; instead, it remained in that golden state, and even the length after being stretched was the same.

Upon closer inspection, the golden section of rope appeared to have completely changed its properties. Although it looked more dazzling, the original delicate texture, resembling finely woven strands of golden thread, was gone, and its flexibility had also decreased considerably when he squeezed it.

‘Uh… can this still be reverted?’

Ji Yuan picked up the longer section of the golden thread, brought it close to the shimmering rope, and tapped them together. There was no reaction; it still separated back as it was supposed to.

‘Did I go too far?’

“Tsk… well…”

Suddenly, Ji Yuan had a flash of inspiration. He reached out and grabbed the section of rope that had changed, pulling hard again, even using his spiritual energy to exert force, but the rope remained unmoved.

Since he couldn’t break it off by hand, he drew his sword again the next moment.

“Shiiiing—”

“Ding!”

When the Green Vine Sword struck this section of golden rope, it actually slightly spread out by two inches. Although Ji Yuan did not use any spiritual power at all, and although the immortal sword was just casually slashing without sword light or sword intent, this seemed to be the first time he could ever recall encountering something that the Green Vine Sword could not successfully cleave in a single strike.

“Buzz—”

As the immortal sword trembled slightly, a terrifying sword intent rose up. The sword body was filled with a bright silver light, seemingly preparing to strike again, but Ji Yuan directly reached out and pressed it down.

“Don’t be stubborn, it’s just a small test, it doesn’t have to be split open.”

With that, Ji Yuan returned the sword to its sheath, patted the blade, and then calmed the Green Vine Sword down, letting it hang behind him again.

Ji Yuan’s gaze never left the section of golden rope. He reached out and touched it, feeling it carefully. He could feel a faint, almost imperceptible mark. He tried to infuse it with some spiritual energy, and sure enough, the mark disappeared immediately.

“This is quite interesting! It’s really a treasure!”

As Ji Yuan murmured to himself with a smile, the old beggar in the opposite room finally couldn’t hold back any longer.

“Mr. Ji, what are you up to in the middle of the night instead of sleeping?”

It was fine before, but just now he suddenly felt Ji Yuan’s sword intent soaring into the sky, which startled the old beggar. If he hadn’t been able to sense any evil energy around him, the old beggar would have almost thought that some demon had attacked.

Ji Yuan quickly apologized to the guest room across the courtyard.

“It is truly my fault for disturbing Mr. Lu’s sleep. I will go to sleep now, I will go to sleep now, hahaha…”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan stood up and solemnly bowed to the person opposite, before putting away the Xiezhi scroll and the two threads of different lengths that had been sitting on the table. He then turned and walked into his guest room.

“Laughing so happily and mysteriously…”

The old beggar muttered something to himself in his room. Although he was curious, he lay down again. Even though he had a good relationship with Ji Yuan, he couldn’t interfere with other people’s cultivation.

Back in his room, Ji Yuan couldn’t sleep at all. Like a child who had finally gotten a fun toy, he was so restless that he wanted to get out of bed and play with it secretly in the middle of the night.

Of course, Ji Yuan had not been playing around just now, but because of this special thread, many wonderful ideas had popped into his mind.

Sitting on the bed, Ji Yuan flicked his sleeve, out of which flew a series of spiritual talismans. He had bought these when he had been browsing the market of the Peak Ferry Crossing. They could be used to cast barriers, and in Ji Yuan’s discerning eyes, they were quite extraordinary.

At that moment, Ji Yuan unleashed twelve spirit talismans around the inn, followed by a row of spirit coins in his hand. He then launched these talismans, placing one spirit coin on top of each talisman, and sticking them around the room.

The surroundings seemed to fall silent all at once. This didn’t mean that the sounds from inside were no longer audible, but rather that there was now a sensory and mental change in perception brought about by the effect of the barrier, as if the world inside and outside the house had been separated.

After doing all this, several wisps of crystal-clear silk threads flew out from Ji Yuan’s sleeve, followed by several neat stacks of scales. Then, the Edict Lightning Talisman, which was still shimmering with stored lightning and had not yet fully recharged, also appeared.

“The spiritually-refined silk thread belongs to metal, the golden scales of the Mirror Sea Sturgeon belong to water, the heavenly tribulation lightning belongs to wood, the True Fire of Samadhi belongs to fire but also contains yin and yang… I’m still missing earth!”

Indeed, Ji Yuan wanted to try to refine a spiritual weapon for himself. He already had a preliminary idea in mind. This golden thread was so special as it didn’t belong to any of the five elements, but he could reintegrate it into the five elements, making it a rare item that combined all five. It wouldn’t be through a barrier, but rather through achieving a perfect balance of all Five Elements at the same qualitative level.

Although Ji Yuan did not possess any advanced weapon-refining techniques, he was familiar with some of the more common weapon-refining methods and always preferred the method of Edicts.

After all, he was still lacking some materials, and treasures were not forged in a day. Today was just a test to see if they could be fused together. Only when they could all be integrated could be discuss next steps. And the most important catalyst was the True Fire of Samadhi.

T/n: Ji Yuan really did the cultivator equivalent of sneaking his phone under his covers LOL

Chapter 497: Alright, Alright, I Understand

Ji Yuan was now certain that the True Fire of Samadhi, under the right conditions, would not burn the golden thread, but would only change its state.

However, Ji Yuan was also quite certain that none of the other magical silk threads or golden scales in his hands could withstand the burning of the True Fire. Perhaps only the tribulation lightning would not be immediately burned by the True Fire.

But as the saying went, in the Five Elements, wood generates fire. Other types of wood might be fine; they would probably be burned by the True Fire of Samadhi before they even realized what’s happening. But if this Heavenly Tribulation Lightning were to be intertwined with the True Fire of Samadhi, and there were to be even the slightest loss of control…1

Just thinking about it sent chills down Ji Yuan’s spine. The uncontrolled eruption of the True Lightning-Fire of Samadhi was one of the most terrifying things he had imagined.

“However, self-control has always been one of my strengths.”

Ji Yuan muttered to himself. He began with the spiritually-refined silk threads, casting a technique to weave them in with a small section of the golden thread. Then he directly exhaled a mouthful of True Fire of Samadhi.”

The next moment, the flames collided with the threads. The special silk that Ji Yuan had refined could not withstand it for even a split second, turning to ash instantly. Ji Yuan felt slightly embarrassed, but fortunately the True Fire of Samadhi was his specialty.

They tried another golden scale. This time, the golden silk thread was wrapped around the golden scale, and when the True Fire of Samadhi burned it, a thin filmy layer of light actually appeared on the golden scale. Because water and fire were incompatible, the essence of Yin water emitted ripples, briefly resisting the True Fire of Samadhi.

The next instant…

“Bang!!”

“Splash splash splash…”

The room looked as though a waterfall had burst open, with large waves of water spraying out, soaking Ji Yuan halfway through. The water then evaporated in a very short time, leaving nothing but a lingering mist.

Ji Yuan’s entire body was now shrouded in mist, making him appear ethereal and otherworldly.

As expected, the initial attempt failed. Spiritual treasures were not so easy to refine. Cultivators who specialized in this path needed to devote years of effort.2 Although he had extremely strong control and was quite talented in the immortal path, it was impossible for him to succeed overnight.

“I still have this one left that I haven’t tried…”

Ji Yuan glanced at the lightning-streaked Edict and hesitated for a moment.

‘Let’s try refining the silk thread again. Perhaps I can first refine the golden thread with True Fire, then immediately extinguish the flames and let the silk replace the original golden thread, weaving it into the newly altered golden thread!’

Without hesitation, Ji Yuan began. He didn’t use a new section of golden thread, but instead used the section that had already been scorched before, and started refining it anew.

Despite the slight excitement brought on by interest and anticipation, Ji Yuan remained calm and carefully experimented with the spiritual artifact in his mind.

Before he knew it, a night had passed, and Ji Yuan, who had seemingly made no progress, had begun to understand the basics.

The crafting of spiritual artifacts or treasures was not always about having the most skillful techniques. Experience and ingenious methods were also essential, as were favorable timing and circumstance. Perhaps it was the lack of earth element materials, or perhaps something else entirely.

Of course, he also needed to learn from experience, and humbly seek guidance from experts. As for experts, there was a true expert currently right across the courtyard with whom Ji Yuan had a good relationship, and whom Ji Yuan found trustworthy.

It was already dawn. This was, after all, the Qiao residence, and Ji Yuan promptly removed the barriers, allowing the morning light to bathe the paper doors and windows in a soft glow.

Ji Yuan, who hadn’t slept all night, rose from the bed feeling refreshed and walked to the door.

The door creaked open just as the dawn finished rising, making Ji Yuan feel warm and cozy.

The old beggar, who also hadn’t slept all night, also opened the door at that moment and greeted Ji Yuan with a smile.3

“Good morning, Mr. Ji. How was your rest?”

“I rested well. How about Mr. Lu?”

Ji Yuan answered with a smile.

“It was alright, but it would have been even better if I knew what Mr. Ji was up to last night…”

Ji Yuan smiled and shook his head. Just when the old beggar thought that Ji Yuan would naturally remain silent or speak in riddles, Ji Yuan spoke up and said something that surprised him.

“Actually, I just wanted to try to refine a treasure using yesterday’s golden thread.”

“Oh?”

The old beggar showed great interest.

“How did it go?”

Ji Yuan pointed to the stone table in the courtyard.

“I have many questions and would like to ask Mr. Lu for advice.”

The old beggar stared intently at Ji Yuan. Seeing Ji Yuan’s sincere expression, he smiled even more broadly. He quickly came out of the room and walked towards the stone table.

“Come, come, Mr. Ji, come over here and tell me slowly. This old beggar will definitely tell you everything I know!”

Ji Yuan nodded, went to the table in the courtyard, and with a flick of his sleeve, a teapot and teacups appeared on the table. The teapot was steaming hot, and a jar of honey with a sweet aroma was placed on the tea tray.

“Hahaha, this honey is a real gem! Only you possess it, Mr. Ji. This old beggar is in for a treat!”

The old beggar, being a man of such stature, could naturally distinguish between good and bad spiritual objects. Back in Ji Yuan’s courtyard, he had felt that this honey was very special. After eating the dishes made by Ji Yuan yesterday and tasting this honey, he was even more certain of this. Even someone with his level of cultivation could benefit from it, so it was definitely no ordinary spiritually-infused substance. Moreover, Ji Yuan had never refined this honey before; it was all-natural. It could only be said that the flowers used to make the honey were extraordinary.

“Haha, if Mr. Lu likes it, when the honey matures in the future, I will specially reserve a jar for you.”

“Two jars!”

The old beggar held up two fingers and waved them back and forth, and Ji Yuan quickly shook his head.

“No, no, it’s not that I’m stingy, but this honey is hard to find in the world. The fact that I can spare even a jar is enough to show our deep friendship. For me to give an ordinary person even a spoonful is already a sign of a profound bond with me.”

Hearing the phrase “deep friendship” made the old beggar very happy.

“Hahaha… Indeed, this old beggar knows that Mr. Ji is not a stingy person. One jar is still one jar, but don’t forget it, otherwise this old beggar will be squatting at the gate of Ju’an Pavilion begging for food every day.”

After the jokes, the two immediately got down to business. Ji Yuan didn’t hold back and told the old beggar all about the various changes in the golden thread and his thoughts.

If someone else had said these things, the old beggar would have just listened and laughed. But since it was Ji Yuan who said them, even though some of the content sounded like absurd lapses in “common sense” to him, the old beggar couldn’t help but ponder it carefully, since it came from Ji Yuan.

“It does not belong to the Five Elements, but here it is incorporated into the Five Elements… Using nothingness to support existence, achieving innate balance, and also taking into account Yin and Yang…”

If anyone else had said this, the old beggar would have immediately cursed them as a “fool,” but after Ji Yuan finished speaking, he remained silent for a long time, pondering the possibilities.

“That’s right. The other four elements are easy to deal with, but the main problem is that I’m currently lacking an earth element, so I can’t try to transform it.”

The old beggar didn’t show it on his face, but his heart trembled. He understood Ji Yuan’s words to mean that he had a grasp of the other four elements and the Yin-Yang transformation, but he was missing the Earth element.

“If there is no spiritual Earth for the time being, Mr. Ji, in my humble opinion, it would be best to gradually integrate it into the Earth through the principle of mutual generation.”4

“Yes, I think so too. But all things in Heaven and Earth are born from Yin and Yang, and forging weapons ultimately requires the object to be refined. So I plan to use the True Fire of Samadhi as the base, manifesting the form of fire while taking into account Yin and Yang, and then incorporate the Heavenly Tribulation Lightning of the Wood element to amplify the fire. What do you think, Mr. Lu?”

The old beggar frowned.

“The True Fire of Samadhi? Heavenly Tribulation Lightning?”

Ji Yuan humbly sought guidance and naturally spoke frankly, revealing in detail his two most powerful skills.

“I dare not claim to be skilled at controlling fire, but I do have a powerful fire-attribute divine power called the True Fire of Samadhi, and another equally extraordinary lightning-controlling divine power called Lightning Control Edict Talisman.”

Ji Yuan was a rather modest person, a fact the old beggar understood. Ji Yuan had always been ashamed to show people the extraordinary power of the “Universe in the Sleeve” ability, which he had only glimpsed on paper years ago. Now that he was saying its power was remarkable, it must be something truly unfathomable, beyond the comprehension of ghosts and gods.

“May this old beggar have a look?”

“Of course!”

Ji Yuan nodded, then slightly opened his mouth.

“Whoosh…”

It wasn’t the breath of True Fire, but rather he directly spat out a ball of True Fire of Samadhi.

Whoosh…

The true fire had long existed within the realm of his mental world. The moment it manifested in the mind of a person of such spiritual cultivation as the old beggar, it immediately became a raging sea of ​​fire, setting everything ablaze—the mountains, the water, the wind, the clouds…

Shaking off the fleeting illusion, a closer look at the suspended True Fire before him revealed its steady, reddish-gray flames. Seemingly they did not even raise the surrounding temperature.

‘Danger! This fire is very dangerous!’

“The True Fire of Samadhi is one of my most prized supernatural abilities. It is composed of the True Fire of the Alchemy Furnace formed from the Divine Fire of the Heart Acupoint, and the True Fire of Yin and Yang formed from the Yin-Yang Qi. It represents the three elements of Heaven, Earth, and Man. It is derived from Samadhi,5 refining both fires into one, the True Fire of Samadhi. Those struck by it cannot escape. Its power is too great, so I rarely use it.”

The old beggar took a deep breath and nodded solemnly. He did not want to test it. He let Ji Yuan withdraw the True Fire, and then watched as a lightning-streaked spiritual talisman flew out from Ji Yuan’s sleeve.

“Sizzle, sizzle… sizzle, sizzle, sizzle…”

The oppressive feeling this time was much stronger than the restrained state of the True Fire of Samadhi, which had been held back and not released. This was mainly because the Lightning Talisman had not fully recovered.

The lightning flashed, shifting between gold and red, and the spiritual runes flickered erratically. The aura of tribulation lightning occasionally emanated from it, stirring up the surrounding wind and causing sand and stones in the courtyard to roll around the stone table.

The old beggar looked up at the sky and saw that the clouds were moving and gathering, simply due to the appearance of the Lightning Edict.

“This talisman is called the Lightning Edict Talisman. Years ago, a black dragon that had been injured for many years was seriously wounded, its essence dissipating. I used the Edict technique to forcibly collect its essence, transforming it into righteous Yang with the intent to exorcise evil, then condensed it into a lightning talisman. Since then, it has been storing lightning for many years, and even absorbed a remarkable Heavenly Lightning strike. Now it is still a little short of being able to stabilize again.”

Ji Yuan added after thinking for a moment.

“This talisman should not be used lightly either.”

The old beggar forced a smile.

“If this technique were to be used, it would likely descend as a divine punishment onto the world. Mr. Ji, with your benevolent nature, you will naturally use it with caution.”

Since they had already discussed the elements of fire and wood, Ji Yuan decided to talk about something else, and took out a golden scale.

“This item was obtained from the Mirror Sea within Jingxuan Ocean Pavilion’s waters; it is a living vessel of Yin Water essence…”

“The Glazed Water Golden Scaled Sturgeon?”

“Indeed, it is that fish. I previously forced most of the fish’s Yin water essence into its scales, and then removed it to serve as a water element item.”

To serve as the water element?

The old beggar’s eyes widened.

“And then there’s the metal element. Although it may seem slightly inferior, its flexibility is excellent, making it a very important item…”

“Alright, alright, alright… Mr. Ji, you don’t need to say anything more. This old beggar knows how capable you are, so stop… Let’s talk about earth, let’s talk about earth. You’re lacking the Earth element, we’ll think of a way…”

“Let’s talk about the refining method first. I am not very knowledgeable in this area, and am certainly not as skilled as Mr. Lu. Last night I tried to…”

In the end, Ji Yuan was genuinely eager to learn and didn’t try anything to provoke him. The old beggar breathed a sigh of relief and quickly got into the flow, feeling an even stronger sense of excitement than Ji Yuan.

With so many rare treasures in the world, and with him and Ji Yuan, two experts, discussing and perhaps even refining together, how extraordinary would the resulting spiritual artifact be?

Just thinking about it made even a highly accomplished immortal like the old beggar incredibly excited.

Research rabbit hole time!

So: lightning is classified under the Wood element. On first glance, that seems… kind of unexpected — you would expect it to be something like fire, right? A quick research rabbit hole later and I have something of an answer as to why it’s wood. Actually, I have a LOT of answers as to why it’s wood. Please take this with a grain of salt, I’m doing some heavy-duty interpretation and I am certainly not an official source.

First off, the Chinese Five Elements place a lot of emphasis on the generation cycle, not necessarily on the properties of the elements themselves. When lightning strikes it generates a fire. You know which of the five elements generates fire? Wood! Clouds form when water rises into the air, thereby generating lightning, which also conforms to Water generating Wood.

There’s also the secondary relationship of elemental restraint/suppression. In the Five Elements, metal is the one that restrains wood, and lo and behold, lightning can be conducted/controlled through metal.

Third, there’s the hexagram connection, which to be honest I don’t understand all that well because I am a beginner layperson. The gist is that there’s a hexagram (Zhen) that represents the East but also represents Thunder, and East is associated with Wood.

Lastly, and perhaps most importantly, the Five Elements don’t refer to their physical forms, they refer to concepts, that have been assigned those elements to refer to their metaphysical properties (for example, a wooden token shaped like a flowing river belongs to Water due to its properties, not the material its made out of). Wood refers to the concept of growth, expansion, flexibility, etc., all of which describe the ever-branching, ever-expanding nature of lightning to a T. It does not have the properties of Metal (cleansing, contracting), or Fire (moving upwards, ripening/fermenting), or Water (stillness, conserving), or Earth (centralizing, stabilizing, conserving).

Again, please take all this with a grain of salt. I am not a professional, nor have I studied all of this in-depth. I’m just a very easily distracted translator with a fondness for mythology and obscure research rabbit-holes. But yeah, that’s what I found!

Important side note here: yes, lightning here is classified under the Wood element. My research binge was getting WAY too long and I was getting WAY too invested so see the end-chapter T/n for the full paragraph.

Chapter 498: The Immortal Wine Left Behind

Ji Yuan and the old beggar were having a lively discussion in the courtyard. During this time, the old servant from the Qiao family brought out breakfast, which they ate together in the courtyard.

While the two were discussing the refining of spiritual artifacts, an old eunuch arrived at the front courtyard of the Qiao family. He was none other than Gong Shun, a trusted eunuch of the old emperor.

Qiao Yong led the Qiao family in bowing and receiving the imperial decree in the front courtyard.

“By the mandate of Heaven, I know that the former Admiral of the Western Garrison, Qiao Yong, is a loyal and righteous man. You have been resting at home for several years, and We originally intended to let you rest and recuperate more. However, the affairs of the court and the country are busy, and virtuous ministers who are loyal to the emperor and love the country are rare. We hope that you will return to the court to help the country… Effective immediately, you are hereby reinstated to your original position…”

Unsurprisingly, he was reinstated to his original position and even allowed to recall his old subordinates.

Although it was somewhat expected, it still greatly excited the entire Qiao family, including Qiao Yong. It was as if they had truly returned to high society. Although the Qiao family could endure hardship, who wouldn’t want to live a better life, and even to bring glory to their family?

The old eunuch read the decree aloud, and only after the last word was uttered did the Qiao family members loudly express their gratitude.

“Thank you for your great kindness, Your Majesty!”

Qiao Yong’s breathing trembled slightly as he looked up at the eunuch who was delivering the imperial edict. The eunuch gently closed the edict and pushed it forward with both hands.

“Hahaha… Lord Qiao.”

Qiao Yong quickly gave a bow, approached the old eunuch, and received the imperial edict with both hands.

“Thank you for your trouble, sir. I, Qiao, am currently in a difficult financial situation…”

When a chief eunuch delivered an imperial edict, one usually had to offer him a bribe. These kinds of eunuchs could sometimes play an unexpected role, especially those like Gong Shun who were extremely favored by the emperor.

Gong Shun was a shrewd man and naturally knew Qiao Yong’s awkward situation, so he quickly declined with both hands.

“Oh, Lord Qiao, you are overthinking this. I was just doing my duty and had no other intentions. By the way, His Majesty asked me to pass on a message to you before I came.”

“Please speak, sir!”

The old eunuch glanced in the direction of the Qiao family’s back courtyard and whispered.

“Are the two immortal masters still here? His Majesty intends to meet with them again to learn about the path of immortality?”

Qiao Yong understood the emperor’s meaning and naturally nodded in agreement.

“Please return and tell His Majesty that I will definitely convey the message. However, the actions of immortals are beyond the comprehension of mortals, and I can only do my best.”

“Oh, Lord Qiao, there’s no need to worry. The Emperor currently favors you greatly.”

“Um, I will still need Your Excellency’s guidance!”

“Hahaha, it’s no trouble, no trouble at all…”

Qiao Yong now understood very well that in the path of officialdom, being upright and incorruptible was certainly an excellent and extremely rare quality, but being too rigid made one easy to break. Unless the emperor and important ministers were very tolerant, it was certainly not a valid method to survive long-term.

As soon as the eunuch delivering the imperial decree left, Qiao Yong hurriedly went to the guest rooms in the backyard. His voice had already reached the guest courtyard before he even arrived.

Previously, everyone addressed them as “Immortal Masters,” but later Ji Yuan told him to just call him “Mr. Ji,” and the old beggar naturally told him to just call him “Old Beggar.” However, while calling one Mr. Ji was acceptable, calling the other “Old Beggar” was too disrespectful. The Qiao family didn’t dare, and compromised by calling him Mr. Lu.

“Mr. Ji, Mr. Lu! Mr. Ji, Mr. Lu, I’m here to bother you again, sirs…”

Qiao Yong’s voice stopped abruptly. When he turned the corner at the courtyard gate, he saw that Ji Yuan and the old beggar were no longer in the guesthouse courtyard. Just a quarter of an hour ago, when he had come by, the two of them were still chatting about earth and water.

“Did they leave?”

Qiao Yong panicked. He had just promised to deliver the message, and he had already left himself some leeway, not daring to say for sure that it would work out. Now, he might not even be able to deliver the message.

He strode into the courtyard, first looking at the guest rooms on both sides. Both doors were closed properly. Then he looked at the stone table in the courtyard, where the breakfast bowls and plates were neatly arranged, and the rice porridge and dried radish on them had been eaten clean.

Undeterred, Qiao Yong first went to Ji Yuan’s rooms and pushed open the door. The bedding inside was neat and clean, and the room was very bright and airy. There was nothing left that had not there before. Then he quickly went to the old beggar’s room, which similarly looked as if no one had ever been there.1

“Alas… the two immortals have left… how can I explain this to the Emperor!”

Qiao Yong walked dejectedly into the courtyard, preparing to clear away the dishes. But as he approached, he noticed that something hidden behind the dishes and trays. It was a small jade bottle with a note attached to it.

Qiao Yong was overjoyed. Ignoring the jade bottle for the moment, he carefully picked up the paper, studied it carefully, and then breathed a sigh of relief. At least he had something to report to the emperor.

After putting down the paper, Qiao Yong carefully picked up the small jade bottle on the stone table. The small jade bottle was about half a palm’s length and two fingers’ thickness. When he shook it, he could feel that there was liquid inside, about a cup’s worth.

‘Is this the Immortal Wine, the Ambergris that Mr. Ji referred to in his note?’

Seeing that no one was around, Qiao Yong secretly uncorked the small jade bottle. Instantly, a rich aroma of wine wafted out, much stronger than the wine Ji Yuan had poured the night before, and it made Qiao Yong’s heart flutter.

Shaking himself, Qiao Yong quickly recorked the small jade bottle, afraid that he might succumb to temptation and drink it directly.

Half an hour later, in the imperial study of the palace, only the old emperor, the old eunuch Gong Shun, Qiao Yong, and the Imperial Preceptor Men Yutong were left in the room.

The old emperor had read Ji Yuan’s note several times, and only put it down after reading it for the third time to make sure he hadn’t missed anything.

“Alas, Immortal Master Ji and Immortal Master Lu have left without saying goodbye. Although Immortal Master Ji said he would help inform Xianxia Island that we have been searching for them, I wonder how the immortals of Xianxia Island will react… And this immortal wine… Imperial Preceptor, what do you think?”

The old emperor’s expression was somewhat complicated. The immortal wine was certainly good, but it wasn’t like he hadn’t drunk something like it before. The Imperial Advisor had once used a technique to infuse spiritual energy into wine, turning ordinary mortal brews into immortal wine. It was indeed pleasant to drink, but that was all it was.

Men Yutong was currently observing the small jade bottle. Hearing the old emperor’s words, he knew that the emperor had misunderstood and quickly moved closer to explain.

“Your Majesty, I dare not make a definitive judgment on the matter of Xianxia Island, but with the help of Immortal Master Ji, there is surely a greater chance of success. As for this wine, it is a true divine brew, vastly different from my crude method of using techniques to infuse spiritual energy into the wine. The true divine brew, from the selection of ingredients to the refining process, and then to the care of the finished product, is a unique and wondrous method, somewhat akin to alchemy. Moreover…”

As he spoke, Men Yutong reached out and pinched the cork stopper of the small bottle, carefully pulling it off.

The unique aroma of Ambergris filled the air, captivating and intoxicating those present.

“Furthermore… in the note left by Immortal Ji, there is a line that says ‘carefully refined by the sovereign of rivers and lakes’… Your Majesty, Immortal Ji used ‘sovereign’ instead of ‘god’…”

“Uh, is there a difference?”

The old emperor subconsciously reached out towards the small jade bottle, feeling a mixture of nervousness and anticipation.

“Of course there’s a difference… there’s a huge difference…”

Men Yutong carefully handed the jade bottle to the old emperor and nodded as he explained.

“Nowadays, the dragons reign supreme in the waters. The only one qualified to be called the sovereign of rivers and lakes is a True Dragon! Your Majesty, this wine was carefully crafted by a True Dragon, and is called the Fragrance of Dragon’s Drool.2 Perhaps it means that even a True Dragon would drool over this wine! In other words, this is the true wine of immortality!”

The old emperor shuddered, gripping the jade bottle tightly in his hand, suppressing the strong urge to drink it immediately, and still managed to ask a question.

“Imperial Preceptor, does this wine require any medicinal ingredients? Would it be a waste of its potency if I drank it directly?”

“Your Majesty, rest assured, since Immortal Ji did not give any special instructions, it must be safe to drink directly. Uh, if Your Majesty is at ease, could you perhaps leave some for me to study? Just a few drops will suffice!”

The old emperor could no longer resist. He simply nodded to the Imperial Advisor, gulped, then held the small jade bottle to his lips, tilted his head back, and drank the wine in it. As he drank, the aroma of the Ambergris seemed to intensify several times over.

“Glug… glug…”

…The sound of the old emperor swallowing the Ambergris.

“Gurgle…”

“Gurgle…”

…The sounds of Qiao Yong and the Grand Eunuch Gong Shun swallowing. Even Men Yutong, standing to the side, felt his throat move slightly.

As soon as the wine touched his lips, the old emperor felt a surge of heat flow from his throat down into his stomach, then instantly into his body, rushing to every part of his limbs. The heat flowed continuously throughout his body, and his muscles and bones felt as if they were being burned, but it was not painful; on the contrary, it was very comfortable. However, at the same time, his mind grew confused, and his head became dizzy.

“Good… wine…”

He only managed to utter those two words before the exaggerated potency of the Ambergris kicked in. The old emperor swayed twice, then fell to one side.

“Your Majesty!”

Qiao Yong and the old eunuch each took a step forward, supporting the fallen old emperor on either side, while Men Yutong quickly reached out and caught the small jade bottle that had slipped from the old emperor’s hand.

After carefully examining the bottle, Men Yutong sighed softly. He’d promised to leave a few drops; now only a faint lingering fragrance remained…

Even so, Men Yutong quickly sealed the jade bottle with the cork stopper, not wanting to waste even a little bit of the lingering fragrance.

“Imperial Preceptor, is His Majesty alright?”

The old eunuch asked worriedly. Men Yutong smiled.

“He was drinking; the alcohol was strong, and he is now drunk. What could possibly happen? Just send His Majesty to his palace to rest. He will surely wake up in a day, or three to five days.”

“Yes!”

The old eunuch and Qiao Yong were finally relieved.

…

Ji Yuan and the old beggar left not because they realized Tu Siyan had woken up, but purely because the discussion about refining the spiritual weapon was so heated that the Qiao family’s backyard was no longer a suitable place. The most suitable place was actually Nine Peaks Mountain.

Before that, the two of them naturally had to make a detour to Pozi Mountain. Although there was no indication that Tu Siyan would wake up immediately, the fox demon could not be judged by common sense, so they naturally had to go and check on her since they were passing by.

Sure enough, the closer they got to Pozi Mountain, the more Ji Yuan and the old beggar vaguely sensed that the fox had awakened.
